# Friction log — dogfooding round 0.14 → 0.17

Written during the round, not reconstructed afterwards. One section per
repo, plus an orchestrator section for cross-repo observations and for
every defect verified centrally with a minimal reproduction.

Severity vocabulary: `annoyance` / `real problem` / `blocker`.

The report that draws conclusions from this is
[`2026-08-02-0.14-to-0.17.md`](./2026-08-02-0.14-to-0.17.md). This file is
the raw material and deliberately includes small observations the report
does not have room for.


---

# orchestrator

## Friction log — orchestrator (round setup + cross-repo observations)

### `packs_loaded` reports what was loaded, not what ran
- **repo:** improbable (TS/TSX only, zero `.py` files)
- **command:** `crimes scan . --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `coverage.packs_loaded` = `["universal", "language-js", "language-py"]`
  on a repo with no Python whatsoever.
- **why it matters:** the protocol's own robustness advice is "check
  `packs_loaded`, don't just count findings" — but the field cannot
  distinguish *loaded* from *ran* from *found nothing*. A user debugging
  "why did the Python pack find nothing in my Python repo" gets no signal
  from the one field that is supposed to answer that. A `packs_used` or a
  per-pack file count would.
- **severity:** real problem

### `evidence` conflates code evidence with score arithmetic
- **repo:** improbable
- **command:** `crimes scan . --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `swallowed_error` evidence entries include
  `"confidence 0.78 = 0.72 base + 0.06 (protected operation touches external request)"`
  and `"severity raised by: external request"` in the same array as genuine
  code evidence like `"calls inside the protected region: fetch, res.json"`.
- **why it matters:** `evidence` is documented as the receipts for the
  finding. Score derivation is meta *about* the finding, not evidence *for*
  it. Any consumer that wants to render or reason over the evidence has to
  string-match to filter it out — which is exactly what this round's card
  builder had to do. A separate `score_rationale` field would keep
  `evidence` meaning one thing. Note this is a schema change, so it costs a
  minor bump.
- **severity:** annoyance (but a schema-shaped one, so cheaper to fix now
  than later)

### `crimes` is not installable as a global for testing without publishing
- **repo:** n/a
- **command:** `which crimes`
- **what happened:** no global binary; the round needed a hand-written
  shim pointing at `packages/cli/dist/index.js`.
- **why it matters:** minor for this round, but it means every
  "try it on your repo" instinct requires either `npx crimes` (network) or
  a build. Worth confirming `npx crimes@latest scan` is genuinely the
  frictionless path the docs claim, since that is the documented first-run
  command.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `--all` is a byte-for-byte no-op in `--format json`
- **repo:** hono, p-limit (verified on both)
- **command:** `crimes scan . --all --format json` vs `crimes scan . --format json`
- **what happened:** identical MD5. `hono` both `b8cac7b8813aed91268c77d3593b2d21`;
  `p-limit` both `ae256124a5331c55529fbbfe6c0fbfcf`.
- **why it matters:** `--all` is the documented way to opt out of "signal over
  exhaustiveness", and the dogfooding protocol itself (and `docs/`) tell users
  to pass `--all --format json`. In JSON mode the flag changes nothing. That is
  defensible as a design — JSON is the full contract, `--all` only affects the
  human renderer — but it is not what the flag's name or the docs imply, and a
  user filtering on it will conclude their config is broken. Either document
  JSON as always-complete and warn when `--all` is passed redundantly, or make
  the default JSON view actually summarised.
- **severity:** real problem (documentation/contract clarity, not correctness)

### Control result: p-limit is not silent
- **repo:** p-limit (df47604), 8 files, ~200 lines of source
- **command:** `crimes scan . --format json`
- **what happened:** 10 findings — 0 high, 6 medium, 4 low. Types:
  `swallowed_error` x3, `boolean_naming_drift` x2, `large_function`,
  `return_shape_roulette`, `direct_date`, `large_file`, `unbounded_async_fanout`.
- **why it matters:** p-limit is a few hundred lines of exhaustively reviewed
  code by a maintainer known for minimalism. Ten findings is more than zero.
  The mitigating and genuinely good news is **0 high** — the tool declines to
  alarm. But `unbounded_async_fanout` firing on a library whose entire purpose
  is bounding concurrency deserves individual scrutiny, and `large_file` /
  `large_function` on an 8-file repo suggests thresholds that do not scale down.
- **severity:** real problem

### CONFIRMED BUG: `large_function` (Python pack) reports a size claim it just disproved
- **repo:** pydantic and zulip, found independently by two blind judges
- **source:** `packages/core/src/detectors/py/large-function.ts:75-108`
- **what happens:** the detector fires on EITHER `tooLong` (lines > threshold)
  OR `tooDeep` (nesting >= DEEP_NESTING). But the summary is built
  unconditionally as:
      `${fn.name}` is ${lines} lines[ and nests N levels deep]. At this size an
      agent must read the whole body before it can safely change any part of it.
  When a finding fires ONLY on nesting, the prose still leads with the line
  count and still says "At this size" — about a size that passed. Evidence
  line 83 then prints `14 lines (threshold 50 for shape "domain")` directly
  beneath it.
- **real examples:** pydantic `get_strict` — 14 lines, threshold 50, depth 4.
  `deep_update` — 9 lines. `flatten_errors` — 18 lines. zulip — 26, 19, 23 lines
  against a 50-line threshold.
- **why it matters:** the underlying finding (deep nesting) is legitimate. The
  message destroys it. Both blind judges independently classified these as
  self-contradicting and rated the detector noise — one wrote "the finding is
  really about nesting, and the size prose contradicts it". This is the single
  clearest case in the round of a **message bug being indistinguishable from a
  correctness bug** to the reader. `large_function` is the highest-volume
  detector in the corpus (2583 findings).
- **fix shape:** branch the summary on `tooLong` / `tooDeep`; lead with whichever
  actually tripped. Suppress or reword the sub-threshold evidence line.
- **severity:** blocker

### CONFIRMED BUG: `pass_through_abstraction` builds chains from name collisions
- **repo:** zulip (card zulip-0018)
- **what happens:** reported `get_services` "forwards through 5 layers across 5
  files". The actual body is one line: `return services.get(bot_id)`. The
  "chain" was assembled from unrelated `get` methods in `markdown_config`,
  `message_list` and `sub_store` — matching on the method NAME, not on a call
  path.
- **why it matters:** this is a correctness bug, not a calibration one. The
  finding asserts a specific structural claim that is simply false, with
  fabricated evidence naming five real files. `pass_through_abstraction` fired
  19 times, all in one repo.
- **severity:** blocker

### `sync_io_in_hotpath` has no working notion of "hotpath"
- **repos:** zulip, pydantic
- **what happens:** fires on code that is provably not a request path —
  a `if __name__ == "__main__"` setup CLI (zulip-0001), a Django management
  command `class Command(ZulipBaseCommand)` (zulip-0026), a `release/push.py`
  script (pydantic-0022), and an `@cache`-decorated once-per-process function
  (zulip-0039). Two more sit inside `if settings.DEVELOPMENT:` with a comment
  saying nginx serves the file in production (zulip-0009).
- **why it matters:** the detector's entire claim is about WHERE the I/O
  happens. 202 findings across 8 repos, and the "hotpath" half of the name is
  not being established at all.
- **severity:** real problem

### `boolean_naming_drift` flags names that cannot be renamed
- **repos:** zulip, pydantic
- **what happens:** four separate findings on `atomic = False` inside
  `class Migration(migrations.Migration)` — a Django-defined attribute name.
  Also 18 names "bound to a boolean expression with nothing saying they hold
  true/false" that are Pydantic fields explicitly declared `name: bool`.
  Also flags names its own convention clause exempts ("or an idiomatic bare
  name"): `nullable`, `plural`, `frozen`, `from_attributes`, `lock_acquired`.
- **why it matters:** 637 findings, third-highest volume in the corpus, and
  a large share target identifiers that are framework-owned (unrenameable),
  already type-annotated as bool, or explicitly exempted by the detector's own
  stated convention.
- **severity:** real problem

### `swallowed_error` does not read the comment that explains the catch
- **repos:** hono (20 of 43 cards), improbable, p-limit
- **what happens:** in nearly every case the code carries an explanatory
  comment or an immediately visible fallback. One judge noted the detector
  recognised deliberateness on exactly one card while missing an identical
  comment two functions above. Also fires where the next line is `throw err`
  (hono-0011) and where the catch calls three recovery functions (improbable).
- **why it matters:** 289 findings across 7 repos. It has a
  `BEST_EFFORT_FUNCTION_RE` recognition path, and the evidence suggests it is
  far too narrow.
- **severity:** real problem

### SYSTEMIC: detectors with OR-triggers print evidence for the condition that did NOT trip
- **repos:** pydantic, zulip, drf — found independently by three blind judges
- **the pattern:** a detector fires on `A || B`, but builds its summary and its
  leading evidence line from `A` unconditionally. When only `B` tripped, the
  finding presents a measurement that PASSED as though it were the reason,
  and the reader sees a self-refuting statement.
- **confirmed instances:**
  - `py/large-function.ts:75-108` — fires on `tooLong || tooDeep`, always says
    "is N lines … At this size". 12 of drf's 50 cards, plus pydantic and zulip.
  - `todo-density.ts:40` — `if (matches.length < 3 && density < threshold) return []`,
    i.e. fires on count>=3 OR density>threshold, then prints
    "4.2 markers per 1k LOC (threshold 10)".
- **why it matters:** this is the round's most damaging finding, because it is
  cheap to fix and expensive in trust. Every blind judge independently used the
  word "contradicted". A reader who catches the tool stating something its own
  evidence disproves stops believing the findings that ARE correct — and the
  underlying signals here (deep nesting, TODO count) are legitimate.
- **fix shape:** derive summary and evidence from whichever condition actually
  tripped. Audit every detector with a compound trigger for the same shape.
- **severity:** blocker

### `weak_test_signal` does not follow assertion helpers
- **repos:** drf (7 cards), zulip (3), pydantic (5)
- **what happens:** claims "N of M test functions assert nothing" where the tests
  delegate to a helper that asserts. drf-0033: 11 of 11 tests route through
  `_resolve_urlpatterns`, which the card itself prints asserting on three
  attributes. drf-0037: 10 tests call `_assert_datetime_rendering`, shown
  ending in `self.assertInHTML`. zulip-0004: tests call `_verify_archive_data`.
  Also counts `self.assert*` but not `self.fail`, and not
  `model_dump_json(warnings='error')`, and treats `@pytest.mark.benchmark`
  functions as assertion-free when measuring is their entire purpose.
- **why it matters:** 619 findings across 8 repos — 4th highest volume. Extract-
  a-shared-assertion-helper is one of the most common test idioms there is, and
  the detector reads it as the absence of testing. It is most wrong on exactly
  the well-factored suites it should be quietest about.
- **severity:** blocker

### Vendored and framework-owned code is treated as domain code
- **repos:** drf, zulip, pydantic
- **what happens:** drf-0007/0009/0036 target vendored minified
  google-code-prettify. 10 pydantic cards target `pydantic/v1/`, the frozen
  vendored v1 tree. drf-0014/0028 flag Django's own mandated package layout
  (`management/commands/*`) as reaching into internals. Django `Meta.abstract`,
  `Meta.proxy`, `Migration.atomic` flagged as badly-named.
- **why it matters:** `util/scope-class.ts` is documented as the single answer
  to generated/vendored/production classification. On real repos it is not
  catching vendored trees, framework-mandated layouts, or frozen legacy
  subtrees. This inflates every downstream count.
- **severity:** real problem

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: source under a dot-directory is silently skipped, and reported as clean
- **source:** `packages/core/src/discovery/files.ts:25` — `dot: false`, hardcoded,
  no config or CLI override anywhere in `discovery/`.
- **minimal repro (built and run by the orchestrator, not reported second-hand):**
  identical Python file, 8 lines, nesting depth 5, one `open()`.
    as `visible.py`               -> "2 findings across 1 file"
    as `.agents/skills/bad.py`    -> "No crimes detected. Suspiciously clean."  EXIT 0
- **found in the wild:** ebg keeps all 62 of its `.py` files under `.agents/`.
  `coverage.files_by_language` had NO `py` key, yet `packs_loaded` still listed
  `language-py`. An A/B against the same tree with `.agents` renamed to `agents`
  went from 759 findings to 967, and turned up a real
  `cross_language_concept_alias_drift` that the real scan reported as zero.
- **why it matters:** this is the worst failure mode a scanner can have — a
  confident all-clear on code it never opened, with a cheerful message and a
  zero exit code. `.agents/`, `.github/`, `.claude/`, `.server/`, `.config/`
  are all common. Nothing in the output hints that anything was skipped.
- **severity:** blocker

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: `crimes context` ids do not resolve in `crimes explain`
- **repro (orchestrator-run, repo `improbable`):**
    $ crimes context src/MetricsGrid.tsx     -> id=crime_00001 (a MetricsGrid finding)
    $ crimes explain crime_00001             -> file: src/server/migrations.ts
  A different file. `context` numbers its findings locally from 1; `explain`
  resolves against the global scan ordering. They only agree when the file
  happens to rank first.
- **why it matters:** `context <file>` then `explain <id>` is the documented
  pre-edit agent workflow and the core of the agent-native positioning — the
  `crimes` skill and `crimes init --agents` both point at it. An agent following
  it gets a confident, detailed rationale for a finding in a different file,
  with exit 0 and no warning. Independently found on ebg and reproduced here.
- **severity:** blocker

### `cross_language_route_drift` can only ever see a Python backend
- **source:** `packages/core/src/detectors/cross/route-drift.ts:124` — skips any
  file whose `pack !== "language-py"`.
- **consequence:** a TypeScript backend is structurally invisible to it. ebg has
  22 `app/api/**/route.ts` handlers exporting 23 verbs against 9 client
  `fetch("/api/…")` sites — a textbook drift surface — and the detector cannot
  produce a finding on it in principle, not by calibration.
- **why it matters:** combined with the corpus-wide zero, the 0.15 release's
  headline detector has (a) never fired on real code and (b) excludes the most
  common polyglot shape in the JS ecosystem.
- **severity:** blocker

### `agent_risk` has collapsed into size — a stated product invariant
- **repo:** ebg (measured), consistent with the corpus-wide ranking
- **what happens:** 15 of the top 20 findings by `agent_risk` are
  `large_function` / `large_file`. Nothing from the 0.16 slate appears, despite
  `parallel_destination` firing 52x and `orphaned_destination` 39x in that repo.
- **why it matters:** `CLAUDE.md` states `agent_risk` "is the differentiator and
  must not be collapsed into severity". Observably it is a length ranking. This
  is the score the whole product thesis rests on.
- **severity:** blocker

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: the 0.17.0 fingerprint fix did not fix the bug class
- **claim under test:** v0.17.0 release notes — "Self-scan collisions: 3 -> 0",
  and "The bug is in the fingerprint function's inputs, so it gets one general
  answer" (the `discriminator` field).
- **what is actually true:** only **2** detector files set `discriminator`
  (`exact-duplicate-block.ts`, `magic-domain-literal-scatter.ts`). The general
  mechanism shipped; it was wired into the instances the self-scan happened to
  surface. At least four other detectors have the identical shape.
- **measured on real repos (orchestrator-verified from the scan JSON):**
  | repo | findings | unique fingerprints | lost to collisions |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | hono | 377 | 288 | **89 (24%)** |
  | zulip | 3759 | 3433 | **326** |
  | buildfest | 1556 | 1362 | **194 (12%)** |
  | pydantic | 578 | 552 | 26 |
  Worst groups: `weak_test_signal` x8 (empty symbol), `commented_out_code` x12
  (empty symbol), `large_function` x9 (`describe callback`),
  `duplicate_component_shape` x10.
- **why it matters:** these are exactly the consequences 0.17.0 named as its
  reason for a wire-format break. `crimes ignore` on one `commented_out_code`
  finding silently suppresses up to **12**. `crimes diff` is blind to 24% of
  hono's findings. The self-scan was a biased sample — this repo has few test
  files and little commented-out code — and the fix inherited that bias.
- **fix shape:** any detector that can emit more than one finding per
  (type, file, symbol) needs a discriminator. Empty `symbol` is the tell.
  Consider asserting uniqueness in the scan pipeline so this cannot regress.
- **severity:** blocker

### `crime_NNNNN` ids are not stable across runs of an unchanged repo
- **repo:** hono, two back-to-back scans at the same SHA
- **what happens:** identical finding multiset, different ORDER, and ids are
  positional — so 5 ids changed identity. Run A `crime_00024` = `large_file` on
  `secure-headers.ts`; run B `crime_00024` = `hardcoded_localhost` on a
  benchmark. Suspected cause: the clock-derived `recency` score drifting
  0.71 -> 0.70 and flipping a sort tie.
- **why it matters:** `crimes explain <id>` is the documented workflow and does
  not survive a re-run. 0.17.0 fixed evidence-string determinism; finding
  IDENTITY ordering is still clock-dependent.
- **severity:** blocker

### `TEST_FILE_RE` misses `test.js` — the ava / node:test default filename
- **repo:** p-limit
- **what happens:** a bare top-level `test.js` matches none of `*.test.js`,
  `_test.js`, `__tests__/`, `tests/`. Cascade: charged on the `domain` 300-line
  budget instead of the 1500-line `test_file` budget; `tier: "domain"`;
  `test_gap` degraded to the 0.50 no-signal midpoint; `crimes context index.js`
  reports "no test file matched" for a package whose 378-line suite sits beside
  it and imports it on line 8.
- **severity:** real problem

### The `docs` shape and the data-format exclusion are unreachable at default config
- **source:** `DEFAULT_SOURCE_INCLUDES` in `packages/core/src/config.ts`
- **what happens:** it contains no `json`, `yaml`, `yml`, `txt`, `rst`, `adoc`.
  So (a) the 0.17.0 note that ".json/.yaml stay on the domain budget — a
  3000-line config file is a finding worth having" describes a policy that
  never runs: hono's 735-line `package-lock.json` and a synthetic 1201-line
  `.json` both produce ZERO findings; and (b) 5 of the 7 extensions the `docs`
  shape claims (`.txt`, `.rst`, `.adoc`, `.markdown`, and the data formats)
  are dead code. Overriding `include` makes both fire immediately — the shape
  logic is correct, the files never reach it.
- **severity:** real problem

### `test_gap: 0.5` means "no signal" but renders as a confident causal sentence
- **repo:** p-limit
- **what happens:** `explain` renders the 0.50 midpoint as "a test file matches
  this module's name but does not import it" — false on both clauses, and
  contradicting `crimes context` on the same file, which says no test matched.
- **severity:** real problem

### `explain` exits 2 on a finding the scanner itself emitted
- **repo:** hono
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_00369`
- **what happened:** `crimes: no detector registered for finding.type
  "oversized_raster"`, exit 2. 22 of 23 hono types explained cleanly.
- **severity:** real problem

### `hotspots` ranks manifest churn as the top risk, over a different file set
- **repo:** hono
- **what happens:** #1 risk is `package.json` at 72%, ~14 points clear of
  anything real, because it churned 29x in 90 days on version bumps. It also
  ranks files `scan` never scanned (`package.json`, `readme.md`, `index.d.ts`)
  — `hotspots` and `scan` operate over different file universes (929 vs 546
  files on ebg). On a quiet repo it pads the list with rows reading
  "0 findings - highest none" rather than saying nothing is hot.
- **severity:** real problem

### NOTE FOR ANYONE READING THE ARTIFACTS
Several `artifacts/*.json` were 0 bytes at various points because a scan was
SIGTERMed (exit 143), not because crimes found nothing. Do not read a 0-byte
artifact as a clean scan. This is itself a mild finding: a killed scan leaves
a file that is indistinguishable from a successful empty one.

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: `large_file` counts total lines + 1 and calls it "non-empty lines"
- **repo:** pydantic (orchestrator-verified against the files on disk)
- **measured:**
  | file | crimes claims | real total | real non-blank |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `pydantic/fields.py` | 1914 | 1913 | 1654 |
  | `pydantic/types.py` | 3336 | 3335 | 2647 |
  | `pydantic/networks.py` | 1371 | 1370 | 1084 |
  The claim is exactly `total + 1` every time, labelled "non-empty lines".
  Overstatement vs the true non-empty count is 16-26%.
- **consequence — it manufactures findings.** Of 109 `large_file` findings on
  pydantic, **11 (10%) would not fire at all** on the true non-empty count:
  `_migration.py` (claimed 318, real 298, threshold 300),
  `_dataclasses.py` (316 / 253), `v1/env_settings.py` (351 / 290), five
  `pydantic-core/*.rs` files, and two markdown docs — so the 0.17.0 `docs`
  shape inherits the bug (`docs/migration.md` claimed 1168, real 889,
  threshold 1000).
- **why it matters:** `large_file` is the second-highest-volume detector in the
  corpus (779 findings). A ~10% phantom rate is roughly 78 findings that exist
  only because of an off-by-one plus a blank-line miscount. It also means the
  `lines` field and every ratio ("2.9x the configured threshold") are wrong.
- **severity:** blocker

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: `crimes scan` on a nonexistent path exits 0 and reports clean
- **command:** `crimes scan /tmp/this-path-does-not-exist-xyz`
- **what happens:**
      CRIME SCENE REPORT
      repo: this-path-does-not-exist-xyz  ·  0 findings
      No crimes detected. Suspiciously clean.
      EXIT=0
  The JSON form is equally well-formed: valid `schema_version`, empty
  `findings`, `files_total: 0`, exit 0.
- **why it matters:** a path typo in CI produces a passing, well-formed clean
  report. `verdict` and `explain` both correctly exit 2 on bad input; `scan` is
  the outlier. Combined with the dot-directory bug, there are now two distinct
  ways to get a confident all-clear on code that was never read.
- **severity:** blocker

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: test-scope classification is structurally inert on Python
- **source:** `packages/core/src/config.ts:17` `DEFAULT_NON_DOMAIN_PATTERNS` =
  `scripts/**`, `examples/**`, `fixtures/**`, `public/**`, `**/__tests__/**`,
  `**/*.test.{ts,tsx,js,jsx}`, `**/*.spec.{ts,tsx,js,jsx}`.
  **No `tests/**`, no `test_*.py`, no `*_test.py`.**
- **measured:** every finding is `tier: "domain"` — pydantic 578/578 (including
  118 under `tests/`), drf 104/104 (21 under `tests/`), zulip 3725/3759 with
  662 test-file findings all `domain`.
- **the sharp part:** crimes ALREADY has the right classifier.
  `util/test-files.ts:23` matches `test_[^/]+\.py$` and `.*_test\.(?:py|go)$`.
  `tier` uses a third, glob-based, JS-only mechanism instead. `classifyScope`
  is imported by exactly 4 detectors, all 0.16.0 JS-side — zero Python and zero
  universal detectors consult it.
- **why it matters:** `CLAUDE.md` presents `util/scope-class.ts` as "one answer
  to generated / vendored / migration / fixture / test / config / production".
  There are three answers and the one wired into scoring cannot see a Python
  test. Test files are ~62-64% of both pydantic and drf, and every finding in
  them is scored as production code.
- **severity:** blocker

### PEP 696 type-parameter defaults fail to parse; one form loses the whole file
- **what happens:** `class M[T, U = int]`, `def f[T = int]`,
  `def f[*Ts = *tuple[int]]` all produce parse errors. `type Al[T = int] =
  list[T]` loses the **entire file's** symbol table. The same forms without
  defaults parse cleanly.
- **why it matters:** Python 3.13 syntax. Combined with the next entry, a repo
  adopting it degrades silently.
- **severity:** real problem

### `hasSyntaxErrors` is computed and then never surfaced
- **what happens:** consumed by exactly one detector (`weak_test_signal.py`).
  There is no `coverage.parse_errors`, nothing on stderr, nothing in the JSON.
  pydantic's one unparseable file was found only by importing the parser
  directly.
- **why it matters:** this is the same failure family as the dot-directory bug
  and the nonexistent-path bug — crimes knows something went wrong and does not
  say so. A repo where the grammar chokes reports as clean.
- **severity:** real problem

### `commented_out_code` matches English prose, including Rust doc comments
- **repos:** pydantic (77 findings) vs drf (2)
- **what happens:** 9 of a deterministic 10-sample are English prose. One is
  `pydantic-core/src/definitions.rs:1-5`, a Rust `///` doc comment. The rule
  keys on tokens `def`/`class`/`fn`, so prose *about* classes and fields
  matches. This is why pydantic scores 77 and drf 2 — a writing-style
  difference, not a code-quality one.
- **note:** on airflow this detector is **8019 findings, 45% of the entire
  scan**. It was not in the judged sample.
- **severity:** real problem

### Corpus-integrity lesson: a broken clone looks exactly like a clean repo
- **repo:** drf
- **what happened:** `git-lfs` is not installed, so `git checkout` aborted the
  whole worktree write at `docs/theme/src/drf-logos.fig`. The clone had 15
  files and no `rest_framework/` or `tests/` at all.
- **why it matters:** had the agent not counted `.py` files by hand first, drf
  would have scanned to ~0 findings and been written up as the round's best
  control result. crimes gave no indication it was looking at an empty repo.
  This is the dot-directory bug wearing different clothes, and it is a
  methodological warning for any future round.
- **severity:** real problem (for the product: silent zero; for us: process)

### `mixed_utc_local_methods` fired zero on 296K lines of Python
- across pydantic and drf combined. Add to the zero-firing list.
- **severity:** annoyance (a finding about the detector)

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: `.gitignore` is not respected
- **claim under test:** `CLAUDE.md` § Stack decisions — "File walking: fast-glob
  + `ignore` (respect `.gitignore`)".
- **orchestrator repro, from scratch:**
      /tmp/gitig3/.gitignore        -> "generated/"
      git check-ignore generated/big.ts -> generated/big.ts   (ignored)
      crimes scan .
      -> "2 findings across 1 file · 1 high"
      -> "generated/big.ts   1. God Function  2. God File"
  The gitignored file is the ONLY file in the report. Also confirmed via
  `coverage.files_total`, which counts ignored files.
- **in the wild (buildfest):** `migrate-output/` is line 8 of `.gitignore` and
  is a verbatim copy of `apps/airquote`. It produced **100 findings directly,
  229 more citing it — 323 of 1556 = 20.8% of the whole report** — and
  **136 of 158 (86%) `exact_duplicate_block` findings**. It also appears in
  `coverage.by_package`.
- **why it matters:** duplication detectors are maximally vulnerable to
  build output, and build output is exactly what `.gitignore` names. This has
  stayed invisible because the hardcoded default excludes cover
  `node_modules`/`dist`/`.next` — the common cases — so the missing
  `.gitignore` support only shows up on project-specific ignore rules.
  Together with the dot-directory bug, crimes both **scans what it shouldn't**
  and **skips what it should**, and neither is reported.
- **severity:** blocker

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: silent, nondeterministic finding loss under fd pressure
- **repo:** buildfest, fd sweep
- **what happens:** at `ulimit -n` 48 and above, output is byte-identical to
  baseline (`sha256 d694948ff7eb…`) — the 0.17.0 bounded pool is correctly
  sized and holds. Below ~48 the scan **silently reports fewer findings,
  nondeterministically**: three runs at `ulimit -n 40` on an unchanged tree
  gave 1522 / 1535 / 1543 findings. Every one exited 0, with empty stderr, an
  **identical `coverage` block**, and `summary.high` pinned at 187 while ~11%
  of the report vanished.
- **root cause, located — it is NOT `indexes.ts`:**
  `packages/core/src/ast-hash/function-index.ts:72-79`
      try { source = await readFile(abs, "utf8"); }
      catch { return hits; }   // EMFILE becomes "this file has no functions"
  Same shape at `packages/core/src/jsx/shape-index.ts:65-66`. The bare catch
  cannot distinguish `ENOENT` from `EMFILE`, so each exhaustion silently
  deletes one file from the duplicate corpus — partial and racy, which is
  strictly worse than the all-or-nothing `undefined` that `indexes.ts` was
  designed to produce.
- **margin between correct and silently wrong: 8 descriptors.**
- **fix:** re-throw or count `EMFILE`/`ENFILE`, and surface a read-failure
  count in `coverage`. Touches no detector.
- **severity:** blocker

### `churn` and `recency` score 0.00 on a quiet repo and render as a claim about the code
- **repo:** buildfest (zero commits in the default 90-day window)
- **what happens:** `scores.churn` and `scores.recency` are 0.00 on all 1556
  findings, and `scan` renders that as "churn low" — an assertion about the
  code, when the truth is "not measured". `hotspots` degrades further: every
  file scores an identical `risk 0.40 · 0 changes`, so ranking collapses to
  alphabetical and the **#1 hotspot is a PNG**. With `--since 1y` the same
  command is genuinely good (`package.json` 0.91, 17 changes).
- **why it matters:** same family as `test_gap: 0.5` rendering as a confident
  causal sentence. The tool has no way to say "no signal" and defaults to
  saying something reassuring instead.
- **severity:** real problem

### Positive results worth recording
- **Determinism holds.** 9 buildfest runs produced the same 2,412,426 bytes,
  including `id` fields. The 0.17.0 reproducibility claim is real.
- **Performance is not the problem.** 1177 files in 27s at 308 MB max RSS.
  zulip: 3588 files in 81s.
- **Both zero cases pass** on buildfest, stated positively: `language-py`
  loaded and produced exactly 0 findings on a repo with 0 `.py`; all three
  `cross_language_*` produced exactly 0 on a single-language repo.
- **Both 0.17 surfaces confirmed live:** the `docs` shape fired on 3 `.md`
  with `shape: document` and a distinct 1000-line threshold; `discriminator`
  is present on 225 findings.

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: `dependency_provenance_gap` checks Python imports against npm manifests
- **repo:** zulip. Severity **high**, on `package.json`.
- **verbatim:** "450 external package(s) are imported but declared in no
  applicable package.json. They resolve today through hoisting; a clean install
  can fail." Evidence lists:
      `_typeshed` — imported at zerver/lib/test_console_output.py
      `abc`       — imported at corporate/lib/stripe.py
      `aioapns`   — imported at zerver/lib/push_notifications.py
- `abc` and `_typeshed` are the **Python standard library**. `aioapns` **is**
  declared, in `pyproject.toml`. Every cited import is from a `.py` file, and
  the manifest set searched is "package.json, starlight_help/package.json".
- **why it matters:** the detector has no notion that a Python import belongs to
  a Python manifest. On any repo containing Python it emits a HIGH-severity
  finding that is 100% wrong and whose implied fix is to add the Python stdlib
  to `package.json`. Note the *second* finding from the same detector on the
  same repo (medium, `@typescript/native` declared but absent from
  `pnpm-lock.yaml`) is plausibly correct — so the JS path works and the
  language dispatch is the defect.
- **severity:** blocker

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: `blast_radius_importers` prints a component size as a per-file count
- **repo:** zulip, verified from the scan JSON
- **measured:** 3,759 findings carry `blast_radius_importers`, and there are
  only **37 distinct values**. 866 findings share the value **798**; 825 share
  **324**. `blast_radius` is pinned at 1.0 on **1,758 of 3,759 (47%)**.
- **in the human report:** `zerver/data_import/slack.py` renders as
  "blast top-quartile (798 importers)". Actual direct importers: 5.
  `inbox_ui.ts` prints 324; actual 4.
- **why it matters:** hundreds of unrelated files cannot each have exactly 798
  importers — this is the size of a connected component in the import graph
  being rendered as a direct fan-in count. It is a number a maintainer can
  disprove in five seconds, printed next to findings that are otherwise
  correct. `explain` renders it honestly ("50+ transitive importers (cap
  reached)"); `scan` and `context` do not — so the same score is truthful in
  one renderer and false in two.
- **severity:** blocker

### Cross-language: diagnosed, on the second ideal input
- **repo:** zulip. `route_drift` 0, `type_drift` 0, `alias_drift` 0.
- **the diagnosis (both halves are blind):**
  - `routeFromDecorator()` is the ONLY route source and reads **decorators
    only**. Zulip declares **387** URLs as Django URLconf function calls —
    `rest_path("realm/domains", GET=…, POST=…)` in `zproject/urls.py`.
    Repo-wide `@app.get`-shaped decorators: **0**. So `route_drift`
    early-returns on an empty backend set and never reaches its comparison.
    Secondary blocker: Django routes are relative (`"realm"`) and the
    extractor rejects anything not starting with `/`.
  - The frontend half fails too. Zulip makes **140** calls shaped
    `channel.post({url: "/json/typing", data, …})`. The receiver `channel`
    fails `REQUEST_RECEIVERS = /(^|\.)(axios|http|client|api|request)$/`, and
    the URL sits in an object-literal first argument, so `literalText()`
    returns undefined. **`swallowed_error.js` parses that exact call fine and
    quotes it in the human report** — two collectors in the same parser
    disagree about what an HTTP call is.
  - `type_drift` did run, and honestly found no name matches — but its Python
    pool is junk: the `allValued` fallback accepts any class whose members are
    all string constants, so 14 of 19 "closed sets" are things like
    `Integration` (image-path templates). Zulip's 29 real enums are
    `IntEnum`/`Enum` with **integer** values, which the extractor never records.
- **and you cannot tell any of this from the output:** `"cross-language"` is
  never a member of `packs_loaded` — it is `["universal", ...registeredPacks()]`.
  No JSON field distinguishes "ran and found nothing" from "never ran".
- **severity:** blocker (for the 0.15 release's premise)

### 749 files of Zulip's rendered UI were dropped at discovery, silently
- `.hbs` (469), `.html` (224) and `.erb` are outside the default include globs.
  They appear in **neither** coverage histogram — not `files_by_language`, not
  `universal_only_by_extension` — so nothing in the output tells the user that
  the entire template layer was never opened. Same family as the dot-directory
  and `.gitignore` bugs.
- **severity:** real problem

### `docs_code_drift`: 12 of 12 verified false positives
- root-relative documentation links are resolved against the repo root instead
  of `docs/`, plus extensionless Sphinx-style links are not understood. All six
  targets spot-checked exist on disk.
- **severity:** real problem

### Quantified: `large_function` self-refutes on 19% of its Python findings
- zulip: **172 of 898** `large_function.py` findings quote a line count at or
  below the threshold in the same evidence string. Canonical case:
  `zerver/tornado/event_queue.py:1602 process_realm_user_add_event` —
  "God Function … 9 lines (threshold 50 for shape 'domain')" on a clean 9-line
  event fan-out. Visible in the **default** human output at #17 of the top file.
- `large_function` is **1,461 findings = 39% of the entire zulip report**, and
  `agent_risk` ranks it first: top 20 by `agent_risk` is 18 `large_function` /
  `large_file`, **zero Python**, on a repo that is 71% Python.
- **severity:** blocker (already recorded; this entry adds the measurement)

### The positioning check, measured
- On zulip, **81% of the report (3,038 of 3,759 findings)** falls in what the
  agent characterised as the "a linter already does this" bucket — line counts,
  naming, commented-out code, TODO density. `CLAUDE.md` states crimes is
  explicitly **not** trying to replace ESLint/Biome. The defensible remainder is
  **721 findings, ~1.2 per 1k lines** — which is a genuinely reasonable number,
  and is the product that appears to be hiding inside this one.

### VERIFIED BLOCKER: no size or line-length guard before parsing — 135x slowdown
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **measured:** `scan --all --format json` = **675 s**. Same repo with 92 vendor
  bundles removed = **5 s**. One 1.9 MB bundle alone accounts for **251
  seconds** and produces 23 findings. Longest line in the repo: **1,621,186
  characters**. `diff HEAD~5...HEAD` never completed (killed >540 s).
- **root cause, verified at source:** `packages/core/src/config.ts:216-217`
  excludes `**/*.min.js` and `**/*.generated.*` — **filename patterns only**.
  Modern bundlers emit `name-HASH.js` (`mermaid.core-DKFhFOEB.js`), which
  matches neither. No size guard, no line-length guard, and no `--exclude` or
  `--max-file-size` flag on `scan`, so under `--no-init` there is no
  workaround at all.
- **the compounding harm:** `util/scope-class.ts` classified these bundles as
  production domain code. **444 of 851 findings (52.2%)** are inside the
  vendored `reference/` directory, and **15 of the top 20 by `agent_risk` are
  minified third-party bundles**. All five entries in the default report's
  "Top files by risk" are somebody else's code, with findings reading
  `Unprefixed Boolean 'So' @L1`. The report's closing call to action is
  `→ Start with crimes context chrome-extension/reference/claude-1.0.77/assets/mcpPermissions-CJK8I7C7.js`.
- **severity:** blocker

### `.gitignore` — confirmed absent at source, and on a second repo
- **source check:** grep for gitignore parsing across
  `packages/core/src/discovery/` returns **nothing**. `discovery/files.ts`
  passes `ignore: options.exclude` to fast-glob and nothing else. `CLAUDE.md`
  lists "fast-glob + `ignore` (respect `.gitignore`)" as a *locked* stack
  decision; it was never implemented. The `dot: false` behaviour is incidental,
  not gitignore support.
- **second independent repo:** mysidekick-cc — `api/_server.mjs` (34 findings)
  and `public/call.bundle.js` (12) are esbuild output, both gitignored, both
  untracked: **46 of 241 findings = 19.1%**. `_server.mjs` is a 2,668-line
  bundle of `src/*.ts`, so every finding in it duplicates one already reported
  against the real source. The report's single closing recommendation is
  `→ Start with crimes context api/_server.mjs`. The tool's one piece of advice
  points at a generated file.
- **severity:** blocker (already recorded; this adds source confirmation and a
  second repo)

### Absent signals are scored as zeros and rendered to the user as good news
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **what happens:** across all 175 Swift findings, `blast_radius = 0`,
  `blast_radius_importers = 0`, `test_gap = 0` — constant, not distributed,
  because no pack claims Swift. The schema has no "unknown", so absence
  becomes the best possible score.
- **sharpest instance:** `crimes context ProjectsStore.swift` — 4,949 lines,
  97 commits in 90 days, no test file — prints **`test gap: bottom-quartile`**,
  the identical phrase a well-covered TS file receives. Swift test discovery
  looks for `.test`/`.spec`/`__tests__` and misses
  `Tests/OrtomateStudioTests/*Tests.swift` (20+ files, standard SwiftPM).
  In the command designed to brief an agent before it edits, the tool reports
  the **opposite** of the truth.
- **same family as** `test_gap: 0.5` narrated as a causal sentence, and
  `churn: 0.00` rendered as "churn low".
- **severity:** blocker

### The honesty gap is one line of output wide
- **repo:** ortomate-studio (429 Swift files, 85% of source LOC)
- **the good news, recorded properly:** the JSON is completely honest. All 429
  Swift files appear in `files_total`, and `universal_only_by_extension` names
  `".swift": 429` explicitly. `--explain-coverage` prints an unusually good
  sentence: *"These files were not claimed by any loaded language pack. They
  received universal-pack checks only (file size, raster assets, duplicate
  filenames, hardcoded localhost/local paths, docs link checking)."*
- **the gap:** that flag is **off by default and printed last**, after the
  report it should be qualifying. In the 174-line default report the string
  "swift" appears **zero** times. The 175 Swift findings come from just 4
  detector types; ~45 detectors ran on 16,012 lines of TS/JS and on **none** of
  the 91,778 lines of Swift.
- **fix:** promote one header line — `429 of 619 files: universal checks only
  (.swift)`. Cheapest high-value fix identified in the entire round.
- **severity:** real problem

### `packs_loaded` is a constant — confirmed at source
- `packages/core/src/coverage.ts:58` builds it as
  `["universal", ...router.registeredPacks()]`, and registration happens at
  module load. So `language-py` appears on **every scan of every repo**,
  including one with 429 Swift files and zero Python. It cannot answer the
  question its name implies. The honest signal sits right beside it —
  `files_by_language` has no `py` key — but nothing marks `packs_loaded` as
  non-diagnostic, and this round's own protocol told agents to read it.
- **severity:** real problem

### `hotspots` is the honest command and `scan` is the one the docs push
- On mysidekick-cc, `hotspots` ranks `src/server.ts`, `src/prep/compileBrief.ts`,
  `src/db.ts` — and the gitignored bundles are **absent**, because churn-based
  ranking drops untracked files for free. On ortomate-studio it puts
  `ProjectsStore.swift` (97 changes) at #1. Both times it beats `scan`.
- **the cheap fix this suggests:** `scan` could borrow the tracked-in-git check
  `hotspots` already has, which would fix a large part of the `.gitignore` and
  vendored-bundle problems at once.
- **severity:** worth acting on

### The default report is not a summary
- **repo:** mysidekick-cc. The first block prints **34 findings for one file**;
  items 24-34 are eleven consecutive `Catch and Release` entries, seven of them
  the identical `res.text()` shape. `--top` caps *files* at 5 but does not cap
  findings *within* a file, so the default already prints everything for the
  files it shows — contradicting the PRD's "top findings only, `--all` is
  opt-in". Collapsing repeats of one charge in one file
  (`Catch and Release x11`) would halve the report with no information loss.
- Four unreconciled file counts across three commands on one repo: 74, 63,
  239, 220.
- **severity:** real problem

# ebg

## Friction log — ebg

**Repo:** `~/dev/ebg` (polyglot: TS/TSX/Python/Swift)
**SHA:** e3b7b1e3b15a5a9b7a0548fc9d86aae2c57f7bb2
**Binary:** `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes` 0.17.0
**Started:** 2026-08-02

---
### Python pack loaded but zero `.py` files were ever walked
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes scan /Users/andrew/dev/ebg --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `coverage.packs_loaded` includes `language-py`, but
  `coverage.files_by_language` is `{"js": 376}` — there is **no `py` key at
  all**, and `.py` does not appear in `universal_only_by_extension`
  (`{".md":109, ".swift":58, ".sh":3}`). The repo has 62 tracked `.py` files.
  Zero findings have a `.py` file. The files were not parsed, not skipped-
  with-a-reason, not counted: they are simply invisible.
- **why it matters:** This is exactly the failure mode the protocol warns
  about — `packs_loaded` reports what was *loaded*, not what *ran*. A user
  with a Python subtree sees "language-py" in coverage and reasonably
  concludes their Python was analysed. It was not. There is no signal
  anywhere in the JSON or the human output that 62 files were dropped.
- **severity:** real problem

### Coverage accounting does not add up to the files actually analysed
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** same
- **what happened:** `files_total` 546 = `js` 376 + `files_universal_only`
  170 (`.md` 109 + `.swift` 58 + `.sh` 3). But 25 findings are on `.jpg`
  files (`oversized_raster`). Images are evidently walked and analysed yet
  appear in no coverage bucket. So `files_total` under-reports the true
  analysed set, and the language histogram is not a partition of it.
- **why it matters:** `coverage` is the only thing that tells a user what
  was and wasn't looked at. If it silently omits a whole category that
  produced 25 findings, it can't be used to reason about blind spots.
- **severity:** real problem

### No `coverage.by_package` at all (0.15 surface absent)
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** same
- **what happened:** The JSON `coverage` object has exactly five keys:
  `files_total`, `files_by_language`, `files_universal_only`,
  `universal_only_by_extension`, `packs_loaded`. `by_package` is absent.
  The repo has a root `package.json` and a vendored
  `ios/Vendor/NFCPassportReader/Package.swift`.
- **why it matters:** 0.15 shipped `coverage.by_package` as part of the
  contract. On a real repo it does not appear, so either it is gated on a
  multi-manifest condition this repo doesn't meet, or it regressed. Either
  way a consumer cannot rely on the key existing.
- **severity:** real problem

### All three `cross_language_*` detectors fired zero times
- **repo:** ebg — the only polyglot repo in the local corpus
- **command:** same
- **what happened:** `cross_language_route_drift`,
  `cross_language_type_drift`, `cross_language_concept_alias_drift`: zero
  findings each. Given that the Python side was never walked (see above),
  this is a *vacuous* zero, not an informative one.
- **why it matters:** The one repo in the local round that could have
  exercised the 0.15 cross-language pack could not, because the input to
  half of each detector never existed.
- **severity:** real problem

### ROOT CAUSE: the file walker is `dot: false`, so any source under a hidden directory is invisible
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** controlled A/B in scratchpad (nothing written to ebg):
  copied `~/dev/ebg/.agents/skills` to `$S/pytest_visible/skills` and
  `~/dev/ebg/.agents` to `$S/pytest_dot/.agents`, then scanned both.
- **what happened:**
  - `pytest_visible` (same 62 `.py` files, non-hidden parent):
    `files_by_language: {"py": 62}`, **190 findings**, 178 of them from
    `language-py` (`large_function` 116, `sync_io_in_hotpath` 43,
    `boolean_naming_drift` 15, `direct_date` 4, `large_file` 12).
  - `pytest_dot` (identical bytes, hidden parent): `files_total: 0`,
    `files_by_language: {}`, **0 findings**, exit 0.
  - Source: `packages/core/src/discovery/files.ts:25` — `dot: false`.
    There is no CLI flag to override it, and `crimes.config.json`
    `include` globs won't help because fast-glob's `dot` option, not the
    pattern, is what excludes them.
- **why it matters:** The entire Python surface of the only polyglot repo
  in the local corpus was dropped for a reason unrelated to Python. Worse,
  the degraded case is indistinguishable from a clean one: scanning the
  hidden copy prints **"No crimes detected. Suspiciously clean."** with
  exit 0 and `packs_loaded: [universal, language-js, language-py]`. A user
  whose source lives under `.server/`, `.functions/`, `.agents/`,
  `.github/scripts/` etc. gets a confident all-clear on an unscanned repo.
- **severity:** blocker

### `--explain-coverage` prints a pack as "loaded" and then omits it entirely
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/ebg --no-init --explain-coverage`
- **what happened:** Output reads `packs loaded: universal, language-js,
  language-py` followed by `files by language pack:` with a single line for
  `language-js`. `language-py` has no line at all — not "0 files", just
  absent. Same in the JSON. The only place that could have told the user
  "a pack you loaded claimed nothing" says nothing.
- **why it matters:** `--explain-coverage` exists precisely to answer "what
  did you not look at". It answers it wrongly by omission.
- **severity:** real problem

### Cross-language verdict, with evidence (counterfactual scan)
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes scan $S/ebg_unhidden --all --format json --no-init`
  where `$S/ebg_unhidden` is `git archive HEAD` of ebg with `.agents`
  renamed to `agents` — same bytes, nothing hidden.
- **what happened:** With the Python visible, `files_by_language` becomes
  `{"py": 62, "js": 375}`, total findings rise 759 → 967, and

  (`agents/skills/docx/.../merge_runs.py:93`, "delete" concept named
  `remove`/`trash` in Python vs `delete` in TS, 11 files).
  `cross_language_route_drift` and `cross_language_type_drift` remain zero.
- Shape check, by hand, in ebg:
  - **route_drift — shape genuinely absent.** Zero matches for
    `@app/@router/@bp.(get|post|…)` or `from fastapi|flask|django|
    starlette|aiohttp` in any `.py`. The Python import histogram is
    `pathlib 39, sys 30, defusedxml 21, zipfile 20, argparse 18, lxml 6` —
    these are Claude-skill CLI scripts, not a service.
  - **type_drift — shape genuinely absent.** Zero occurrences of `Enum`
    anywhere in the `.py`. All 14 Python classes are validator/handler
    classes (`BaseSchemaValidator`, `RedliningValidator`, `RectAndField`,
    `ReviewHandler`), none a constant/closed set.
  - **alias_drift — shape present, and it fires.** Zero in the real scan

### `cross_language_route_drift` is blind to the route contract this repo actually has
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** read `packages/core/src/detectors/cross/route-drift.ts`
- **what happened:** `collectBackendRoutes()` skips every file where
  `entry.pack !== "language-py"` (line 124). The backend half of the
  contract can only ever be Python. ebg has a textbook drift-able surface
  that is entirely TypeScript: **22** `app/api/**/route.ts` handlers
  exporting **23** verbs (13 `GET`, 10 `POST`), against **9** client
  `fetch("/api/…")` call sites. The detector cannot look at it.
  (I hand-checked all 9 fetch paths against the 22 route dirs — all
  resolve, so there is no drift to miss *in this repo*. The blindness is
  structural, not a missed finding here.)
- **why it matters:** The detector is named `cross_language_route_drift`
  but its real contract is "Python backend ↔ JS frontend". On a Next.js /
  Remix / SvelteKit / Express repo — a large share of the target market —
  the same class of bug exists and is invisible. The name and the
  `whyItMatters` copy ("the contract between a frontend and its backend is
  written down twice") both over-promise relative to the implementation.
- **severity:** real problem

### Python parse robustness: PASS, with positive evidence
- **repo:** ebg (via the unhidden counterfactual)
- **what happened:** 51 of 62 `.py` files produced ≥1 finding. Specific
  modern-syntax files, all confirmed to produce findings:
  - `match` (3 files, e.g. `.../docx/scripts/office/validate.py`) —
    `large_function main [25,107]`, `nesting depth 3`. The real file is 111
    lines with `def main` at 25, the `match` at 80, `if __name__` at 110.
    The parser bounded `main` correctly *through* the match block and
    counted the case arms as nesting — so it structurally understood
    `match`, not merely tolerated it.
  - walrus `:=` (5 files) — 2–8 findings each, e.g. `comment.py` (7).
  - PEP 604 `X | None` (8 files) — 2–7 findings each.
- ebg contains **no** PEP 695 generics and no `except*`, so I probed those
  in the scratchpad. `class Registry[T]`, `def parse_generic_fn[T]`,
  `type Alias = …`, and `except* ValueError as eg` all parse: findings are
  emitted with correct symbols and line ranges (verified A/B against
  `class Registry:` and `class Registry(Generic[T])` — identical results).
- **severity:** none — this is the one thing that worked cleanly.

### BUG: `sync_io_in_hotpath` is a file-level finding wearing a function's name and a fabricated line range
- **repo:** ebg (and reproduced minimally in scratchpad)
- **command:** `crimes scan … --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** In ebg's Python, `sync_io_in_hotpath` fires **43 times
  across exactly 43 files** — one per file. Each is attributed to a single
  function symbol, but the `lines` range is `[first blocking call, last
  blocking call]` across the *whole file*. Example:
  `agents/skills/docx/scripts/office/validators/base.py` →
  `symbol: repair_whitespace_preservation`, `lines: [120, 763]`.
  `repair_whitespace_preservation` actually spans **115–141**. Lines 756 and
  763 are inside `_validate_single_file_xsd` (750–785). The finding's range
  crosses 8 unrelated functions.
  Minimal repro (`$S/pp_multi/m.py`, three identical 62-line functions
  `alpha`/`beta`/`gamma`, each with 60 `open()` calls):
  `sync_io_in_hotpath alpha [2, 187] — "180 blocking calls"`.
  There should be three findings of 60 calls each; instead there is one,
  named `alpha`, spanning to line 187 (inside `gamma`).
- **why it matters:** Two separate harms. (1) The line range is *wrong*, not
  merely coarse — anything that renders a code window (`explain`, the
  judging cards, an editor integration) will show a 643-line span and
  attribute other functions' I/O to `repair_whitespace_preservation`.
  (2) The count is inflated: "180 blocking calls" describes a file, while
  the symbol describes a function. A reader opening the named function
  finds 4 of the 17 calls claimed.
- **severity:** real problem

### `large_function` double-counts a function and the callback that is its whole body
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes context 'app/agency/[agency]/(protected)/cases/actions.ts' --no-init`
- **what happened:** The file's `context` view lists 24 findings, 14 of them
  `God Function`. Five are the *same function twice*:
  `applyRegistryTrust [881,1096]` and `<anonymous> [888,1095]`;
  `declareBuyer [381,516]` / `<anonymous> [389,515]`;
  `runEbg`, `savePrimaryContact`, `uploadTrustDeed` likewise. The repo's
  server actions all have the shape
  `export async function f() { …3 lines…; return withTenant(id, async () => { …everything… }) }`
  so the callback is 96–99% of the outer body and both are charged.
  Repo-wide: 10 of 125 `large_function` findings (8%) are an inner
  function covering ≥80% of an outer one. `case-workflow.ts run/<anonymous>`
  and `ownership-stack.ts recordOpenCorporatesOwnershipStack/visit` too.
- **why it matters:** It is the top-ranked file in the scan, so this is the
  first thing a user reads, and a quarter of what they read is a repeat.
  The `<anonymous>` half also carries no actionable identity — "An anonymous
  function is 208 lines long" cannot be located without opening the file.
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes verdict` takes 53 seconds to say "UNCHANGED"
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes verdict --no-init` (cwd = repo)
- **what happened:** `real 53.28 / user 14.26 / sys 3.21`. Output is 8 lines:
  `Verdict: UNCHANGED / New: 0 / Fixed: 0`. `scan --all --format json` on the
  same repo is 6.0s. So verdict is ~9× a full scan, and 39s of the 53s is
  not CPU — it is blocked on git.
- **why it matters:** This is the command intended for a pre-commit / CI
  gate. A minute of wall clock for a no-op answer is the kind of thing
  people remove from their hook after the second time.
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes hotspots` is 27s and disagrees with `scan` about how many files exist
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes hotspots ~/dev/ebg --no-init`
- **what happened:** `real 27.19 / user 8.17` (again git-bound). Header says
  `929 files`; `scan` on the same repo says `files discovered: 546`. Neither
  number is explained, and 929 ≠ 877 tracked files either. Also the top
  three entries all render `risk 1.00 (100%)` — a three-way tie at the cap,
  so the ranking conveys nothing at the top where it matters most.
- **why it matters:** Two commands reporting two different repo sizes with
  no note of why makes both numbers untrustworthy.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `crimes context` prints 270 lines for one file
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes context 'app/agency/…/cases/actions.ts' --no-init`
- **what happened:** 270 lines of output, of which 14 near-identical
  `God Function` blocks each repeat the same 2-sentence boilerplate
  ("Bodies this size usually mix unrelated responsibilities, and an agent
  editing one section often misses interactions in another") verbatim.
  There is no `--top` on `context` (there is on `scan`).
- **why it matters:** `context` is pitched as the pre-edit command for an
  agent. 270 lines with an 8× repeated paragraph is a poor use of a context
  window, and for a human it buries the five genuinely distinct findings
  (Return Shape Roulette, Logic in the Alibi, fan-in/out, Parallel
  Destination) under a wall of size complaints.
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes context` prints finding ids that resolve to different findings under `crimes explain`
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes context 'app/agency/…/cases/actions.ts' --no-init`
  then `crimes explain crime_00001 --no-init`
- **what happened:** `context` labels its findings `id=crime_00001 …
  crime_00024`, numbered locally from 1 for that file. `scan` and `explain`
  agree with each other on a *global* numbering. So:
  - `context` says `crime_00001` = `God Function applyRegistryTrust`,
    `app/agency/…/cases/actions.ts:881–1096`
  - `explain crime_00001` returns `God Function listAmlIdentifiedPeople`,
    `lib/portal-token.ts:478–663`
  - `context` says `crime_00024` = `Parallel Destination` in `actions.ts`;
    `explain crime_00024` returns `God Function run` in
    `trigger/case-workflow.ts`.
  Verified for three ids; `explain` is deterministic across two runs (same
  md5), so this is a collision, not flakiness.
- **why it matters:** `context` is the documented pre-edit command and it
  ends every finding with `id=…`, which reads as an invitation to run
  `crimes explain <that id>`. Doing so silently returns a different file's
  finding. Nothing errors. An agent following the obvious workflow will act
  on the wrong code.
- **severity:** blocker

### `crimes explain` on an unknown id exits 0
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_99999 --no-init`
- **what happened:** prints `crimes: no finding with id or fingerprint
  "crime_99999" — re-run 'crimes scan' and check the output.` and exits **0**.
- **why it matters:** A lookup failure that reports success cannot be
  scripted against. Combined with the id-collision above, a wrapper cannot
  even detect that it asked for something that does not exist.
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes diff` (human) takes 62s and refuses to say what the new crimes are
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~5...HEAD --no-init`
- **what happened:** `real 62.50 / user 14.84`. Entire output:
  `New crimes: 2 / Fixed crimes: 9 / Unchanged crimes: 686`. No file, no
  charge, no line. To find out *which* two, you must re-run the whole 62s
  command with `--format json` and parse it. There is no `--all`, `--top`,
  or verbose flag on `diff`.
- **why it matters:** The single question `diff` exists to answer is "what
  did I just add". It answers "two things" and stops. At a minute per run,
  needing two runs is a two-minute round trip for two lines of information.
- **severity:** real problem

### `high_fan_in_fan_out`: the summary and the score disagree about the importer count
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~5...HEAD --no-init --format json`
- **what happened:** `lib/jurisdiction.ts` — summary and evidence both say
  `fan-in: 12 importers (p95 cutoff: 11, p99: 38)`, while the same finding's
  `scores.blast_radius_importers` is **124** and `blast_radius` is `1`
  (saturated). Two numbers for the same quantity in one object, off by 10×.
- **why it matters:** `blast_radius` is one of the six contract scores and
  it is being computed from a different importer set than the evidence the
  user is shown. Whichever is right, one of them is wrong, and a reader who
  spot-checks against the evidence will conclude the score is fabricated.
- **severity:** real problem

### CORRECTION to the `by_package` entry above: it is gated, not regressed — but the gate excludes real polyglot repos
- **repo:** ebg
- **what happened:** Read `packages/core/src/discovery/by-package.ts`.
  `by_package` requires `MIN_PACKAGES = 2` directories containing one of
  `package.json, pyproject.toml, setup.py, Cargo.toml, go.mod`. ebg has
  exactly one (the root `package.json`). So its absence is correct by
  design. But: the 62-file Python subtree has no `pyproject.toml`/`setup.py`
  — it is a directory of scripts, which is how a great many polyglot repos
  carry their Python — and `Package.swift` is not in `MANIFESTS` at all
  despite 58 Swift files in a proper SPM layout. So `by_package` is absent
  even in the unhidden counterfactual (62 py + 375 js).
- **why it matters:** The feature's own docstring gives the motivating case
  as "a repo that is 70% TypeScript with one Python service". ebg is
  literally that repo and gets nothing, because the detection of "package"
  is manifest-file presence rather than language-directory structure.
- **severity:** real problem

### Honest read of the default human `scan` as a user
- **repo:** ebg
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/ebg --no-init`
- **what happened:** 109 lines. It says "Top files by risk" and then prints
  **every** finding for the top 5 files — 78 findings, not a top-N. The
  claim in the docs is "signal over exhaustiveness, default shows top
  findings only"; the default shows exhaustive findings for a truncated
  file list, which is a different thing.
  - The #1 file's list is **22 of 25 entries that are all size complaints**
    (God Function ×20, God File, plus two Return Shape Roulette on the same
    symbol). The three findings that are *not* about size — Duplicated
    Role/Status/Plan Check, High Fan-In/Fan-Out, Parallel Destination —
    are at positions **23, 24 and 25**, i.e. the last three lines before
    the block ends. The differentiated detectors are ranked below a
    61-line function that is 1.0× the threshold.
  - Items 1 and 3 are the same function (`applyRegistryTrust` and its
    `withTenant` callback). Items 5 and 6 are the same function
    (`findContactDetails` / its inner arrow). So the top of the top file
    repeats itself twice in the first six lines.
  - `lib/config.ts` is 9 consecutive `Environment Roulette` lines, one per
    env var (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `COMPANIES_HOUSE_API_KEY`, …). One
    finding per variable where one per file would say the same thing.
  - Same-file contradiction visible without opening anything:
    `app/agency/[agency]/(protected)/cases/[id]/page.tsx` gets
    `Parallel Destination` ("`/agency/:agency/cases/:id` and
    `/analyst/cases/:id` serve overlapping user intent") at item 13 and
    `Orphaned Destination` ("`/agency/:agency/cases/:id` appears to be
    unreachable from any nav, doc link, or in-repo import") at item 15.
    One says it is a live destination duplicated elsewhere; the other says
    nothing reaches it.
  - **The id line is unusable.** Each block ends with
    `id=crime_00004 … crime_00065` — a *range with an ellipsis*. There is
    no way to get the id of item 23. `explain` takes an id. So the default
    output cannot be fed into the next command in its own workflow.
  - `<anonymous>` appears 6 times as a symbol in the top file. "An
    anonymous function is 208 lines long" is not locatable from the summary.
- **why it matters:** With ~70 detectors, the default view is dominated by
  the two oldest and least differentiated ones. A first-time user's
  impression of the product is "it counts lines", and the findings that
  justify the positioning (agent risk, cross-file authority, IA drift) are
  visually last.
- **severity:** real problem

### `agent_risk` collapses into "big file / big function" on a real repo
- **repo:** ebg
- **what happened:** Top 20 findings by `scores.agent_risk`: **15 of 20**
  are `large_function` or `large_file`. The remaining 5 are `direct_date`
  (2), `return_shape_roulette` (3-way, two of which are the same symbol) and
  `logic_in_comments` (2). Nothing from the 0.16 correctness/authority set,
  nothing from IA drift, appears in the top 20 despite `parallel_destination`
  firing 52× and `orphaned_destination` 39×.
- **why it matters:** `CLAUDE.md` states `agent_risk` "is the differentiator
  and must not be collapsed into severity". Ranked on a real repo it is
  almost perfectly correlated with function length. Whatever the formula
  intends, the observable behaviour is a size ranking.
- **severity:** real problem

### Positive statements (the zero case)
- **repo:** ebg, as scanned (i.e. Python invisible)
- Python detectors produced **zero** findings — correct, there were zero
  `.py` files in the walked set, though for the wrong reason (see the
  dot-directory blocker).
- All three `cross_language_*` detectors produced **zero** — correct given
  one language in the walked set; and `route_drift`/`type_drift` remain
  correctly zero even when Python is made visible, because the shapes they
  need are genuinely absent (no web framework, no `Enum`).
- **Reproducibility:** two consecutive `scan --all --format json` runs
  produced byte-identical finding sets — same 759 findings, same ids, same
  order. No nondeterminism observed.
- No file was written into `~/dev/ebg`. `git status --porcelain` before and
  after shows only the pre-existing untracked
  `docs/aplyid-company-context.md`. No `.crimes/` was created.

### Detector coverage on ebg
- 31 of 68 registered detectors fired; **37 fired zero times**, including
  all three `cross_language_*`, `circular_dependency`, `deep_import`,
  `layer_violation`, `todo_density`, `concept_alias_drift`,
  `missing_agent_context`, `client_exposed_secret`, and the whole
  `*_disagreement` family (`type_`, `unit_`, `default_`, `requiredness_`).
- **severity:** informational, but `deep_import` and `circular_dependency`
  firing zero on an 81k-line Next.js app with a 30-import file is
  surprising enough to be worth a look.

### Follow-up on the `deep_import` zero — mostly justified
- Import-depth histogram over `app lib components trigger`: 241 at depth 2,
  603 at depth 3, 2 at depth 4, 1 at depth 7. The depth-7 one is
  `@/app/agency/[agency]/(protected)/cases/actions`. So a zero is defensible,
  though that single import is exactly the shape the detector describes and
  it was not flagged. `circular_dependency` zero is unverified either way.
- **severity:** annoyance

---
_End of log. Nothing was written to `~/dev/ebg`; final `git status
--porcelain` shows only the pre-existing untracked
`docs/aplyid-company-context.md`, and no `.crimes/` exists._

# buildfest

## Friction log — buildfest (scale / performance case)

**Repo:** `~/dev/buildfest`
**SHA:** `e14ef7c6fa58d220e5b1ec7b754e5792202ccadc` (2026-02-13, "update package publish process")
**Binary:** `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes` — 0.17.0
**Host:** darwin 25.5.0, 10 cores, 64 GB RAM, node v24.18.0
**Default `ulimit -n`:** 1048576 (kern.maxfilesperproc 245760)

Baseline before any crimes run: no `.crimes/` directory; `git status --porcelain`
had 3 pre-existing untracked entries (`.playwright-mcp/`,
`docs/supabase-cleanup-prompts/`, `docs/supabase-triage.md`).

Repo shape: 1529 tracked files (485 `.ts`, 367 `.tsx`, 71 `.js`, 128 `.md`,
116 `.json`, 79 `.sql`), 181,093 LOC across ts/tsx/js/jsx/mjs/cjs, 166 commits.
Working tree on disk is 18 GB (node_modules + playwright videos + test-results);
`.git` is 346 MB.

---

## Entries

### Baseline runs completed, no writes into the repo
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `/usr/bin/time -l ~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** exit 0, clean stderr. 26.67s wall (20.51 user / 2.61 sys), max RSS 308 MB, peak footprint 331 MB. 1556 findings, 2.3 MB JSON. Human `scan` run: 23.48s, max RSS 365 MB, 108 lines out. `git status --porcelain` identical before and after both runs; no `.crimes/` created. `--no-init` behaved.
- **why it matters:** positive result — 1177 files / 181k LOC in 27s single-threaded-ish with a 300 MB ceiling is fine for a repo this size, and read-only really is read-only.
- **severity:** (not friction — recorded as a positive control)

### `.gitignore` is NOT respected — a gitignored build-output directory contaminates 21% of all findings
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `migrate-output/` is line 8 of `~/dev/buildfest/.gitignore` and is a verbatim copy of `apps/airquote`. crimes walked into it anyway. `git ls-files migrate-output | wc -l` = 0; `git check-ignore -v migrate-output` = `.gitignore:8:migrate-output/`.
  - 100 findings are *located in* gitignored files.
  - 229 more findings name `migrate-output` in their summary/evidence — i.e. real source files are flagged because they "duplicate" a directory git has been told to forget.
  - 323 / 1556 findings = **20.8%** of the whole report.
  - `exact_duplicate_block`: **136 of 158 (86%)** are this one directory.
  - It even shows up as a first-class entry in `coverage.by_package` (`migrate-output/airquote`, 69 files) and in the human report's evidence strings as "production files".
  - Cross-check: other gitignored dirs (`node_modules`, `.next`, `dist`, `.turbo`) *were* excluded — because they're in the hardcoded default exclude list, not because `.gitignore` was read. Untracked-but-not-ignored files (`docs/supabase-cleanup-prompts/`) *were* scanned, which is correct. So the built-in defaults work and the `.gitignore` layer does not.
- **why it matters:** CLAUDE.md states the file walker is "fast-glob + `ignore` (respect `.gitignore`)". It isn't respecting it. Every monorepo with a codegen/migration/scratch output directory will get a report where the loudest detector is measuring a directory the maintainer deliberately excluded. This is the single largest precision leak in this scan and it is not a tuning problem — it's a walker bug.
- **severity:** blocker

### Human reporter truncates `evidence` at 2 items — and for duplicates the dropped item is the only useful one
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest --no-init`
- **what happened:** every duplicate line reads e.g. `Exact Duplicate Block   hash 9473ef42a72c… across 2 file(s), 2 occurrence(s), packages/auth/src/providers/UnifiedAuthProvider.tsx:367-395 (checkBusinessAccess)` — it names the file you are already looking at and hides the counterpart behind a truncated hash.
  The JSON is fine — `crime_00014` carries three evidence strings and the third is `packages/core/src/auth.tsx:308-336 (checkBusinessAccess)`. The human renderer prints `evidence[0]` and `evidence[1]` and drops the rest. For `exact_duplicate_block` the dropped one is *the answer*.
- **why it matters:** a duplicate finding is only actionable if you know the other location, and the terminal view is the default view. As shipped you have to re-run with `--format json` and grep the hash. Had the counterpart been printed, the `migrate-output/` contamination above would have been obvious in ten seconds instead of needing a script. Reporter-only fix; the schema already has the data.
- **severity:** real problem

### 251 of 1556 findings (16%) have no `lines` field at all
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes scan ... --format json`
- **what happened:** `lines` is present on 1305 findings and absent on 251. Every `exact_duplicate_block` (158), `high_fan_in_fan_out` (41), `oversized_raster` (13), `duplicate_component_shape` (12), `accessible_interaction_risk` (6), `duplicated_role_status_plan_check` (6), `near_duplicate_block` (5), `design_token_escape` (3), `concept_alias_drift` (2), `docs_code_drift` (2), `circular_dependency` (1), `duplicated_navigation_source` (1) and `svg_with_embedded_raster` (1) lacks it. For duplicates the line range exists but only as free text inside an `evidence` string (`"...:367-395 (checkBusinessAccess)"`).
  Other optional keys: `symbol` 588/1556, `related_files` 369/1556, `discriminator` 225/1556.
- **why it matters:** the JSON output is stated to be the product contract. Any consumer that wants to place a finding in an editor — an LSP shim, a GitHub annotation, an agent deciding what to read — has to string-parse `evidence` for a sixth of all findings, and only for some detectors. Machine-readable line data should not be optional on a location-bearing finding.
- **severity:** real problem

### The human report is file-grouped, so one root cause becomes a wall
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest --no-init` (108 lines, 12.8 KB)
- **what happened:** the report shows 5 files; within each file it prints *every* finding for that file, unranked and uncapped. File 2 (`quote-search.ts`) is 17 findings of which 11 are consecutive `Exact Duplicate Block` lines; file 3 (`credits.ts`) is 21 findings of which 9 are consecutive duplicates. Roughly 40 of the 108 lines are one repeated sentence with a different hex string.
- **why it matters:** "Signal over exhaustiveness" is a stated product constraint and the default view violates it. A user's eye slides off the block, and the genuinely distinct findings above and below it (Policy Doppelgänger, Half-UTC, DST-Naive Day Math) get lost inside it. A per-file cap, or collapsing N duplicates of the same detector into one line with a count, would fix it.
- **severity:** real problem

### Two different file counts in the same eight lines of output
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest --no-init`
- **what happened:** the header says `1556 findings across 558 files`; the footer says `Showing 5 of 481 files.` Nothing explains the 77-file gap (it appears to be the `tests/` + `scripts/` files rolled into "Also flagged elsewhere").
- **why it matters:** small, but it's the first and last number a user reads and they contradict each other. Costs trust for no benefit.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `coverage.files_by_language` collapses TS/TSX/JS into one bucket called `js`
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes scan ... --format json`
- **what happened:** the repo is 485 `.ts` + 367 `.tsx` + 71 `.js`; coverage reports `{"js": 992}` and nothing else. Also 79 tracked `.sql` and 116 `.json` files are invisible — they aren't in `files_by_language` *or* in `universal_only_by_extension` (which only lists `.md` 149 and `.sh` 36).
- **why it matters:** the protocol asks us to verify from coverage that a pack actually saw what we think it saw. With one bucket you cannot tell whether the TSX side of the parser ran at all, and you cannot tell whether the 79 SQL files were skipped deliberately or dropped silently. `files_total` (1177) minus js (992) minus universal-only (185) = 0, so SQL/JSON simply aren't counted anywhere — that's an unexplained hole in the coverage contract.
- **severity:** real problem

### Zero case — positive statement
- **repo:** buildfest (single-language: JS/TS, zero `.py`)
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `coverage.packs_loaded` = `["universal","language-js","language-py"]`. Packs that actually produced findings = `universal` (181) and `language-js` (1375). **`language-py` produced exactly 0 findings** and there are 0 `.py` files — correct. **The three `cross_language_*` detectors produced exactly 0 findings** — correct for a single-language repo; the cross-language pack does not even appear in `packs_loaded`.
- **why it matters:** positive control passed. `packs_loaded` listing `language-py` on a repo with no Python is mildly misleading (it reports load, not applicability, which the protocol already flags) but it did not produce a single false positive.
- **severity:** annoyance (the `packs_loaded` wording only)

### 0.17 surfaces confirmed present
- **repo:** buildfest
- **what happened:** the `large_file` `docs` shape fired on 3 Markdown files with a distinct threshold and an explicit shape line, e.g. `docs/specs/ALLURE_SPEC.md` — `2327 non-empty lines`, `2.3× the configured 1000-line document threshold`, `shape: document (matches .md/.mdx/.markdown/.rst/.adoc/.txt)`. `discriminator` fingerprints are present on 225 findings across `exact_duplicate_block`, `magic_domain_literal_scatter` and `near_duplicate_block`.
- **why it matters:** both 0.17 features are real and visible in the wild, not fixture-only. The document threshold (1000) is clearly separated from the code threshold (300), and the summary text is genuinely different rather than the code copy with a noun swapped.
- **severity:** (positive control)

### FD-LIMIT BUG HUNT — the 0.17.0 bounded pool bounds the fd count but the *failure mode is still silent*, and it is now nondeterministic
- **repo:** buildfest (1177 files, the largest in the corpus)
- **commands:**
  ```bash
  # baseline, unbounded descriptors
  /usr/bin/time -l ~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest --all --format json --no-init

  # the experiment — one run per limit, stdout captured, stderr captured
  bash -c "ulimit -n <L>; cd ~; exec ~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest \
    --all --format json --no-init" > scan-<L>.json 2> scan-<L>.err
  ```
- **what happened:**

  | `ulimit -n` | reps | exit | total findings | `exact_duplicate_block` | `duplicate_component_shape` | `near_duplicate_block` | stderr |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1048576 (default) | 1 | 0 | **1556** | 158 | 12 | 5 | clean |
  | 1024 | 1 | 0 | 1556 | 158 | 12 | 5 | clean |
