  | 256 | 1 | 0 | 1556 | 158 | 12 | 5 | clean |
  | 128 | 1 | 0 | 1556 | 158 | 12 | 5 | clean |
  | 64 | 4 | 0 | 1556 | 158 | 12 | 5 | clean |
  | 48 | 3 | 0 | 1556 | 158 | 12 | 5 | clean |
  | **40** | 3 | 0 | **1522 / 1535 / 1543** | **126 / 137 / 145** | 12 | 3 / 5 / 5 | **clean** |
  | 40 | 1 | **143** | *(empty stdout)* | — | — | — | **clean** |
  | **32** | 2 | 0 | **1391 / 1393** | **6 / 10** | **0** | 4 / 2 | **clean** |
  | 32 | 1 | **143** | *(empty stdout)* | — | — | — | clean |
  | **28** | 2 | 0 | **1386 / 1386** | **5** | **0** | **0** | **clean** |
  | **24** | 1 | 0 | **1383** | **2** | **0** | **0** | **clean** |
  | 16 | 1 | 1 | *(empty)* | — | — | — | `Error: spawn git EMFILE` + raw node stack |
  | 12 | 1 | 1 | *(empty)* | — | — | — | `Error: spawn git EMFILE` + raw node stack |

  **The good news, stated plainly:** at every realistic descriptor budget the fix holds. 1024 (Linux default), 256 (classic macOS default), even 64 and 48 all produce output that is **byte-identical** to the unbounded baseline — `sha256 = d694948ff7ebefbb8c978ce320c907ed3c5e694dd6c8290f8b671ee0bde97c34` across six independent runs at six different limits. `DEFAULT_IO_CONCURRENCY = 32` is correctly sized and the pool really does keep the fd count flat.

  **The bad news:** below ~48 the scan does not crash and does not warn. It **silently reports fewer findings**, and *which* findings it drops changes run to run. Three runs at `ulimit -n 40` on a byte-identical tree returned three different answers (1522, 1535, 1543 findings; 126, 137, 145 duplicates). Every one exited **0** with **empty stderr** and an **identical `coverage` block** — `files_total: 1177`, `packs_loaded: ["universal","language-js","language-py"]`, `by_package` 31 entries. There is nothing in the output a user or an agent could use to tell a degraded scan from a clean one. `summary.high` even stays pinned at 187 in every run, so the headline number is stable while a tenth of the report evaporates.

  **Root cause, located.** It is not `indexes.ts`. `safelyBuildFunctionHashIndex` never fires, because the index build *succeeds* — it succeeds on a truncated corpus. In `packages/core/src/ast-hash/function-index.ts:72-79`:
  ```ts
  const perFile = await mapWithConcurrency(candidate, async (abs) => {
    const hits: FileHits = { exact: [], shape: [] };
    let source: string;
    try {
      source = await readFile(abs, "utf8");
    } catch {
      return hits;          // <-- EMFILE becomes "this file has no functions"
    }
  ```
  `packages/core/src/jsx/shape-index.ts:65-66` has the identical shape. That bare `catch` is presumably there for binaries and races, but it cannot tell `ENOENT` (ignore it) from `EMFILE` (the run is invalid). So each descriptor exhaustion silently deletes one file from the duplicate corpus, the pool moves on, the index reports success, and the detector emits a random subset. That is why the loss is *partial and nondeterministic* rather than all-or-nothing — a strictly worse failure than the index-level `undefined` that `indexes.ts` was designed to produce.

  **Two more failure modes at the bottom end.** At 32 and 40 the process was twice killed with **exit 143 (SIGTERM), empty stdout, and completely empty stderr** — no message at all. And at 16 and below the very first `spawn git` throws an unhandled `'error'` event, so the user gets a raw `node:events:487 throw er;` stack trace with an `errno: -24` object rather than a sentence. Neither path is handled.

  **Portability caveat worth flagging:** the pool is a fixed 32 and the docstring reasons about macOS 256 / Linux 1024, which is sound. But this box reports `ulimit -n 1048576`, so the *stock* configuration was never anywhere near the cliff — I had to construct it. The margin between "works" (48) and "silently wrong" (40) is 8 descriptors. Anything else in the process holding fds — a language pack that opens a tree-sitter WASM blob, a `git` subprocess, a future parallel parser — eats directly into that margin.
- **why it matters:** the 0.17.0 release note claims the fd fan-out is fixed. Half of it is: the descriptor count is genuinely bounded and the numbers above prove it at every realistic limit. The half that is not fixed is the one that actually costs users trust — **a degraded scan is indistinguishable from a clean one**, and now it is nondeterministically degraded, so two engineers on the same commit can disagree about what the tool said and neither can prove anything. The fix is small and does not touch detector behaviour: re-throw (or count and surface) `EMFILE`/`ENFILE` in those two `catch` blocks, and put a read-failure count into `coverage` so "the scan was complete" becomes an assertable fact instead of an assumption.
- **severity:** blocker (the silent-degradation half); the descriptor bound itself: **fixed, verified**

### FD sweep — final numbers (supersedes the reps counts in the table above)
- **repo:** buildfest
- **what happened:** completed sweep, 2-3 reps at each low limit. Confirms the *variance*, which is the point:
  - `40`: 1522 / 1535 / 1543 findings (edb 126 / 137 / 145) — **three different answers on an unchanged tree**, plus one SIGTERM kill.
  - `32`: 1391 (edb 6, dcs 0) / 1393 (edb 10, dcs 0), plus one SIGTERM kill.
  - `28`: 1386 / 1386 (edb 5, dcs 0, ndb 0) — stable but wrong.
  - `24`: 1383 (edb 2) / 1384 (edb 1) / 1388 (edb 6, dcs 1) — **three different answers again**.
  - `20`, `16`, `12`: `Error: spawn git EMFILE`, unhandled `'error'` event, raw stack trace, exit 1, empty output. At least this one is loud.
  - Every exit-0 run above had **empty stderr** and an **identical `coverage` block**.
- **severity:** blocker (same defect as the entry above; this is the reproducibility evidence for it)

### DETERMINISM CHECK — clean pass, no caveats
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest --all --format json --no-init`, run 3× on an unchanged tree
- **what happened:** all three runs exit 0 with **0 bytes on stderr** and are **byte-identical**, `sha256 = d694948ff7ebefbb8c978ce320c907ed3c5e694dd6c8290f8b671ee0bde97c34`, before *and* after stripping every `id` field (id-stripped `sha256 = e8f14698482f…`, also identical across all three). Because `id` is assigned in rank order, byte-identity with ids included is the stronger statement: the *ranking* is stable too, not just the finding set.
  Counting the six fd-limit runs at 64/128/256/512/1024/default, that is **9 independent runs producing the same 2,412,426 bytes**. The three detectors named in the 0.17.0 note are covered directly: `exact_duplicate_block` (158 findings, 158 identical `hash <12-hex>… across N file(s)` evidence strings), `near_duplicate_block` (5), `magic_domain_literal_scatter` (62). No drift in the finding set, the evidence strings, the scores, or the ordering.
- **why it matters:** the 0.17.0 reproducibility claim holds, and the sorted-insertion fix in `function-index.ts` is doing what its docstring says. This is the cleanest result of the run.
- **severity:** (positive control — pass)

### `churn` is 0.00 on all 1556 findings, and only `explain` ever tells you why
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `scores.churn == 0` and `scores.recency == 0` for **every single one of the 1556 findings**. The repo has 166 commits and is not shallow — but its last commit is 2026-02-13 and today is 2026-08-02, so the default 90-day window contains **zero** commits (`git rev-list --count --since=90.days HEAD` = 0; `--since=365.days` = 166).
  So the number is *correct*. The problem is that nothing says so. `scan` prints `Risk: churn low` on every file — "low" is a claim about the code; the truth is "not measured". Only `crimes explain` ever renders the real sentence: `churn: 0.00 — no commits touched this file in the last 90 days`. That string exists in the codebase and is never shown in the command a user actually runs.
- **why it matters:** churn is one of the six scores and a stated differentiator. On any repo that has been quiet for a quarter — a library between releases, a repo you just cloned, anything you inherited — a sixth of the scoring model silently zeroes out and the ranking degenerates, and the default output actively misreports it as a low-risk signal rather than an absent one. `scan` should say "churn: not measured (no commits in window)" and, ideally, widen or suggest widening the window.
- **severity:** real problem

### `hotspots` default 90d window makes the command actively misleading — and its top hit is a PNG
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes hotspots ~/dev/buildfest --no-init` (54.6s — the slowest command in the suite)
- **what happened:** with zero commits in the default window, every file scores an identical `risk 0.40 (40%)` with `0 changes`, so the "ranking" collapses to alphabetical order. The #1 hotspot in a 181k-line TypeScript monorepo is:
  ```
    1. apps/airquote/public/images/airquote-header.png  risk 0.40 (40%)
       0 changes
       1 finding · highest high
  ```
  Entries 2-10 are also all `risk 0.40 · 0 changes`, all under `apps/airquote/` because "a" sorts first.
  Run with a window that actually contains history and it is a genuinely good command: `crimes hotspots ~/dev/buildfest --since 1y` (36.9s) returns `package.json` at `risk 0.91 · 17 changes · latest 2026-02-13`, then `scripts/dev-health-check.ts` at `0.84 · 23 changes`, then `apps/bossary/src/app/layout.tsx` at `0.82 · 14 changes` — real spread, real dates, obviously useful.
  Separately: the header file count changes with the window — `since 90d · 558 files` vs `since 1y · 3101 files` — so the same field means two different things depending on a flag.
- **why it matters:** a churn-ranked command whose churn input is empty should refuse or warn, not emit a confidently-ordered list of constants. As shipped, the first thing a new user runs on a quiet repo tells them their header image is their biggest risk. That is the kind of first impression that ends the trial. Fix is cheap: detect zero commits in window, print one line, and suggest `--since`.
- **severity:** blocker (for first-run credibility on any repo quiet for >90 days)

### `crimes diff` human output is three integers and names nothing
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~20...HEAD --no-init` (56.5s; 937 files changed in the range)
- **what happened:** the entire output is 7 lines:
  ```
  CRIMES DIFF
  base: HEAD~20
  head: HEAD

  New crimes: 76
  Fixed crimes: 37
  Unchanged crimes: 1046
  ```
  No file, no line, no charge, no way to see what the 76 new ones are. `--help` offers `--format`, `--no-color`, `--root`, `--show-suppressed`, `--fail-on` — there is no `--all` or `-v`. Meanwhile `--format json` returns `new_findings` as a fully populated 76-element array with types and paths (`{"type":"large_function","file":"packages/auth/src/ui/sign-up-form.tsx"}`).
- **why it matters:** the whole point of `diff` is "what did this branch introduce". The data is right there in the same process and the human renderer throws it away. This is the single highest value-per-line-of-code fix in the list — it is purely presentational and it turns a useless command into the one you would wire into a pre-push hook.
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes verdict` spends 68 seconds to compare a commit with itself and doesn't mention that
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes verdict --no-init` (cwd = repo), 67.8s — the slowest command measured
- **what happened:** exits 0 with `Verdict: UNCHANGED / New: 0 / Fixed: 0 / Reason: no new findings and no fixed findings`. The actual reason is that `origin/main` and `HEAD` are the same commit (`e14ef7c…` both) and `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD` is empty. It ran two full scans of 1177 files to discover that.
- **why it matters:** 68 seconds is a long time to be told nothing, and the stated reason is not the real reason. A `git rev-parse` comparison up front costs microseconds and would let it say "base and head are the same commit — nothing to compare" instantly. Users will read "UNCHANGED" as "I checked and it's fine" rather than "I compared X to X".
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes context` is the best command in the tool
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes context apps/airquote/src/lib/credits.ts --no-init` (cwd = repo), **2.13s**, 83 lines
- **what happened:** fast, scoped, and led with the thing an agent actually needs — `Agent guidance` (3 imperative lines), `Related files` with a stated reason for each (`shares domain token "credits"`), `Likely tests`, then findings with full evidence. This is the one output I would keep in an agent loop.
  Two caveats. (a) Run from outside the repo it fails with `crimes: file not found: apps/...` and exit 2 — `--root` exists and would have fixed it, but the error message doesn't mention it. (b) It silently resolved the root to the nested package `apps/airquote`, not the monorepo root: the header reads `file: src/lib/credits.ts` and it reports **6** findings where the monorepo-wide `scan` reported **21** for the same file. Neither number is wrong, but nothing tells you the scope changed, and the `id=crime_00001` it prints is local to that narrowed run and does not correspond to the `crime_00477` the full scan gave the same finding.
- **why it matters:** mostly a positive result, but (b) is a trap: ids printed by `context` look like ids printed by `scan` and are not interchangeable. Either qualify them or drop them from `context`.
- **severity:** annoyance

### The "stable fingerprint" is not in the JSON, and `explain --help` documents the wrong shape for a sixth of findings
- **repo:** buildfest
- **commands:**
  ```bash
  crimes explain "exact_duplicate_block::packages/auth/.../UnifiedAuthProvider.tsx::checkBusinessAccess"   # exit 2
  crimes explain "exact_duplicate_block::packages/auth/.../UnifiedAuthProvider.tsx::::9473ef42a72c"        # exit 0
  ```
- **what happened:** `explain --help` says the fingerprint is `<type>::<file>::<symbol>`. That works for symbol-bearing detectors (verified on `name_behavior_mismatch::…::getSupabaseClient`). For the 251 findings with no `symbol` the real shape is four parts with an empty slot — `<type>::<file>::::<discriminator>` — which is documented nowhere except inside `explain`'s own suppression hint, i.e. you must already have run `explain` by id to learn how to call `explain` by fingerprint. And there is **no `fingerprint` field in the scan JSON at all** (keys are `id, type, charge, severity, confidence, file, summary, evidence, effort, fix_shape, scores, suggested_actions, pack, detector_id, tier` + optional `lines, symbol, related_files, discriminator`).
  My first attempt at the 3-part form also cost a **28-second full re-scan** before failing, because `explain` without `--from` re-scans the repo and gives no indication it is about to.
- **why it matters:** the fingerprint is sold as the durable handle — it's what `crimes ignore` takes and what any suppression file or CI baseline would key on. A durable handle that isn't emitted in the machine-readable output, and whose documented format is wrong for 16% of findings, isn't durable. Emitting `fingerprint` on every finding is a small additive schema change and removes the whole class of problem.
- **severity:** real problem

### Honest read of the default `scan` output, as a user
- **repo:** buildfest
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/buildfest --no-init` — 23.5s, **108 lines / 12.8 KB**
- **what happened:** the length is right. 108 lines is a screen and a half; I read the whole thing without scrolling fatigue, and the closing line — `→ Start with 'crimes context apps/dev-portal/src/app/admin/settings/page.tsx' — it concentrates the most risk in this scan` — is a genuinely good ending: one command, one file, no ambiguity.
  What it gets wrong is *within* the frame. The five files it picked are reasonable, but roughly 40 of the 108 lines are `Exact Duplicate Block   hash <hex>… across 2 file(s), 2 occurrence(s)` repeated with a different hex string, and (per the entries above) 86% of those are a `.gitignore`d directory and none of them name the other file. So the densest third of the report is simultaneously the least trustworthy and the least actionable part of it, and it sits directly on top of the findings I'd actually act on (`Policy Doppelgänger · plan plans rule — normalised rule: ($expr || .free), 13 occurrence(s) across 5 file(s)`, `Half-UTC, Half-Local`, `DST-Naive Day Math`), which are individually excellent.
  It does lead with what matters at the *file* level and buries the lede at the *finding* level. My honest reaction: I trusted line 2, skimmed lines 30-70, and re-engaged at the last line.
- **why it matters:** the ordering machinery is fine — it's the per-file expansion that is uncapped. Collapsing repeated same-detector findings into one line with a count would cut ~35 lines of noise and surface ~10 distinct findings currently hidden inside the block, with no scoring change.
- **severity:** real problem

### `.gitignore` bug — minimal reproduction (6 lines, outside any real repo)
- **repo:** synthetic, built in scratch — confirms the buildfest finding is a general walker defect, not a `migrate-output/` quirk
- **command:**
  ```bash
  mkdir -p t/src t/generated && cd t && git init -q .
  printf 'generated/\n' > .gitignore
  printf 'export function alpha(x: number) {\n  const now = new Date();\n  return x + now.getTime();\n}\n' > src/a.ts
  cp src/a.ts generated/a.ts
  git add -A && git commit -qm init

  git ls-files                       # -> .gitignore src/a.ts        (generated/ correctly untracked)
  git check-ignore -v generated/a.ts # -> .gitignore:1:generated/    generated/a.ts

  crimes scan t --all --format json --no-init
  ```
- **what happened:**
  ```
  files_total: 2
  findings: 2
  files with findings: src/a.ts, generated/a.ts
  ```
  crimes walks straight into a directory that `git check-ignore` reports as ignored by the repo's own `.gitignore`, counts it in `coverage.files_total`, and emits findings from it.
- **why it matters:** this reduces the 21%-contamination result on buildfest to a one-line cause and makes it trivially testable. `node_modules`/`dist`/`.next`/`.turbo` are excluded by the hardcoded default list, which is why the bug has stayed invisible — those cover the common cases. Any project-specific ignore (codegen output, migration scratch, vendored snapshots, fixture corpora) is scanned as production code. Given `exact_duplicate_block` is inherently cross-file, one ignored copy of a directory poisons findings in the *real* files too, which is how 34 ignored files produced 323 contaminated findings.
- **severity:** blocker

---

## Top 5, ranked by how much they cost the product

1. **`.gitignore` is ignored** — blocker, one-line repro, 21% of this repo's findings contaminated and 86% of `exact_duplicate_block`. Walker fix.
2. **Silent, nondeterministic degradation under descriptor pressure** — blocker. The 0.17.0 pool bound genuinely works at every realistic `ulimit -n`; the *failure mode* below ~48 is still exit 0, empty stderr, identical `coverage`, and a different answer each run. Cause is the bare `catch` around `readFile` in `ast-hash/function-index.ts:77` and `jsx/shape-index.ts:66`.
3. **`hotspots` on a repo quiet for >90 days** — blocker for first impressions. Constant risk, alphabetical order, a PNG at #1. Needs a zero-commits-in-window guard.
4. **`crimes diff` human output is three integers** — real problem, pure reporter gap, the 76 new findings are already in the JSON.
5. **The duplicate-block wall in `scan`, and the missing counterpart file** — real problem. ~40 of 108 lines are one repeated sentence, and the one datum that would make them actionable (`evidence[2]`, the other file) is truncated by the human renderer.

**Honourable mentions:** `churn` silently 0 on every finding with only `explain` explaining why; `lines` absent on 16% of findings; no `fingerprint` field in the JSON while `crimes ignore` requires one; `verdict` burning 68s to compare a commit with itself.

**What worked, stated plainly:** determinism is perfect (9 byte-identical runs). Performance is good (27s, 308 MB peak for 1177 files / 181k LOC). Read-only is genuinely read-only (`--no-init` honoured, no `.crimes/`, no stray worktrees, `git status` byte-identical before and after ~30 command invocations). The zero cases pass (`language-py` loaded, 0 findings, 0 `.py`; `cross_language_*` 0 findings on a single-language repo). Both 0.17 surfaces are real in the wild. `crimes context` (2.1s) and `crimes explain --from` are the two best outputs in the tool.

---

# zulip

## Friction log — zulip

- **repo:** `~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/zulip`
- **SHA:** `f6470bb378888dc5f274aafd426ee79880550dc5`
- **build:** crimes 0.17.0 (`~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes`)
- **ulimit -n:** 1048576
- **size:** 8919 tracked files; 2016 `.py` (418,474 lines), 471 `.ts` (150,490 lines),
  12 `.js` (3,546), 224 `.html` (17,925), 469 `.hbs`, 411 `.md` + 464 `.mdx`.
  Working tree 296M (154M of it `.git`).
- **role in round:** second independent test of the 0.15.0 `cross_language_*`
  detectors, and a partial CONTROL — Zulip has a famously strict review culture.

Entries appended in the order they happened.

---

### Timed baseline
- **command:** `/usr/bin/time -l crimes scan $R --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** exit 0, **81.27s wall** (45.11 user / 5.04 sys), **max RSS 397,197,312 B = 378.8 MiB**, peak footprint 453,686,368 B. 5,234,432 B of JSON. Nothing on stderr.
- **why it matters:** baseline. Memory is fine; 81s on a 570k-line repo is tolerable but not fast.
- **severity:** n/a

### The human `scan` took 1.8x longer than the `--all` JSON scan
- **command:** `crimes scan $R --no-init` vs `crimes scan $R --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** JSON `--all` = 81.3s. Plain human scan = **147.8s**. The human scan renders *fewer* findings (130 lines of output vs 3,759 findings) yet takes nearly twice as long. Run-to-run variance on the same machine, same repo, nothing else running.
- **why it matters:** either detection is nondeterministically slow or the human renderer is doing real work per finding it then throws away. Either way "the default command is the slowest one" is a bad first impression — a new user's very first `crimes scan` on a large repo is a 2.5-minute wait with no progress output past the spinner.
- **severity:** real problem

### First human `scan` was killed with exit 143 at 22.8s
- **command:** `/usr/bin/time -p crimes scan $R --no-init > out 2> err`
- **what happened:** `EXIT=143` (SIGTERM), `time: command terminated abnormally`, 0 bytes of output written. Immediate identical re-run succeeded. Not reproduced on retry.
- **why it matters:** could be environmental (sandbox), but crimes produced **no partial output and no error line** — a user who hits this sees an empty file and no explanation. Recorded, not diagnosed.
- **severity:** annoyance (unreproduced)

### *** ZERO cross-language findings on a textbook client/server repo ***
- **command:** `crimes scan $R --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `cross_language_route_drift`, `cross_language_type_drift`, `cross_language_concept_alias_drift` = **0 / 0 / 0**. And `coverage.by_package` is **absent from the JSON entirely**, and `"cross"` is **not in `coverage.packs_loaded`** (`["universal","language-js","language-py"]`).
- **why it matters:** Zulip is the canonical shape these detectors were written for — 2,016 Python files with Django URL routing in `zproject/urls.py`, consumed by 471 TypeScript files that call those endpoints via `channel.get/post`. If the detector can't see this pair, the 0.15.0 pack has never fired outside its own fixtures on either dogfood repo. Diagnosed below.
- **severity:** blocker (for the 0.15 surface)

### Detector count table (3,759 findings, 572k lines of code = 6.6 findings / 1000 lines)
- `large_function.py` **898**, `large_function.js` **563** → **39% of the entire report is one detector.**
- `large_file` 355, `deep_import.py` 275, `commented_out_code` 267, `boolean_naming_drift` .py 265 + .js 190 = **455**.
- Those six lines are **2,813 / 3,759 = 75%** of the report.
- **why it matters:** on the most heavily-reviewed repo in the corpus. See control assessment.
- **severity:** real problem
### Cross-language diagnosis — three independent structural misses, all confirmed by running the parsers directly
- **command:** custom probe over `packages/language-py/dist` + `packages/language-js/dist` on all 8,919 Zulip files
- **what happened:**
  - `PY ROUTES total = 0` across **2,016 Python files, 0 parse errors**.
  - `JS FETCHSITES total = 0` across all `.ts`.
  - `PY CLOSED SETS = 0`; `JS CLOSED SETS = 25`; **name matches = 0**.
- **causes, in order:**
  1. **Routes are only read from decorators.** `routeFromDecorator()` in `packages/language-py/src/parse/routes.ts` is the *sole* route source. Zulip declares **387** URLs as Django URLconf **function calls** — `rest_path("realm/domains", GET=…, POST=…)` and `path(…)` inside `v1_api_and_json_patterns` in `zproject/urls.py`. Repo-wide count of `@app.get`-shaped decorators: **0**. Django/Flask-`add_url_rule`/`urlpatterns` is the dominant Python web idiom and the extractor cannot see it at all. The FastAPI decorator shape is the *only* one supported.
  2. Even if `path()` were parsed, `routeFromDecorator` rejects any path not starting with `/`. Every Zulip route is relative (`"realm"`, `"messages/<int:message_id>"`) because the prefix comes from `include()`. So a `path()` extractor would still need prefix composition to produce anything.
  3. **Frontend calls are only read from `fetch(...)` or `axios|http|client|api|request` receivers with a positional string URL.** Zulip makes **140** calls through its own `channel` module: `channel.post({url: "/json/typing", data, success})`. Receiver `channel` fails `REQUEST_RECEIVERS`; and the URL lives in an **object-literal first argument**, which `literalText()` returns `undefined` for. jQuery-`$.ajax({url})` / any options-bag HTTP wrapper is invisible. This is a very common shape.
  4. **`cross_language_type_drift` is structurally impossible here.** The TS half only accepts **string-literal** unions (25 found). The Python half only records a member value when the RHS is a **string** node. All 29 of Zulip's `Enum`/`IntEnum` classes use **integer** values (`MEMBERS_ONLY = 1`). String-enum-vs-string-union is a narrow slice of real Python.
  5. **`cross_language_concept_alias_drift`** requires an IA alias group whose aliases split *strictly disjointly* by language. Zulip's textbook case — backend `Stream`, product-facing `channel` — did not produce one.
- **why it matters:** two dogfood repos, zero cross-language findings. The 0.15.0 pack has still never fired outside its own fixtures. The failure is not calibration, it is **coverage of idioms**: FastAPI-decorator + bare-`fetch` + string-enum is one narrow stack, and Zulip (Django + jQuery wrapper + IntEnum) misses all three.
- **severity:** blocker

### `packs_loaded` cannot tell you whether the cross-language pack ran
- **what happened:** `coverage.packs_loaded = ["universal","language-js","language-py"]`. `"cross-language"` is **never** a member — `buildCoverage()` computes it as `["universal", ...router.registeredPacks()]`, and the cross slate is not a language router pack. Every cross finding is emitted with the pack of its anchor file anyway (there are none here to check).
- **why it matters:** the protocol explicitly warns "`packs_loaded` reports what was loaded, not what ran". For the cross pack it doesn't even report that. There is **no field in the JSON** that distinguishes "cross-language ran and found nothing" from "cross-language never ran". I had to read the source and re-run the parsers by hand to answer the round's key question.
- **severity:** real problem

### `coverage.by_package` is absent on a textbook polyglot repo
- **what happened:** Zulip has `package.json` + `pyproject.toml` **both at the repo root**, plus one nested `starlight_help/package.json`. `buildByPackage()` requires `MIN_PACKAGES = 2` *nested* (non-root) package dirs; Zulip has 1, so the field is dropped.
- **why it matters:** `by_package` is the 0.15.0 answer to "which part is the Python one", and the single most common polyglot layout — one root manifest per language — never qualifies. The field only appears for pnpm/uv-style workspaces, not for the client/server repos the cross pack targets.
- **severity:** real problem

### `contract_drift` summaries name the same identifier twice and read as nonsense
- **example:** "``CustomProfileFieldData`` and ``CustomProfileFieldData`` appear to describe the same record but disagree on 3 fields."
- **why it matters:** the sentence is literally "X and X disagree". A user cannot tell from the summary that these are two *different declarations in different files*; the disambiguation is only in `evidence`. Every same-name pair (most of the 15) reads this way.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `contract_drift` matched three pairs of local variables both called `parsed`
- **files:** `web/src/invite.ts:224` vs `stream_edit_subscribers.ts:343`, `user_profile.ts:633`, `user_group_edit.ts:2209`
- **what happened:** three separate `high`/`medium` findings claiming `parsed` and `parsed` "describe the same record". They are unrelated local zod parse results in unrelated modules that happen to share a throwaway variable name and both have a `code` field.
- **why it matters:** the concept-matcher normalises on the identifier, and `parsed`/`result`/`data`/`opts` are throwaway names, not concepts. 3 of 15 `contract_drift` findings (20%) come from one such name.
- **severity:** real problem
### CORRECTION to the probe above — `parsePyFile` is async; my first two probes silently read a Promise
- **what happened:** `parsePyFile()` returns a Promise. Reading `.functions` off it yields `undefined`; my probe printed `PY CLOSED SETS = 0` when the real answer is 19. Re-ran with `await`.
- **why it matters:** noting it because it is *my* error, not crimes', and because the corrected numbers change one of the three diagnoses below from "structurally impossible" to "ran and legitimately found nothing".
- **severity:** n/a (self-correction)

### Corrected cross-language numbers (all with `await`)
```
PY: files=2016  functions=13311  classes=2906  filesWithSyntaxErrors=0
PY ROUTES        = 0     <- route_drift early-returns on backend.length===0
PY CLOSED SETS   = 19
JS FETCHSITES    = 0     <- route_drift would early-return here too
JS CLOSED SETS   = 25
NAME MATCHES     = 0     <- type_drift RAN and found nothing
```
- `cross_language_route_drift`: **blocked**, never reached its comparison. Two independent causes (Django URLconf `path()`/`rest_path()` not read; `channel.post({url})` options-bag not read).
- `cross_language_type_drift`: **ran**, both sides non-empty, zero name matches. Genuine zero. But 14 of the 19 Python "closed sets" are junk — the `allValued` fallback grabs any class whose members are all string constants, e.g. `Integration` (members are image-path templates `images/integrations/logos/{name}.png`), `AppleAuthMixin` (OAuth URLs), `MissingEventHeaderTestCase` (a test fixture URL). Only ~5 are actual enums. So even the input pool is mostly noise.
- `cross_language_concept_alias_drift`: **ran** (`aliasGroups` non-empty — the single-language `concept_alias_drift.js` fired once), zero groups split strictly-disjointly across languages. Zulip's own famous rename (backend `Stream` / product-facing "channel") was not caught.
- **severity:** blocker for route_drift; real problem for the type_drift input pool.

### Python parser health — clean
- 2,016 `.py` files, **0 with `hasSyntaxErrors`**, 13,311 functions + 2,906 classes extracted.
- Spot checks with exact function counts: `zproject/backends.py` 177/177 defs, `webhooks/github/view.py` 76/76, `lib/digest.py` 25/25, `lib/onboarding.py` 8/8.
- `match` (PEP 634, 7 files) parses: `build_preset_config` at `webhooks/common.py:84–112` bounds the `match` at :92 correctly. Walrus parses: `send_initial_realm_messages` 326–589 spans the `:=` at :462. PEP 604 `-> int | None` (355 files) parses.
- **Not exercised here:** PEP 695 generics (`def f[T]`), `type X =` statements, `except*` — Zulip is `requires-python = ">=3.10"` and uses none of them. Zero occurrences repo-wide. So 3.12 syntax remains untested by this repo.
- **severity:** n/a — positive result

### `large_function.py` charges "God Function" on a clean 9-line function, and its own evidence says the threshold was not exceeded
- **finding:** `zerver/tornado/event_queue.py:1602 process_realm_user_add_event`
- **summary:** "`process_realm_user_add_event` is 9 lines and nests 4 levels deep."
- **evidence[0]:** `"9 lines (threshold 50 for shape \"domain\")"`
- **the code:** a 9-line event fan-out — `for user: for client: if accepts: if x: continue; add_event()`. Idiomatic, reviewed, nothing wrong with it.
- **scale:** **172 of 898 `large_function.py` findings (19%)** state a line count at or *below* the threshold they quote. Every one is charged "God Function".
- **why it matters:** the detector is really firing on `nesting depth >= 4`, but the charge, the summary's first clause, and the primary evidence line are all about *size*. A user reading "God Function — 9 lines (threshold 50)" concludes the tool is broken, and they are not wrong to. In Python, where every `for`/`with`/`if`/`try` is an indent level, depth 4 inside a function body is completely ordinary.
- **why it matters more:** `large_function` (.py 898 + .js 563 = **1,461**) is **39% of the whole report**. A fifth of its Python half is self-contradicting.
- **severity:** real problem
### `blast_radius` is saturated for half the report, and the human output prints a number a user can disprove in 5 seconds
- **command:** `crimes scan $R --no-init`
- **what happened:** `blast_radius_importers` takes only **37 distinct values across 3,759 findings**. Two of them account for most of it: **798** (866 findings) and **324** (825 findings). No file ever gets two different values. `blast_radius` is `min(transitive_importers / 50, 1)`, so **1,758 findings (47%) are pinned at the maximum, 1.0**, and another 1,471 are at 0. The score is effectively ternary.
- **the disprovable claim:** the human report says of `zerver/data_import/slack.py` — "blast top-quartile (**798 importers**)". Actual direct importers of `zerver.data_import.slack`: **5**. For `web/src/inbox_ui.ts` it prints 324; actual: **4**. For `corporate/lib/stripe.py` it prints 798; actual: **49**.
- **why it matters:** the number is a *transitive* closure, but nothing in the output says so, and in a monolith with import cycles the closure collapses to one giant number shared by everything. Two consequences: (a) `blast_radius` cannot rank anything in a repo of this shape, and it is one of the six scores the aggregate ranking depends on; (b) a maintainer who checks the one concrete number in the risk line finds it off by 160x and stops trusting the rest.
- **severity:** real problem

### The default human report is 26 lines of "God Function" before anything interesting
- **command:** `crimes scan $R --no-init`
- **what happened:** 130 lines total. The top file's section is 37 lines, of which **26 are `God Function`**. The genuinely interesting findings in that section — `Blocking the Hot Path`, `Catch and Release`, `Abstraction Laundering`, `False Identity`, `Logic in the Alibi` — are ranked *below* the twentieth God Function. In `corporate/lib/stripe.py` the God Function list runs from a 291-line function down to a **27-line** one before the first non-`large_function` finding appears.
- **and the self-contradiction is right in the default output**, at #17 of the top file: "God Function · `get_price_per_license()`  **27 lines (threshold 50** for shape "domain"), nesting depth 4".
- **why it matters:** the lede is buried by an order of magnitude. A maintainer's first impression of crimes on their repo is "it counts lines and calls me names about it". The one detector that is 39% of the report is the least differentiated thing crimes does — every linter has a function-length rule, and Zulip has consciously chosen not to enable one.
- **severity:** real problem

### "Showing 5 of 1240 files"
- The default view shows **0.4%** of the files with findings. There is no ordering explanation, no "and here is the one thing to do". The closing hint (`→ Start with crimes context corporate/lib/stripe.py`) is the single best line in the output and it is the last one.
- **severity:** annoyance

### Nice: the tool quotes the exact call shape its own cross-language detector cannot parse
- Human output line 119: ``Catch and Release · check_thumbnail_status → get   protected operation: const response: unknown = await channel.get({ url: `/json/thumbnail/status/${path_id}`, })``
- `swallowed_error.js` reads `channel.get({url: ...})` fine. `collectFetchSite` does not. The two collectors disagree about what an HTTP call looks like inside the same parser.
- **severity:** real problem (it is the fix hint for the cross-language miss)
### `commented_out_code` charges Zulip for having the best explanatory comments in the corpus
- **command:** `crimes context zerver/lib/narrow.py --no-init`
- **what happened:** 9 of the 23 findings on that file are `Commented-Out Code`. Two examples, verbatim from the source:
  - `narrow.py:243–257` — *"This class has an important security invariant: None of these methods ever \*add\* messages to a query's result…"* — 15 lines of prose documenting a security invariant.
  - `narrow.py:1055–1071` — 17 lines of prose explaining topic-name HTML escaping and why `process_fts_updates` needs no special handling.
- **the trigger:** evidence says `">=3 lines contain code-shaped tokens (def/fn/function/class/let/var/const/etc.)"`. In the first block the tokens are the English words **"class"** ("This class has an important…"), `add_term`, `query.filter`. In the second, **"function"** ("The `escape_html` function is used to…").
- **the summary:** *"Code left in comments stales — agents may revive incorrect snippets."* It is not code. Nothing to revive.
- **scale:** 293 `commented_out_code` findings (267 universal + 26 js) — **7.8% of the whole report**.
- **why it matters:** this is the single most backwards result of the run. Zulip's long, careful, prose-heavy comments are the reason its code is maintainable, and they are exactly the artefact an agent benefits from most. crimes charges them as a defect. On the control repo, the detector is inverted.
- **severity:** blocker (for this detector)

### `todo_density` fires below its own stated threshold 62% of the time
- **evidence, verbatim:** `["2× TODO", "10.1 markers per 1k LOC (threshold 10)"]` — two TODOs in a ~200-line file.
- **45 of 73 `todo_density` findings (62%)** quote a per-1k figure at or below the threshold in the same string.
- **why it matters:** third detector in this repo whose evidence line reads as a self-refutation (after `large_function.py` at 19% and `large_file`). The per-1k normalisation means any short file with 2 markers trips a "too many" charge. The summary — "too many in one file" — is false for 2 TODOs.
- **severity:** real problem

### `context`'s "Related files" is `ls` output, alphabetical
- **command:** `crimes context zerver/lib/narrow.py --no-init`
- **what happened:**
  ```
  Related files
    · zerver/lib/__init__.py  — same directory
    · zerver/lib/addressee.py — same directory
    · zerver/lib/alert_words.py — same directory
    · zerver/lib/async_utils.py — same directory
    · zerver/lib/attachments.py — same directory
    … and 5 more
  ```
  `zerver/lib/` has ~200 files. These are the first five alphabetically. `narrow.py` imports none of them. The reason given is literally "same directory".
- **why it matters:** `context` is the command whose whole value proposition is "give an agent the right context before it edits". Handing an agent five arbitrary alphabetical neighbours is worse than handing it nothing — it burns context window on noise. Meanwhile the real answer (`narrow.py` imports `zerver.models`, is imported by `zerver/views/message_fetch.py`) exists in the import index and is not used.
- **severity:** real problem

### `context` on Python otherwise works and is genuinely agent-shaped
- Positives worth recording: the `Agent guidance` block is concrete and correct; `Likely tests → zerver/tests/test_message_fetch.py` is exactly right; `Clues` (churn 2 commits / 90d, 2 authors, last touched 2 weeks ago) is the kind of thing an agent cannot get cheaply. 25s for a 1,657-line file is slow but tolerable.
- The signal-to-noise is the problem, not the shape: 23 findings of which 12 are God Function, 9 are prose comments, 1 is TODOs. **1** (`Boolean Naming Drift`) is arguably novel.
- **severity:** n/a — mixed positive
### `dependency_provenance_gap` applies the npm manifest model to Python and charges Zulip HIGH for importing the standard library
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_01348 --from zulip.json`
- **evidence, verbatim:**
  ```
  · 450 external package(s) imported with no declaring manifest
  ·   `_typeshed` — imported at zerver/lib/test_console_output.py
  ·   `abc`       — imported at corporate/lib/stripe.py
  ·   `ahocorasick` — imported at zerver/lib/alert_words.py
  ·   `aioapns`   — imported at zerver/lib/push_notifications.py
  ·   +442 more
  · manifests searched: package.json, starlight_help/package.json
  ```
  Suggested action: *"Add `_typeshed`, `abc`, `ahocorasick` to the dependencies of the package that imports them."*
- **why it's wrong:** `abc` and `_typeshed` are the Python **standard library**. `ahocorasick`/`aioapns` are pip packages declared in `pyproject.toml`, which the detector does not read — it only searched the two `package.json` files. severity **high**, confidence 0.74, and the confidence ladder even *credits* itself "+0.14 (375 runtime imports affected)" for the size of the mistake.
- **why it matters:** this is a 0.16 detector, rated high, on the control repo, and it is 100% wrong. Any polyglot repo gets it. The detector must either read `pyproject.toml` / `requirements*.txt` + a stdlib list, or exclude `language-py` imports entirely.
- **severity:** blocker (for this detector on polyglot repos)

### `docs_code_drift` — 12 findings, 12 false positives, one root cause
- Every flagged link resolves fine; the detector resolves root-relative doc links against the **repo root** instead of the **docs root**:
  - `presenting-visual-changes.md:131 → /images/firefox-rwd-capture-mobile.png (not found)` — it is at `docs/images/firefox-rwd-capture-mobile.png`.
  - `slash-commands.md:15 → /subsystems/widgets.md (not found)` — it is at `docs/subsystems/widgets.md`.
  - `reviewable-prs.md:91 → ../contributing/presenting-visual-changes (not found)` — extensionless Sphinx/MyST link; `docs/contributing/presenting-visual-changes.md` exists.
  - `html-css.md:284 → ../testing/typescript (not found)` — `docs/testing/typescript.md` exists.
- Verified all six targets on disk. **12/12 false positive.**
- **severity:** real problem (two sub-bugs: docs-root resolution, extensionless links)

### `unsafe_retry` and `unbounded_async_fanout` fired ZERO times
- Two of the ten 0.16 detectors produced nothing on a 590k-line repo with a Tornado event system, a push-notification worker, and an `aioapns` async client. Recorded as a negative result about the detectors, per protocol.
- Also zero repo-wide: all three `cross_language_*`.
- **severity:** observation

### `explain` is the best surface in the tool
- Charge / type / severity+confidence / "What this detector looks for" / "Why it matters" / itemised evidence / confidence ladder arithmetic / risk profile with *units* / suggested actions with risk levels / a ready-to-paste `crimes ignore` line. Nothing here was confusing.
- It is also the only renderer that describes blast radius honestly: `blast radius: 1.00 — 50+ transitive importers (cap reached)`. `scan` and `context` render the same score as `blast top-quartile (798 importers)` with no "transitive" and no "cap reached". **Two renderers, two different stories about the same number.**
- **severity:** n/a positive / annoyance for the inconsistency

### `verdict` costs 2m42s to print six lines
- **command:** `crimes verdict --no-init` (cwd = repo)
- **what happened:** 162s wall, 90s user. Output: `Verdict: UNCHANGED / New: 0 / Fixed: 0`. Exit 0. Correct — HEAD is `origin/main` at the pinned SHA — but it runs two full scans to establish that the two refs are the same commit.
- **why it matters:** a no-op comparison should be free. As a pre-commit or CI gate at 3 minutes on a repo this size, nobody keeps it.
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes diff` human output does not say what changed
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~50...HEAD --no-init` → 176s, exit 0, and the entire output is:
  ```
  New crimes: 2 / Fixed crimes: 2 / Unchanged crimes: 3431
  ```
- The JSON has the answer (`new_findings[]`: a new `sync_io_in_hotpath` at `zerver/lib/push_notifications.py:190`, a new `dependency_provenance_gap`; fixed: a `sync_io_in_hotpath` at :205 and a `duplicated_policy` in `web/src/hotkey.ts`). The human renderer prints none of it, and there is no `--verbose`/`--show-new` flag.
- **why it matters:** "what did these 50 commits introduce" is the whole question. The human renderer answers "2" and stops. A PR-review user has to re-run with `--format json` and read it themselves.
- **severity:** real problem

### `crime_NNNNN` ids are not stable across identical runs
- Two `scan --all --format json` runs on the same SHA, 81.3s and 83.1s: identical totals (3759 / 502 / 1761 / 1496), identical coverage, **set-identical findings (0 in one and not the other)** — but **139 of 3759 positions differ**, and **139 findings change their `crime_` id** between runs (3,615 of 3,754 keep theirs).
- First divergence at index 685: run 1 has `large_function zerver/tornado/event_queue.py:1442-1580`, run 2 has `large_file zerver/lib/streams.py`. Two findings with equal sort keys, tie-broken nondeterministically (parallel file processing order).
- **why it matters:** `crimes explain <id>` takes a per-scan id, and the human `scan` output prints id ranges (`id=crime_00144 … crime_01287`) as the user's handle on a finding. Copy an id from a report, re-run, and 4% of the time you explain a different finding. The stable fingerprint exists and is the right answer — but the human renderer never prints one.
- **detection quality itself is deterministic**, which is the important half. Recorded as a ranking/ID bug, not a detection bug.
- **severity:** real problem
### CONTROL ASSESSMENT — the shape is wrong for a repo of this quality
- **3,759 findings / 590,435 lines of code = 6.37 findings per 1,000 lines.** 1,254 of 3,588 scanned files (35%) carry at least one.
  - Python: 1,635 / 418,474 lines = **3.91 / 1k**
  - TS+JS: 1,290 / 154,036 lines = **8.37 / 1k**
- Zulip runs Ruff + mypy strict + Prettier + ESLint + a mandatory two-reviewer process, and has deliberately **not** enabled a function-length rule. crimes' verdict is 3,759 charges.
- **81% of the report (3,038 findings) is in the "a linter already does this" bucket** — `large_function` 1,461, `large_file` 355, `deep_import` 275, `commented_out_code` 293, `boolean_naming_drift` 455, `todo_density` 73, hardcoded paths 59, image-asset charges 65. CLAUDE.md says crimes is *"explicitly not trying to replace ESLint/Biome"*. On the control repo it is 81% exactly that, and Zulip's maintainers already looked at each of those rules and declined them.
- **19% (721 findings) is the differentiated surface** — `sync_io_in_hotpath` 117, `high_fan_in_fan_out` 121, `name_behavior_mismatch` 63, `direct_date` 79, `weak_test_signal` 192, `swallowed_error` 32, `pass_through_abstraction` 19, `duplicated_policy` 15, `contract_drift` 15, `docs_code_drift` 12, plus singletons. **That** number — ~1.2 per 1,000 lines — is what a repo of this quality plausibly deserves, and it is a defensible report.
- **The detector to name:** `large_function` at **1,461 findings (39% of everything)** on the best-maintained repo in the corpus, with **19% of its Python half self-refuting** (evidence quotes a line count at or below the threshold it names). If one calibration result comes out of this repo, it is that one.
- **Runner-up:** `commented_out_code` at 293 (7.8%), which on this repo is inverted — it charges Zulip's long prose comments, its single best maintainability asset.
- **Third:** `boolean_naming_drift` at 455 (12%). Naming convention, entirely stylistic, and Zulip's own conventions differ.
- **Verified-false-positive detectors:** `docs_code_drift` 12/12, `dependency_provenance_gap` 2/2 (both HIGH-severity claims about Python imports made against `package.json`).
- **severity:** real problem (calibration, top-line)

### ZERO-CASE STATEMENT (positive, per protocol)
- Zulip is **polyglot** (py + ts), so the cross-language zero-case is not the protocol's single-language test. Stated explicitly anyway:
  - `cross_language_route_drift` = **0**, `cross_language_type_drift` = **0**, `cross_language_concept_alias_drift` = **0**.
  - `language-py` findings = **1,635** on a repo that does contain `.py` — the Python pack demonstrably ran and produced findings, so `packs_loaded` is trustworthy *for the language packs*.
  - `language-js` = 1,290, `universal` = 834. All three loaded packs produced findings.
  - No cross-language false positive was emitted. That half of the zero-case passes.
- **The repo was not written to.** `git status --porcelain` empty after the full round; no `.crimes/`, no `crimes.config.json`. Every command used `--no-init`.

### Robustness summary
| check | value |
| --- | --- |
| `ulimit -n` | 1048576 |
| `coverage.files_total` | 3,588 |
| `coverage.files_by_language` | `{py: 2017, js: 688}` |
| `coverage.files_universal_only` | 883 (`.mdx` 464, `.md` 396, `.rb` 12, `.sh` 8, `.bash` 2, `.lua` 1) |
| `coverage.packs_loaded` | `["universal","language-js","language-py"]` — all three produced findings |
| `coverage.by_package` | **absent** (only 1 nested manifest; needs 2) |
| stderr | empty on every successful run |
| exit codes | 0 everywhere, except one unreproduced 143 on the first human scan |
| wall clock | scan json 81.3s / 83.1s · scan human 147.8s · hotspots 62s · context 24–27s · verdict 162s · diff 176s |
| max RSS | 378.8 MiB |
| Python parse errors | 0 / 2,016 files |
| determinism | findings set-identical; 139/3,759 positions and ids unstable |

### Files never covered
- **469 `.hbs` Handlebars templates** and **224 `.html`** are not in `files_by_language` and not in `universal_only_by_extension` — they are simply not discovered. Zulip renders most of its UI through `.hbs`; every `{{#if}}` policy check and every UI string in the product is invisible to the scan. `.py` 2,016 + `.js/.ts` 688 + universal 883 = 3,587 ≈ `files_total` 3,588, so the templates are excluded at discovery, silently.
- **severity:** annoyance (defensible scope decision, but nothing in the output says the templates were skipped)
### CORRECTION — the "human scan is 1.8x slower" entry was measurement noise
- Re-measured. Five runs of effectively identical work on the same SHA:
  | run | wall | user |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `--all --format json` | 81.3s | 45.1s |
  | `--all --format json` (2nd) | 83.1s | 46.7s |
  | human (default) | **147.8s** | 47.2s |
  | human (default, 3rd) | 68.7s | 47.2s |
  | human `--all` | 66.1s | 47.5s |
- **User CPU is flat at 45–48s across all five.** Wall clock ranges **66s → 148s, a 2.2x spread**, entirely outside CPU. So the earlier claim (human renderer is slow) is withdrawn; the real observation is that a scan's wall-clock time is **not predictable within a factor of two** on the same repo and machine, and there is no progress output during the wait. The 148s run was the outlier, not the rule.
- **severity:** annoyance (downgraded from "real problem")

### `.hbs` / `.html` / `.erb` templates are silently outside the scan
- Default include globs (`packages/core/src/config.ts:198–204`) cover `{ts,tsx,js,jsx,mjs,cjs,cts,mts}`, `{py,pyi}`, `{rs,go,rb,java,kt,…,lua,sh,bash,zsh}`, `{md,mdx}` and an image set. **No `.hbs`, no `.html`, no `.vue`, no `.svelte`, no `.erb`.**
- Zulip: **469 `.hbs` + 224 `.html` + 56 `.erb` = 749 files**, the entire rendered UI, never seen. `coverage` does not mention them in either `files_by_language` or `universal_only_by_extension` — they are dropped at discovery, so nothing in the report tells the user a third of their frontend was not scanned.
- **why it matters:** the coverage block is the tool's honesty surface. A file that was never a candidate is invisible in it. A Zulip maintainer reading `files_total: 3588` against `git ls-files | wc -l = 8919` gets no explanation.
- **severity:** real problem
### `agent_risk` does not differentiate — the top 20 is one detector
- The protocol's judging sample includes "the top 20 findings by `agent_risk` per repo". On Zulip that sample is:
  **18 of 20 are `large_function` or `large_file`.** The other two are `name_behavior_mismatch`. All 20 are in `web/src/`; **not one is Python**, despite Python being 71% of the code.
- Distribution across all 3,759: min 0.08, p25 0.30, median 0.37, p75 0.49, p90 0.58, max 0.89. The whole differentiated surface — `swallowed_error`, `duplicated_policy`, `contract_drift`, `sync_io_in_hotpath` — sits in the 0.25–0.75 band, *below* a 682-line function.
- **why it matters:** CLAUDE.md: *"`agent_risk` is the differentiator and must not be collapsed into severity."* On this repo it has collapsed into **file size × churn**, with `blast_radius` contributing nothing (47% of findings pinned at 1.0). The one score that is supposed to make crimes different from a linter is currently ranking the linter findings first.
- **severity:** real problem (top-line)

---

## Closing note

Nothing crashed. No stack traces, no hangs, no non-zero exits except one
unreproduced SIGTERM. Memory is comfortable (379 MiB peak on 590k lines).
The Python parser is clean — 2,016 files, zero syntax errors, `match` and
walrus and PEP 604 all handled. `explain` is genuinely good.

The problems are calibration and coverage, not stability:

1. the 0.15 cross-language pack is blind to Django URLconf and to
   options-bag HTTP wrappers, which is most of the real world;
2. one size-based detector is 39% of the report and self-refutes 19% of
   the time;
3. the report's own ranking signal puts that detector at the top.

---

## Friction log — airflow

Repo: `~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/airflow` @ `acb4dde004fa39b0876923e0d39883bd3c2a2a60`
Binary: `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes` (0.17.0)
`ulimit -n` = 1048576
Size: 421M total (216M .git), 13,659 tracked files, 7,677 `.py`, 594 `.tsx`, 440 `.ts`, 40,206 commits.

### `/usr/bin/time -l` reliably SIGTERMs the long scan; bare invocation does not
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `/usr/bin/time -l ./bin/crimes scan .../airflow --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** two attempts under `/usr/bin/time -l` ended `EXIT=143` (SIGTERM) at 74.6s and 156.7s with a 0-byte JSON and `time: command terminated abnormally`. The identical command without `/usr/bin/time` completed cleanly (exit 0, 25.4 MB JSON) three times running. A short scan (`task-sdk` subtree, 7.4s) under `/usr/bin/time -l` was fine.
- **why it matters:** most likely an environment/sandbox artefact, not a crimes bug — but the protocol prescribes `/usr/bin/time -p` for the canonical timing step, and on this host that measurement method destroys the artefact it is measuring. Anyone following the protocol literally on a repo this size gets a 0-byte JSON and no error from crimes.
- **severity:** annoyance (protocol), not a crimes defect
- **workaround used:** wall clock via `date`, max RSS via a 0.5s `ps -o rss= -p <pid>` poller.

### Scan wall-clock varies ~2.3x run to run on the same input
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes scan <airflow> --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** 134s, 173s across two clean runs (and a killed run had reached 157s). Max RSS 577 MB (PID-targeted poll), `/usr/bin/time` reported 434–444 MB on the truncated runs.
- **why it matters:** the machine had sibling dogfooding agents on it, so this is probably contention rather than crimes. Worth recording because "airflow takes ~2.5 min" is not a stable number.
- **severity:** annoyance

### Machine-generated OpenAPI client code occupies 6 of the top 20 findings by agent_risk
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes scan <airflow> --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `airflow-core/src/airflow/ui/openapi-gen/**` produced 125 findings, all `tier: "domain"`, including the #3 ranked finding overall (`large_file` on `requests/services.gen.ts`, agent_risk 0.79) plus `queries.ts`, `prefetch.ts`, `suspense.ts`, `schemas.gen.ts`, `ensureQueryData.ts`. `services.gen.ts` and `schemas.gen.ts` match `scope-class.ts`'s `GENERATED_RE` (`\.(generated|gen)\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$`) and so are `isNeverReportable`; the sibling files under `openapi-gen/` do not match any rule at all (the directory is `openapi-gen`, not `generated`).
- **why it matters:** two separate problems. (1) `large_file` / `near_duplicate_block` / `commented_out_code` do not consult `scope-class.ts` at all, so even a path the classifier already calls `generated` is reported at full weight. (2) `openapi-gen/` is a common generator output convention that `GENERATED_RE` does not cover. Net effect: the first screen a maintainer sees is dominated by a directory nobody edits by hand.
- **severity:** real problem

### `scope_class` is not exposed on any finding; `tier` is domain/nonDomain and does not track it
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `jq` over the JSON contract
- **what happened:** no finding carries a `scope_class` field. The only adjacent field is `tier`, which takes `domain` / `nonDomain`. All 230 findings inside `migrations/` are `tier: "domain"`; so are all 26 on `.pyi` stubs, all 125 under `openapi-gen/`, and the single `_vendor/` finding. Of 6,770 findings under a `tests/` path only 222 are `nonDomain`.
- **why it matters:** the protocol asks whether scope classification is working. From the JSON contract alone that is unanswerable — the classifier's output is invisible to a consumer. A downstream agent cannot filter "don't show me migrations" without re-deriving path rules the tool already has.
- **severity:** real problem (schema gap)

### `mixed_utc_local_methods` fires ZERO times on the most timezone-obsessed Python codebase in existence
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes scan <airflow> --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** 0 findings. Airflow contains 775 `utcnow()` call sites and 354 `datetime.now(` call sites; 21 files contain both, 9 of them non-test production modules. The clean positive case is `providers/cncf/kubernetes/.../executors/kubernetes_executor.py`: naive `datetime.now()` at lines 464 and 630, `timezone.utcnow()` at line 1098, same module. `direct_date` DID fire on that file at `[464, 630]`, so the Python parser's `dateCalls` saw the naive `now()` reads — what it did not do is classify `timezone.utcnow()` as `kind: "utcnow"`.
- **why it matters:** root cause is the wrapper idiom. Every large Python codebase wraps clock access (`timezone.utcnow()`, `utils.now()`, `clock.utcnow()`); the detector's `kind === "utcnow"` only recognises the bare `datetime.utcnow()` form, which is deprecated in 3.12 and therefore increasingly rare in exactly the modern code the pack targets. The detector is not merely quiet — it is structurally unable to fire on idiomatic Python.
- **severity:** real problem (detector is a candidate for retune-or-sunset)

### 41% of all airflow findings are the Apache license header
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes scan <airflow> --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `commented_out_code` fired 8,019 times. 7,320 of those (41.1% of the repo's entire 17,801-finding output) have line range exactly `(1,16)` or `(1,17)` — the ASF Apache-2.0 header every airflow source file carries. Evidence text: `"16 consecutive comment lines (lines 1–16)"`, `">=3 lines contain code-shaped tokens (def/fn/function/class/let/var/const/etc.)"`. The header contains no `def`/`class`/`let`/`var`/`const`; the likeliest triggers are the `http://…` URL (a `//`) and the quoted `"License"` / `"AS IS"` string literals.
- **why it matters:** every Apache-licensed repo — a very large fraction of the open-source Python world — will see this. It is 8,000 findings that are literally identical, on every file, and it swamps the JSON contract for any downstream consumer. mlflow, which does not carry per-file license headers, produced only 151 `commented_out_code` findings, so the asymmetry is entirely explained by the boilerplate.
- **severity:** blocker for this repo's output being usable

### `hotspots` on airflow returns generated OpenAPI client code for 6 of the top 7
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes hotspots <airflow> --no-init` (164s)
- **what happened:** ranks 1,2,3,5,6,7,8 are all `airflow-core/src/airflow/ui/openapi-gen/**`; rank 13 is `airflow-ctl/src/airflowctl/api/datamodels/generated.py`. Generated clients are regenerated on every API change, so they have both maximum churn and maximum finding count — exactly the two inputs `hotspots` multiplies.
- **why it matters:** `hotspots` is the "where is the risk" command. On the largest repo in the corpus it points almost entirely at files no human edits. `scope-class.ts` already knows two of these paths are `generated`; `hotspots` does not ask it.
- **severity:** blocker

### Every top hotspot scores `risk 1.00 (100%)` — the ranking carries no information
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes hotspots <airflow> --no-init`
- **what happened:** ranks 1 through 14 all read `risk 1.00 (100%)`. mlflow's top 3 also read 1.00, then it degrades sensibly (0.85, 0.84, 0.82…).
- **why it matters:** on a big repo the score saturates and the displayed order is decided by something the output never shows. A user cannot tell rank 1 from rank 14.
- **severity:** real problem

### `large_function` reports a threshold the finding does not meet
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes scan <airflow> --no-init` (default human view)
- **what happened:** `_update_dag_run_state_for_paused_dags` is charged "God Function", summary "is 30 lines and nests 4 levels deep", first evidence line `30 lines (threshold 50 for shape "domain")`. Same for `_executor_to_workloads` (40 lines / threshold 50) and `_execute` (49 lines / threshold 50). The real trigger is nesting depth, which appears at evidence positions 2 and 5.
- **why it matters:** the headline evidence line reads as self-refuting — it states a threshold and then a number below it. A reader who checks the receipt concludes the tool is broken. This is exactly the "summary did not explain itself" case.
- **severity:** real problem

### The default human view prints 33 findings for one file, 24 of them the same charge
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes scan <airflow> --no-init` (no `--all`)
- **what happened:** the top-ranked file, `scheduler_job_runner.py`, lists all 33 of its findings, of which 24 are "God Function". 164 lines of output for 5 files.
- **why it matters:** `--all` is documented as the opt-in for exhaustiveness, but the default already is exhaustive *within* a file. The signal ("this file has 24 oversized functions") is one line; it is delivered as 24.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `context` uses a different `crime_NNNNN` numbering space than `scan` / `explain`
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes context airflow-core/src/airflow/jobs/scheduler_job_runner.py --no-init`, then `crimes explain crime_00002 --no-init`
- **what happened:** `context` labelled the God File on `scheduler_job_runner.py` as `id=crime_00001` and `_executable_task_instances_to_queued` as `id=crime_00002`. `crimes explain crime_00002` returned `large_file` on `api_fastapi/common/parameters.py` — the repo-wide numbering. Both are reproducible; they are simply different ID spaces, and nothing in either output says so.
- **why it matters:** the documented agent workflow is context-before-edit then explain-the-id. Following it hands you a different finding, silently. Only 103 of 17,801 findings carry a `discriminator`, so `id` is the only handle available for the rest.
- **severity:** blocker for the agent workflow

### `context` finds the right test file but does not rank it, and `test gap` contradicts it
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes context airflow-core/src/airflow/models/dagrun.py --no-init`
- **what happened:** "Likely tests" lists 30 paths in alphabetical order. The true 1:1 pair, `tests/unit/models/test_dagrun.py` (5,065 lines), is 20th, indistinguishable from `tests/unit/cluster_policies/__init__.py` and `tests/unit/listeners/dag_listener.py`, which are not tests at all. For `scheduler_job_runner.py` the list is 5 entries, 4 of them wrong, and the "Clues" block simultaneously reports `test gap: bottom-quartile` for a file with a 13,678-line dedicated test.
- **why it matters:** "which test covers this file" is the single highest-value thing `context` can tell an agent, and an unranked 30-item list is not an answer. The internal disagreement (likely-tests found it; test_gap says there is none) means two different pairing heuristics disagree and neither is surfaced.
- **severity:** real problem

### `sync_io_in_hotpath` and `boolean_naming_drift` aggregate across functions but wear a single-symbol label
- **repo:** airflow
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_00062 --no-init`
- **what happened:** the finding carries `symbol: _enqueue_executor_callbacks` and `lines: 1265–1934`, but its evidence lists calls in three different functions (`_enqueue_executor_callbacks`, `process_executor_events`, `_run_scheduler_loop`). `boolean_naming_drift` on `triggerer_job_runner.py` spans lines 438–1283 across six bindings in different classes. Two of the three matched calls are SQLAlchemy `session.get()`, classified into family `network`.
- **why it matters:** the `lines` range is not a region a reader can open — it is the bounding box of scattered hits. Anything consuming `lines` to build a code excerpt (including the judging protocol's ±30-line card) gets the wrong window.
- **severity:** real problem

### `explain` and `context` re-scan the whole repo every invocation
- **repo:** airflow / mlflow
- **command:** `crimes explain <id> --no-init`, `crimes context <file> --no-init`
- **what happened:** airflow `explain` 164s / 168s / 180s per call; airflow `context` 17–19s (it scopes to the `airflow-core` package); mlflow `context` 112s and 97s (no package split at the repo root, so it scans everything).
- **why it matters:** the crimes skill tells agents to run `context` before every edit. Two minutes per pre-edit check is not a workflow anyone keeps. There is a `.crimes/` cache location in the design and it is not being used here.
- **severity:** real problem

### Three commands report three different file counts for the same repo
- **repo:** airflow
- **what happened:** `scan` human header says "across 9089 files"; `coverage.files_total` in the JSON says 9149; `hotspots` says "3896 files"/"10971 files" (airflow: 10971). All from the same commit, same flags.
- **why it matters:** small, but "how much did you actually look at" is the one number a user checks to decide whether to trust the rest, and it changes depending on which command they ask.
- **severity:** annoyance

### Output is deterministic — recording the positive
- **repo:** synthetic probe
- **what happened:** two consecutive `scan --all --format json` runs produced byte-identical id / type / file / lines / evidence tuples for all 16 findings. `explain crime_00002` returned the same finding on two separate invocations.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)

## Friction log — mlflow

Repo: `~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/mlflow` @ `0489fb31d8a144ec3c80d15eb44dbe24b790465e`
Binary: `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes` (0.17.0)

### mlflow scans 2.3x slower than airflow despite being 30% smaller
- **repo:** mlflow
- **command:** `crimes scan <mlflow> --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** 402s wall, 594 MB peak RSS, exit 0, empty stderr, 9.6 MB JSON. Airflow — 13,659 tracked files / 7,677 `.py` / 40,206 commits — took 173s at 577 MB. mlflow is 9,241 files / 2,627 `.py` / 12,842 commits. The one dimension where mlflow is bigger is TS/JS: 3,426 `.ts`/`.tsx` vs airflow's 1,034, and `coverage.files_by_language` confirms it (`js: 3513` vs `js: 1089`).
- **why it matters:** cost tracks the JS pack, not repo size or Python volume. A Python-dominant repo pays 6.7 minutes because it has a React frontend. `hotspots` is 418s, `context` on one file is 97–112s, and `verdict` (two scans) **did not finish inside 10 minutes**.
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes verdict` exits 2 on a `master`-default repo
- **repo:** mlflow
- **command:** `crimes verdict --no-init` (cwd = repo root)
- **what happened:** `crimes: could not pick a default base ref (tried origin/main, main). Pass --base <ref> explicitly, e.g. --base main.` exit code 2. mlflow's default branch is `master`, and `origin/master` exists.
- **why it matters:** the message is clear and actionable, which is good, but `master` is a two-word addition to the fallback list and it is still the default branch on a large share of long-lived repos. Every mlflow contributor hits this on first run.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `crimes verdict --base master` did not complete in 10 minutes
- **repo:** mlflow
- **command:** `crimes verdict --base master --no-init`
- **what happened:** killed at the 10-minute mark with no output. It needs a scan of base and of head; a single scan is 402s, so ~13.5 minutes is the floor. No progress output of any kind while it runs.
- **why it matters:** `verdict` is the CI-gate command. A silent 13-minute run with no progress indicator is indistinguishable from a hang, and it is above most PR-check budgets.
- **severity:** real problem

### Generated protobuf stubs are scanned and reported as production code
- **repo:** mlflow
- **command:** `crimes scan <mlflow> --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `mlflow/protos/` produced 28 findings — 17 `large_file`, 9 `boolean_naming_drift` (`language-py`), 2 `large_function` — all `tier: "domain"`. Includes `service_pb2.py` (5,696 lines) and `service_pb2.pyi` (2,833 lines). `scope-class.ts`'s `GENERATED_RE` does not match `_pb2.py` / `_pb2_grpc.py` / `_pb2.pyi`, and the `large_file` detector does not call `looksGeneratedSource` either. A synthetic 60,002-line file whose first line is `# @generated by protoc. DO NOT EDIT.` also drew a `large_file` finding.
- **why it matters:** `_pb2.py` is the single most common generated-Python convention there is. Both layers of the classifier — the path rule and the banner sniff — exist and neither is consulted.
- **severity:** real problem

### Alembic migrations under `db_migrations/` are classified as production
- **repo:** mlflow
- **command:** `crimes scan <mlflow> --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `mlflow/store/db_migrations/versions/` holds 63 alembic revisions; the tree produced 43 findings, all `tier: "domain"`. `MIGRATION_RE` requires a path segment that is exactly `migrations` / `migration` (or `alembic/versions`, `db/migrate`, `prisma/migrations`); `db_migrations` matches none of them. In airflow the directory *is* called `migrations/versions/` and matches — yet those 230 findings are also `tier: "domain"`, so matching the rule made no observable difference to the output.
- **severity:** real problem

### Jupyter notebooks are silently outside coverage

- **why it matters:** defensible as a scope decision, but on a data/ML repo notebooks are where a lot of the real Python lives. `coverage` is the mechanism for telling the user what was not looked at, and it does not mention them.
- **severity:** annoyance

Still unranked and still padded with `tests/conftest.py` and `dev/benchmarks/tracing/test_trace_perf.py`, but the right answer is in there.

### `mixed_utc_local_methods` fires zero times here too
- **repo:** mlflow
- **what happened:** 0 `language-py` findings (1 `language-js`). Same as airflow. Across both repos — 10,304 real Python files — the detector produced **zero** Python findings.

### Encoding robustness: latin-1 survives, UTF-16 and NUL bytes are silently dropped
- **repo:** synthetic probe (`~/crimes-dogfood/scratch/pyprobe-repo`), built because neither corpus repo contains a non-UTF8 `.py`
- **command:** `crimes scan scratch/pyprobe-repo --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** four files with byte-identical Python bodies. UTF-8 control → 3 findings. Same bytes as latin-1 (high bytes in the docstring) → the same 3 findings, so replacement decoding works. Same bytes as UTF-16 → **0 findings**. Same bytes with a NUL in the docstring → **0 findings**. All four are counted in `coverage.files_by_language.py`, stderr is empty, exit code 0.
- **why it matters:** this is precisely the failure the protocol warns about — a degraded scan is byte-identical to a clean one. `coverage` claims the file was scanned. There is no `files_failed` counter and no warning channel.
- **severity:** real problem (low real-world frequency, but the silence is the defect)

### `sync_io_in_hotpath` attributes 15 calls spanning 8,173 lines to a single function
- **repo:** mlflow
- **command:** `crimes scan <mlflow> --no-init`
- **what happened:** in `mlflow/store/tracking/sqlalchemy_store.py` (10,093 lines) the finding is labelled `Blocking the Hot Path · _hard_delete_experiment()` and lists 15 calls at lines 900 … 9073. The human renderer prints ten of them inline plus "…+5 more" on one line. Twelve of the fifteen are SQLAlchemy `session.delete()` / `session.get()`, classified as blocking I/O.
- **why it matters:** the symbol label is wrong for 14 of the 15 sites, and the `lines` range covers 81% of the file. Any consumer that uses `lines` to fetch surrounding code — including the blind judging card — gets a meaningless window.
- **severity:** real problem

### Modern Python syntax: no parse failures found, on real code or synthetic
- **repo:** airflow + mlflow + synthetic probe
- **what happened:** verified by presence of findings *from inside* files using each feature, never by absence of an error.
  - `match` — airflow 10/16 files, mlflow 59/74 produced `language-py` findings.
  - walrus `:=` — airflow 215/238, mlflow 297/379.
  - PEP 604 `X | None` — airflow 1441/1646, mlflow 410/612.
  - positional-only `/` — airflow 5/6, mlflow 1/1.
  - async generators — airflow 98/106, mlflow 37/57.
  - decorators with complex expressions — airflow 1205/1480, mlflow 288/585.
  - nested f-strings — airflow 14/16, mlflow 6/11.
  - All rates are at or above the repo-wide base rate (airflow 4104/7677 = 53%, mlflow 955/2627 = 36%).
  - PEP 695 generics (`class Box[T]`, `def get[U]`, `def f[T, *Ts, **P]`, `type X = ...`) and `except*` groups appear in **neither** corpus repo, so they were tested synthetically: both parsed, with findings produced from inside a PEP-695-generic function body and from statements after an `except*` block.
- **severity:** n/a (positive — the tree-sitter Python parser is the strongest part of the 0.14 pack)
