# ortomate-studio

## Friction log — ortomate-studio

**Repo:** `~/dev/ortomate-studio`
**SHA:** `8ad20cea3b6e0c1889f62b8bc7578e64300c4139` (branch `andrew-dev`, clean tree)
**Build under test:** crimes 0.17.0
**Case:** GRACEFUL DEGRADATION — 429 `.swift` files (91,778 LOC) vs
95 `.js` + 69 `.ts` + 4 `.tsx` (16,012 LOC). Swift is unsupported.
Swift is **72% of tracked source files** and **85% of source LOC**.

Entries appended as they happen.

---
### `crimes scan` never completes on this repo — killed twice, 358s and 731s
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/ortomate-studio --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** Two independent runs. Run 1: SIGTERM at **358s real**,
  output file **0 bytes**. Run 2 (`/usr/bin/time -l`): SIGTERM at **731s real**,
  256s user, peak RSS **493 MB**, output file **0 bytes**. Non-deterministic
  wall clock, deterministic outcome: no JSON, ever. Exit 143 both times.
  Not an OOM — memory plateaus around 230–490 MB.
- **why it matters:** For a user this is indistinguishable from a hang. There is
  no progress output, no spinner, no per-file logging, and nothing on stderr.
  You stare at a blank terminal for 12 minutes and then have nothing. The JSON
  contract is "the product" per CLAUDE.md, and on this repo the product does not
  emit.
- **severity:** blocker

### Root cause: minified-bundle exclusion is name-based only (`**/*.min.js`)
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** per-subtree bisect — `crimes scan <subdir> --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** Bisected by subtree. `Sources/` (Swift, 6.1 MB) = **3s**.
  `server/` = **4s**. `ios/` = **1s**. `chrome-extension/` = **killed, never
  finishes**. That subtree holds `chrome-extension/reference/claude-1.0.77/assets/`
  — **92 of the repo's 95 tracked `.js` files**, 12 MB, **812 total lines**, with
  a single line of **1,621,186 characters**. These are Vite-hashed vendor bundles
  (`sidepanel-Bb4xbmwz.js`, `index-BYVE4JzA.js`, `mermaid.core-BAp6MO0B.js`).
  `DEFAULT_CONFIG.exclude` in `packages/core/src/config.ts` only has
  `**/*.min.js` and `**/*.generated.*` — pure filename patterns. Modern bundlers
  do not emit `.min.js`; they emit `name-HASH.js`. Nothing in the default path
  looks at file size or line length before handing the file to the parser.
- **why it matters:** Any repo that vendors a built frontend — extremely common —
  will hit this. The fix is cheap (bytes-per-line or max-file-size heuristic) but
  the failure mode is total.
- **severity:** blocker

### No CLI escape hatch for exclusions
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** `crimes scan --help`
- **what happened:** `scan` has no `--exclude`, no `--max-file-size`, no
  `--timeout`. The only way to exclude the vendor bundles is to write
  `crimes.config.json` into the repo — which a read-only evaluator (or anyone
  auditing a repo they don't own) cannot do, and which `--no-init` explicitly
  exists to avoid.
- **why it matters:** When the tool chokes on a directory there is no way to get
  past it from the command line. Broken and unworkaroundable is worse than broken.
- **severity:** real problem

### Timing caveat for this round
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **what happened:** `ps` showed up to **8 concurrent `crimes scan` processes**
  from other agents in this dogfooding round on the same machine. All wall-clock
  numbers here are contended and should be treated as upper bounds. CPU% per
  process sat at 12–90%.
- **severity:** annoyance (measurement hygiene, not a tool defect)

### 251 seconds to analyse ONE 1.9 MB minified file — and it emits 23 findings
- **repo:** ortomate-studio (isolated repro, file copied to scratch)
- **command:** `crimes scan <dir-with-one-file> --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** Copied a single vendor bundle
  (`chrome-extension/reference/claude-1.0.77/assets/sidepanel-Bb4xbmwz.js`,
  1.9 MB, 6 lines, longest line 1.6 M chars) into an empty git repo and scanned
  it. **rc=0 in 251 seconds.** So the full-repo scan is not hung — it is
  ~4 minutes per big bundle × 92 bundles. It would need hours.
  Worse, it did not skip the file: it emitted **23 findings on minified vendor
  code** — 8 `swallowed_error`, 5 `name_behavior_mismatch`,
  5 `return_shape_roulette`, 1 `direct_date` ("94 direct uses of
  `Date.now()`/`new Date()`"), 1 `dst_naive_arithmetic`, 1 `negative_flag_maze`,
  1 `boolean_naming_drift`, 1 `pass_through_abstraction`. One rated `high`.
- **why it matters:** Two defects in one. (1) There is no size/line-length guard
  before parsing, so cost is unbounded. (2) `util/scope-class.ts` is supposed to
  be "one answer to generated / vendored / … / production" and it classified a
  Vite output bundle as production domain code. Every one of those 23 findings is
  noise a maintainer cannot act on — you do not refactor `Date.now()` out of a
  vendored build artifact.
- **severity:** blocker

### CORRECTION — the scan does complete, in 675 s. It is 135× slower than it should be.
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/ortomate-studio --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** A fourth attempt, run detached, **finished: `real 675`,
  `EXIT=0`, 1.3 MB of JSON, empty stderr, 851 findings.** So the earlier
  SIGTERMs at 358 s and 731 s were the harness reaping a long-running job, not
  the tool. Retracting "never completes"; the accurate statement is
  **11 minutes 15 seconds**. The same repo with the 92 vendored bundles removed
  scans in **5 seconds**. Same file count otherwise, same detectors. That is a
  **135× penalty** paid for 12 MB of files nobody wants analysed.
- **why it matters:** Still disqualifying in practice — nothing in `crimes init
  --agents`' pre-edit-hook story survives an 11-minute scan, and the CLI prints
  no progress for the entire time — but "slow" is a different bug from "hangs"
  and the report must say the right one.
- **severity:** real problem (downgraded from blocker)

### 52% of all findings — and 15 of the top 20 by `agent_risk` — are in vendored bundles
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** `crimes scan … --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** Of 851 findings, **444 (52.2%)** are inside
  `chrome-extension/reference/claude-1.0.77/` — a checked-in copy of somebody
  else's built Chrome extension, across 57 distinct minified files. 17 of them
  are rated `high`. Breakdown: 145 `name_behavior_mismatch`,
  120 `return_shape_roulette`, 78 `swallowed_error`, 17 `high_fan_in_fan_out`,
  15 `direct_date`, 14 `boolean_naming_drift`, 12 `unsafe_retry`,
  9 `duplicated_policy`. Ranked by `agent_risk`, **positions 6 through 20 are all
  vendored bundles** — `mermaid.core-*.js`, `cytoscape.esm-*.js`,
  `ganttDiagram-*.js`. Only positions 1–5 are real code.
- **why it matters:** Two things break at once. (1) The headline number is
  double what it should be — 851 vs 407 — so severity counts are meaningless.
  (2) This round's own sampling rule is "top 20 findings by `agent_risk` per
  repo". Following it here sends **15 minified-bundle cards to the blind judge**,
  who will correctly call them noise, and the resulting act rate will indict the
  wrong detectors. `name_behavior_mismatch` and `return_shape_roulette` will look
  catastrophic when the actual defect is scope classification.
- **severity:** blocker

### `crimes diff` inherits the pathology from history and cannot finish
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~5...HEAD --no-init`
- **what happened:** Killed at **>9 minutes**, no output. `diff` re-scans the
  base ref, and every reachable ref in this repo contains the vendored bundles,
  so there is no range for which `diff` is usable. There is no way to scope it.
- **why it matters:** `diff main...HEAD` is the CI story. On this repo the CI
  story does not run.
- **severity:** real problem

### Swift findings are structurally incapable of ranking — two of six scores are hard zero
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** `crimes scan … --all --format json --no-init`, score analysis
- **what happened:** Across **all 175** `.swift` findings:
  `blast_radius = 0` (175/175), `blast_radius_importers = 0` (175/175),
  `test_gap = 0` (175/175). Not distributed — **constant**. The 217 `.ts`
  findings have `blast_radius_importers` spread across 0–55 and `test_gap = 0.5`.
  Consequence: Swift `agent_risk` maxes at **0.56** (mean 0.25) while TS reaches
  **0.79** (mean 0.38). Ranking is by aggregate risk, so 429 files — 85% of the
  repo, 92% of its source lines — are pushed below the TS files by arithmetic,
  independent of their actual risk.
- **why it matters:** These are not measurements, they are absences. There is no
  Swift import graph and no Swift test mapping, so the scorer substitutes 0. The
  schema has no way to say "unknown", so a missing signal is indistinguishable
  from a measured-zero signal — and the ranking treats it as good news.
- **severity:** real problem

### A missing test signal is rendered to the user as a *good* test signal
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** `crimes context Sources/OrtomateStudio/Projects/ProjectsStore.swift --no-init`
- **what happened:** `ProjectsStore.swift` is **4,949 lines**, **97 commits in
  90 days**, and has no test file. `context` prints
  `· test gap: bottom-quartile` — the exact same phrase a genuinely
  well-covered TypeScript file gets, because `test_gap = 0` renders as
  "bottom-quartile" (i.e. *least* gap). It also prints
  `Likely tests: (no test file matched the target basename (looked for siblings,
  __tests__/ and tests/ directories, and the .test / .spec / _test / test_
  naming conventions)…)` — every one of those is a JS/TS convention. This repo
  has **`Tests/OrtomateStudioTests/*Tests.swift`**, 20+ files, the standard SwiftPM
  layout, and none of it is recognised.
- **why it matters:** This is the sharpest form of the honesty problem. The tool
  does not merely omit Swift — on its most-changed Swift file it reports the
  *opposite* of the truth about test coverage, in the command specifically
  designed to brief an agent before it edits that file.
- **severity:** real problem

### `by_package` covers only the JS packages of a Swift-first repo
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** `crimes scan … --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `coverage.by_package` lists exactly two entries —
  `chrome-extension` (109 files, js 107) and `server` (62 files, js 60). The repo
  has three SwiftPM packages: root `Package.swift`,
  `OrtomateRemoteKit/Package.swift`, and `ios/`. None appear. `by-package.ts`
  maps only `language-js`→js and `language-py`→py, so package discovery is
  package-manifest-driven for npm only.
- **why it matters:** A user reading `by_package` on this repo sees a two-package
  Node project. It is a 429-file Swift application with a small Node sidecar.
- **severity:** real problem

### THE HEADLINE — the default report on a 429-file Swift app is five minified vendor bundles and no mention of Swift
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/ortomate-studio --no-init` (574 s)
- **what happened:** Header: `851 findings across 255 files · 55 high 391 medium
  405 low`. "Top files by risk" — **all five** are
  `chrome-extension/reference/claude-1.0.77/assets/*.js`: `mcpPermissions-…`,
  `useStorageState-…`, `sidepanel-…`, `mermaid.core-…`, `wardley-…`. The findings
  read as you would expect from minified code:
  `Return Shape Roulette · I  115 object-literal return shapes`,
  `Unprefixed Boolean 'So' @L1`,
  `Logic in the Alibi  comment says: ",o=/^\/\//;function l(e,t){if(!e.scheme…"`,
  `Abstraction Laundering · qn()`. Closing call to action:
  `→ Start with crimes context chrome-extension/reference/claude-1.0.77/assets/mcpPermissions-CJK8I7C7.js`.
  The string "swift" appears **exactly once** in the 174-line report, and only
  because I passed `--explain-coverage`. Without that flag it appears **zero**
  times.
- **why it matters:** This is the product-integrity question stated plainly. A
  maintainer of this repo runs `npx crimes scan` — the canonical first-run
  command — waits ten minutes, and is told their biggest risk is a third-party
  file they have never opened, in a directory literally named `reference/`.
  Meanwhile 91,778 lines of Swift, 92% of the source they actually maintain,
  are never named.
- **severity:** blocker

### VERDICT on the honesty question — the JSON tells the truth, the default UI does not
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **command:** `crimes scan … --format json` vs `crimes scan …` vs `… --explain-coverage`
- **what happened:** Nothing is silently dropped. `coverage.files_total` = **619**
  and includes every Swift file; `coverage.files_universal_only` = **450** with
  `universal_only_by_extension` = `{".swift": 429, ".md": 19, ".sh": 2}`; and
  `--explain-coverage` prints an unusually good paragraph:
  *"These files were not claimed by any loaded language pack. They received
  universal-pack checks only (file size, raster assets, duplicate filenames,
  hardcoded localhost/local paths, docs link checking)."* That is exactly the
  right sentence. **It is behind an off-by-default flag, printed last, after the
  report it should be qualifying.** The default human path emits no coverage
  signal of any kind.
  What "universal-pack only" costs in practice: the 175 `.swift` findings come
  from just **4** detector types (`large_file` 84, `commented_out_code` 79,
  `hardcoded_localhost` 8, `hardcoded_local_path` 4). The other ~45 detectors —
  every correctness, duplication, authority and agent-risk check — ran on the
  16,012 lines of TS/JS and on none of the 91,778 lines of Swift.
- **why it matters:** The honest answer to "is the tool truthful here" is
  **yes in the contract, no in the interface**. Promoting one line
  (`429 of 619 files: universal checks only (.swift)`) into the default header
  would close the gap entirely. It is the cheapest high-value fix in this log.
- **severity:** real problem

### Methodology note — which artifacts came from where
- **repo:** ortomate-studio
- **what happened:** `scan --all --format json` (`ortomate-studio.json`, 675 s)
  and `scan` human (`ortomate-studio.human.txt`, 574 s) were run against the
  **real repo at `8ad20cea`, read-only, untouched** — verified by
  `ls -A` + `git status --porcelain` diff before and after. Those are the
  authoritative numbers.
  `hotspots`, `verdict`, `context`, `explain` and the `.novendor.*` artifacts
  were run against a **local clone in scratch** (`git clone --local`, same SHA)
  with one extra commit removing `chrome-extension/reference/`, because each of
  those commands re-scans and 11 minutes × 5 was not affordable. The clone shares
  the full git history, so churn/recency figures carry over; **finding counts in
  the `.novendor.*` artifacts are for the 527-file subset, not the repo.**
  `ortomate-studio.diff.txt` is **0 bytes** — `diff` never completed (see above).
- **severity:** n/a — measurement provenance

# mysidekick-cc

## Friction log — mysidekick-cc

**Repo:** `~/dev/mysidekick-cc`
**SHA:** `7e0372210adcebc0ca7601c977bb3b354136bd40` (branch `main`;
`vault/knowledge.md` modified pre-existing, not touched by this round)
**Build under test:** crimes 0.17.0
**Case:** ZERO CONTROL — 176 `.ts` + 1 `.js` (21,943 LOC), zero `.py`.
Single-language TypeScript.

Entries appended as they happen.

---
### `.gitignore` is not respected — 19% of all findings are from gitignored build artifacts
- **repo:** mysidekick-cc
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/mysidekick-cc --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `api/_server.mjs` (34 findings) and `public/call.bundle.js`
  (12 findings) are **esbuild output**, both **explicitly listed in
  `.gitignore`**, both untracked. That is **46 of 241 findings = 19.1%**.
  `_server.mjs` is a 2,668-line bundle of `src/*.ts`, so every finding in it is a
  *duplicate* of a finding already reported against the real source. Same for the
  33 gitignored `vault/daily/*.md` files: `coverage.files_universal_only` is 58
  and the repo only tracks 25 `.md` — the other 33 are gitignored journal entries.
  Root cause is not subtle: `packages/core/src/discovery/files.ts` calls
  `fast-glob` with `ignore: options.exclude` and **nothing else**. There is no
  `.gitignore` parsing anywhere in discovery. `dot: false` is why `.build/`-style
  dot-dirs get skipped — that is incidental, not gitignore support.
- **why it matters:** `CLAUDE.md` states the locked stack decision as
  "File walking: fast-glob + `ignore` (respect `.gitignore`)". It is not
  implemented. The consequence is not cosmetic: the human report's **single
  closing call-to-action** is `→ Start with crimes context api/_server.mjs — it
  concentrates the most risk in this scan.` The tool's one piece of advice points
  the user at a generated file they must not edit.
- **severity:** blocker

### The zero case passes cleanly, but `packs_loaded` lies
- **repo:** mysidekick-cc
- **command:** `crimes scan … --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** Zero `.py` files in the repo. Confirmed positively:
  `cross_language_route_drift`, `cross_language_type_drift`,
  `cross_language_concept_alias_drift` → **0 findings each**. Python pack →
  **0 findings**. `pack` field across all 241 findings is only `language-js` (223)
  and `universal` (18). No `language-py`, no `cross`. That is the right answer.
  **But** `coverage.packs_loaded` is `["universal", "language-js", "language-py"]`.
  `packages/core/src/discovery/coverage.ts:58` builds it as
  `["universal", ...router.registeredPacks()]` — registration happens at module
  load, so `language-py` is listed on every scan of every repo on earth.
- **why it matters:** The protocol's own robustness check is "`packs_loaded` —
  and whether the pack actually produced findings", precisely because the field
  cannot answer that. It is a constant. The honest signal is right next to it:
  `files_by_language` is `{"js": 181}` with **no `py` key at all**. So the data
  exists; the field named `packs_loaded` is just the wrong one to read, and
  nothing labels it as such.
- **severity:** real problem

### The default human report is a wall, not a summary
- **repo:** mysidekick-cc
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/mysidekick-cc --no-init` (no `--all`)
- **what happened:** 103 lines. `--top` caps **files** at 5 but does not cap
  findings **within** a file, so the very first block is a flat numbered list of
  **34 items** for `api/_server.mjs` — items 24 through 34 are eleven
  consecutive `Catch and Release` findings, seven of which are the identical
  shape (`res.text()` inside a try). `src/routes/settings.ts` further down has 7.
  PRD principle is "Default `crimes scan` shows top findings only, not
  everything. `--all` is opt-in" — but the default already prints everything for
  the files it does show.
- **why it matters:** The lede is buried by construction. Nothing distinguishes
  the one finding worth acting on from ten near-identical repeats of the same
  pattern. Collapsing repeats of one charge in one file into
  `Catch and Release ×11 (lines …)` would cut this report roughly in half with no
  information loss.
- **severity:** real problem

### Three different file counts in three commands, none reconciled
- **repo:** mysidekick-cc
- **what happened:** `scan` header: "241 findings across **74** files"; then
  "Showing 5 of **63** files"; JSON `coverage.files_total` is **239**;
  `hotspots` header says "**220** files". Four numbers, no explanation of the
  relationship between any two of them. (74 is real — I verified distinct
  `file` values in the JSON. 63 is 74 minus the files rolled into the
  "Also flagged elsewhere" line. 239 is files *scanned*. 220 is files with git
  history in the window.)
- **why it matters:** Every one of those is defensible on its own and the set is
  incoherent to read. A user cannot tell whether the tool is confused or they are.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `hotspots` is the honest view and `scan` is not — and the tool prefers `scan`
- **repo:** mysidekick-cc
- **command:** `crimes hotspots … --no-init` vs `crimes scan … --no-init`
- **what happened:** `hotspots` ranks `src/server.ts`, `src/prep/compileBrief.ts`,
  `src/db.ts` — the real, high-churn, load-bearing files. The gitignored bundles
  are **absent**, because they have no git history and churn-based ranking drops
  them for free. `scan` puts one of those same bundles at #1. The two commands
  disagree about what matters most in the repo, and the one the docs push you to
  (`npx crimes scan` is "the canonical first-run command") is the wrong one.
- **why it matters:** The fix for `scan` may be as cheap as borrowing what
  `hotspots` already knows: a tracked-in-git check.
- **severity:** real problem

### `context` and `explain` are the best surfaces in the tool
- **repo:** mysidekick-cc
- **command:** `crimes context src/prep/compileBrief.ts --no-init`,
  `crimes explain crime_00001 --no-init`
- **what happened:** Recording a positive. `context` gave agent guidance, related
  files with *why* they are related ("shares domain token 'prep'"), 8 likely
  tests, churn/author/test-gap clues, then every finding with full evidence.
  `explain` showed the confidence arithmetic inline
  (`confidence 0.78 = 0.72 base + 0.06 (handler contains only a comment)`) and a
  copy-pasteable suppression command. Both ran in 3–5 s. Every summary I read
  explained itself without opening the file.
- **why it matters:** These justify the product. They are also the two commands
  the human `scan` report does the least to lead you toward.
- **severity:** n/a — positive

### `crimes diff` returns three integers and nothing to act on
- **repo:** mysidekick-cc
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~5...HEAD --no-init`
- **what happened:** Entire output: "New crimes: 18 / Fixed crimes: 3 /
  Unchanged crimes: 181". No list, no ids, no files, and no hint that
  `--format json` would give more. `--all` is not accepted.
- **why it matters:** "18 new crimes" without saying which ones is not
  actionable. `verdict` at least names a reason and a next action; `diff`, the
  lower-level command, gives less.
- **severity:** annoyance

### Misleading Commander error on a malformed command
- **repo:** mysidekick-cc
- **command:** `crimes "hotspots /path" --no-init --no-color` (my own zsh
  quoting mistake — zsh does not word-split unquoted `$var`)
- **what happened:** Error was `error: unknown option '--no-color'`, exit 1.
  The unknown thing was the *command*, and `--no-color` is a documented,
  working option for that command.
- **why it matters:** Sent me looking for an option-parsing bug in `hotspots`
  that does not exist. Low value, but it cost real minutes.
- **severity:** annoyance

# pydantic

## Friction log — pydantic

Repo: `~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/pydantic` @ `63f2722f6bfedf0c5b645b348a62ca0ce0b5358c`
Binary: `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes` (crimes 0.17.0)

Size at scan time: 405 `.py` (171,350 LOC), 127 `.rs` (pydantic-core submodule,
checked out), 99 `.md`, 57 `.png`, 24 `.yml`, 19 `.toml`.
289 of the 405 `.py` files are tests/conftest (110,559 LOC = 64% of Python LOC).

**Note: this repo is NOT pure Python.** The `pydantic-core/` submodule is
checked out with 127 Rust source files. crimes has no Rust pack, so this is
a live test of `files_universal_only`.

### `tier` is JS-only: 100% of Python findings are labelled `domain`, including tests
- **repo:** pydantic (identical on drf)
- **command:** `crimes scan <repo> --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `[...new Set(findings.map(f => f.tier))]` == `["domain"]` for
  **578/578** pydantic findings and **104/104** drf findings. 118 pydantic
  findings (20.4%) and 20 drf findings (19.2%) are physically inside `tests/`,
  and every one of them is `tier: "domain"`. 21 pydantic findings are in `docs/`
  — also `domain`.
- **root cause (read from source, no change made):**
  `packages/core/src/config.ts:17` `DEFAULT_NON_DOMAIN_PATTERNS` is
  `["scripts/**","examples/**","fixtures/**","public/**","**/__tests__/**",
  "**/*.test.{ts,tsx,js,jsx}","**/*.spec.{ts,tsx,js,jsx}"]`.
  **Not one pattern can match a Python test.** Python uses `tests/`,
  `test_*.py`, `*_test.py`, `conftest.py`; the glob list knows only
  `__tests__/` and the `.test.`/`.spec.` infix on `{ts,tsx,js,jsx}`.
- **the twist:** crimes already has a correct Python-aware test classifier —
  `packages/core/src/util/test-files.ts` `TEST_FILE_RE` matches `tests/`,
  `test_*.py` and `*_test.py`, and its own doc comment brags about unifying
  this in 0.14.0 ("a file is a test file or it isn't"). And
  `util/scope-class.ts` `classifyScope()` builds on it. But `tier` does **not**
  use either — `context-helpers.ts:139` calls `makeTierClassifier(config.scopeTiers.nonDomain)`,
  a third, glob-based, JS-only mechanism. Three test classifiers, and the one
  that reaches the JSON contract is the only one that can't read Python.
- **and `classifyScope` barely matters anyway:** grepping consumers,
  `classifyScope` is imported by exactly 4 detectors — `unsafe-retry`,
  `unbounded-async-fanout`, `swallowed-error`, `dependency-provenance-gap` —
  all 0.16.0 JS-side detectors. **Zero Python detectors and zero universal
  detectors** (`large_file`, `large_function`, `commented_out_code`,
  `todo_density`) consult it. So on a Python repo the shared exclusion policy
  is dead code.
- **why it matters:** This is the exact mechanism the protocol names as the
  answer to "did crimes discount tests". On Python it is inert. A consumer
  filtering `tier == "domain"` to get real code gets the whole test suite too.
  It also means `weak_test_signal.py` (52 findings, 100% in tests, by
  construction) competes for the default top-N against production findings.
- **severity:** real problem

### Parser stress test: the grammar is solid, with ONE real gap (PEP 696)
- **repo:** pydantic
- **command:** direct probe of `packages/language-py/dist/index.js` `parsePyFile()`
  over all 406 `.py`/`.pyi` files, plus a synthetic grammar matrix.
- **whole-repo sweep result:** 406 files, **405 clean, 1 `hasSyntaxErrors`,
  0 threw, 0 files with `def` lines but zero parsed functions.** drf: 154/154
  clean. So the answer to "did it actually parse" is essentially yes.
- **grammar capability matrix** (synthetic files, one construct each, run
  through `parsePyFile`):

  | construct | result |
  | --- | --- |
  | `match`/`case` incl. mapping, sequence, class, or-patterns | clean, symbols correct |
  | walrus `:=` (if and while) | clean |
  | `except*` / `except* (A, B) as eg` (PEP 654, 3.11) | clean |
  | PEP 701 nested-quote f-strings (3.12) | clean |
  | PEP 695 `def f[T]()` | clean |
  | PEP 695 `class C[T]` | clean, class+method extracted |
  | PEP 695 `type Alias = int`, `type Pair[T] = ...` | clean |
  | PEP 695 bounds `class M[T: int]` | clean |
  | PEP 695 `class M[*Ts]`, `class M[**P]` | clean |
  | **PEP 696 type-param defaults `[T = int]` (3.13)** | **`hasSyntaxErrors = true`** |
  | `@overload` chains | clean, all 3 arms extracted separately |
  | metaclass `__new__` / `metaclass=` | clean, `Meta`+`C`+methods extracted |
  | `if TYPE_CHECKING:` + string annotations | clean, imports counted |
  | positional-only `/`, kw-only `*`, `**kw` | clean |
  | PEP 614 arbitrary decorator expressions | clean |

- **the one gap, isolated to a 1-line repro:** any `= <default>` inside a type
  parameter list is a hard grammar error. Verified across all four positions:
  - `class M[T, U = int]:` → ERROR
  - `def f[T = int](x): ...` → ERROR
  - `type Al[T = int] = list[T]` → ERROR (**and 0 functions, 0 classes extracted**)
  - `def f[*Ts = *tuple[int]](x): ...` → ERROR

  The same declarations **without** defaults parse clean, as do bounds, `*Ts`
  and `**P` — so this is specifically PEP 696 (Python 3.13), not PEP 695.
  Bundled grammar is the vendored `tree-sitter-python.wasm` under
  `packages/language-py/dist/`; `web-tree-sitter ^0.26.11`.
- **blast radius today:** small but non-zero. Recovery is graceful for the
  `def`/`class` forms (symbols still come out), but the `type Al[T = int]`
  form loses the **entire file's** symbol table.
- **severity:** real problem (will grow — 3.13 defaults are new but spreading)

### `hasSyntaxErrors` is invisible: a degraded scan and a clean scan are identical
- **repo:** pydantic / drf
- **command:** `grep -rn hasSyntaxErrors packages/*/src`
- **what happened:** `hasSyntaxErrors` is set correctly by
  `language-py/src/parse/index.ts:50` and then consumed by **exactly one
  detector** — `core/src/detectors/py/weak-test-signal.ts:38`, which returns
  `[]` for the file. It is **never** aggregated into `coverage`, never printed,
  never in the JSON. There is no `coverage.parse_errors` / `files_failed` field.
- **why it matters:** This is the precise failure mode the protocol warns about
  ("A degraded scan and a clean scan look identical"). A repo that leans on PEP
  696, or that a future grammar regression breaks, would return a small,
  reassuring finding count. I only found pydantic's one bad file by importing
  the parser directly — **no CLI surface would have told me.**
- **fix shape:** add `coverage.parse_errors: {count, files[]}` (schema change,
  minor + migration) or at minimum a stderr warning (patch).
- **severity:** real problem

### Bonus: tree-sitter flags a file CPython accepts
- **repo:** pydantic
- **file:** `tests/mypy/outputs/mypy-plugin_ini/frozen_field.py`
- **what happened:** the single `hasSyntaxErrors` file in pydantic. CPython
  `ast.parse` accepts it. Minimal repro (5 lines, valid Python):
  ```python
  class C:
      @property
  # a comment at column 0
      def f(self) -> str:
          return "x"
  ```
  A **column-0 comment between a decorator and its `def`, inside an indented
  block** → ERROR node. Move the comment to the decorator's indent and it
  parses clean. Python's tokenizer ignores comment-only lines for INDENT/DEDENT;
  the grammar does not.
- **severity:** annoyance (upstream tree-sitter-python issue, not crimes' code)

### Negative result: `except*` and PEP 695 are NOT exercised by pydantic
- **what happened:** I set out to confirm findings originated from files using
  each construct. Two constructs are **absent from the corpus**:
  - `except*` — `grep -rnE '^\s*except\*'` over 405 files: **zero hits.** My
    first grep "found" one, but it matched the prose `*except*` inside a comment.
  - PEP 695 — all 14 real-looking sites (`def f[T]`, `class M[T]`, `type X =`)
    are inside triple-quoted strings passed to `create_module(...)` in
    `tests/test_validate_call.py` / `tests/test_forward_ref.py`, or inside a
    docstring example in `pydantic/_internal/_generics.py:363`. **Not one is
    module-level code.** The parser correctly extracts nothing there — so would
    CPython's `ast`.
- **why it matters:** worth stating plainly so the round does not claim
  coverage it does not have. pydantic is a weaker modern-syntax stress test
  than its reputation suggests; it targets 3.9+ and puts new syntax behind
  `create_module` + `skipif(sys.version_info < (3,12))`. The synthetic matrix
  above is the real evidence, not the corpus.
- **severity:** annoyance (observation about the corpus)

### `boolean_naming_drift.py` — 33 findings / 116 bindings, and 42% aren't assignments at all
- **repo:** pydantic (33 findings, 116 bindings, 72 distinct names) / drf (7 findings, 13 bindings)
- **command:** `crimes scan <repo> --all --format json --no-init`
- **shape (characterisation only, no verdict):**
  - **Initializer kinds, pydantic:** `boolean literal` 55, **`other` 49**,
    `comparison` 5, `membership` 4, `negation` 2, `identity` 1.
  - **The `other` bucket is annotation-only declarations.** Spot-checked every
    `<other>` in `pydantic/v1/config.py` (10 of them, lines 48–70): the source
    is `anystr_lower: bool`, `validate_all: bool`, `orm_mode: bool` … inside
    `class ConfigDict(TypedDict, total=False)`. **There is no `=` and no value.**
    Same for `pydantic/fields.py:85` — the source is `repr: bool` inside a
    `TypedDict`. The finding renders it as ``​`repr` = <other>`` and the summary
    reads "names **bound to a boolean expression**". For 49 of 116 bindings that
    sentence describes something that does not exist in the file.
  - **Binding site, pydantic:** module/class-body 65, local-in-function 36,
    instance-attribute 15. So the majority are **class-body declarations**, i.e.
    declared API surface, not local control flow.
  - **What the names are.** pydantic: `orm_mode`, `validate_all`,
    `arbitrary_types_allowed`, `populate_by_name`, `str_strip_whitespace`,
    `hide_input_in_errors`, `from_attributes`, `frozen`, `repr`, `init` — these
    are pydantic's **documented public config keys and `Field()` kwargs**;
    several (`repr`, `init`, `init_var`, `frozen`) are name-compatible with
    `dataclasses.field()` by design. drf: `many` (`ListSerializer.many`, DRF's
    single most-used public kwarg), `authenticated_users_only`, `public`,
    `ensure_ascii` (mirrors `json.dumps`), and — at
    `rest_framework/authtoken/models.py:25,60` — **`abstract` and `proxy`, which
    are Django `class Meta` reserved option names.** Those two cannot be
    renamed; Django reads them by name.
  - 0 of 40 findings across both repos are in test files.
- **why it matters:** the fix the detector proposes (`is_`/`has_`/`should_`
  prefix) is, for the majority of these, a breaking public-API rename or an
  impossible framework rename. The evidence string also misdescribes 42% of
  what it matched.
- **severity:** real problem

### `sync_io_in_hotpath.py` — 11 findings, 8 of which are in build/release tooling
- **repo:** pydantic (11) / drf (1)
- **shape:** bucketed by what the file *is*:

  | bucket | n | files |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | release automation scripts | 3 | `release/prepare.py`, `release/push.py`, `release/shared.py` |
  | mkdocs build plugins | 2 | `docs/plugins/main.py`, `docs/plugins/using_update.py` |
  | mypy plugins (compile-time) | 2 | `pydantic/mypy.py`, `pydantic/v1/mypy.py` |
  | test/preview runner | 1 | `pydantic-core/wasm-preview/run_tests.py` |
  | library runtime | 3 | `pydantic/_internal/_git.py`, `pydantic/v1/parse.py`, `pydantic/deprecated/parse.py` |

  **8 of 11 are in code that runs once, offline, on a developer's or CI's
  machine.** There is no worker and no event loop to hold. The summary
  nonetheless says "Each one holds the worker — or the event loop — for its
  full duration", and every finding asserts the call `runs inside <fn>
  (shape "domain")`.
  Of the 3 "library runtime" hits, two are `parse.py` — functions literally
  named `load_file`/`load_str_bytes` whose stated job is to read a file.
  drf's single hit is `rest_framework/management/commands/generateschema.py`
  `handle()` — a Django **management command**, i.e. a CLI entry point.
- **not all wrong:** the async sub-claim is accurate where it fires.
  `wasm-preview/run_tests.py` `main()` genuinely is `async def` (it `await`s
  `micropip.install`), and the evidence correctly escalates for it.
- **root cause is the same missing scope policy:** `release/**`, `docs/plugins/**`
  and `**/management/commands/**` are not in `DEFAULT_NON_DOMAIN_PATTERNS`
  (`scripts/**` is; `release/**` is not), and `sync-io-in-hotpath` does not call
  `classifyScope`. The "hotpath" test is `shape === "domain"`, which is a
  *function-shape* judgement made without reference to whether the file is ever
  on a request path.
- **severity:** real problem

### `direct_date.py` — 3 findings in pydantic, 0 in drf. Genuinely quiet.
- **shape:** `pydantic/v1/types.py:1161,1174` and
  `pydantic/v1/_hypothesis_plugin.py:172,173` (both in the frozen v1 compat
  tree), and `release/prepare.py:113` (a release script). All `date.today()`.
  Evidence is precise: exact call, exact lines, and a correct naive-vs-aware
  sub-claim. **Zero in test files. Zero in drf.**
- **observation:** this detector did *not* over-fire. Of the three named as
  most-likely-to-over-fire, `direct_date` is the one that behaved. Note that
  2 of 3 are in `pydantic/v1/**` — a deliberately-frozen vendored-in copy of
  pydantic v1 that upstream will never modernise. `classifyScope`'s `vendored`
  bucket doesn't match `v1/`, and nothing consults it here anyway.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `docs_code_drift`: indented code fences are not recognised, so Python generics parse as markdown links
- **repo:** pydantic
- **finding:** `crime_00411`, `docs/concepts/models.md` — "16 local links … point at
  file(s) that do not exist on disk", with evidence
  `docs/concepts/models.md:731 → data=1 (not found)`,
  `:733 → data='value' (not found)`, `:735 → data='value' (not found)` …
- **root cause, traced to one regex:**
  `packages/core/src/ia/extract.ts:233` — `const FENCE_RE = /^```/;` — is
  **anchored to column 0**. pydantic's docs put fences inside mkdocs-material
  admonitions and tab blocks, so they are indented:
  `docs/concepts/models.md:715` is `    ```python {upgrade="skip"}`.
  `parseMarkdown` never enters fence state, so the whole code block is scanned
  as prose by
  `MD_LINK_RE = /\[([^\]]+)\]\(([^)\s]+)(?:\s+"[^"]*")?\)/g`.
- **and the collision is exact:** a Python generic subscript immediately
  followed by a call — `Response[int](data=1)` — is **character-for-character a
  markdown inline link** `[int](data=1)`. pydantic's docs are *about generics*,
  so this is maximally unlucky.
- **scale:** `grep -rnE '^[ \t]+```' docs/` = **246 indented fences** vs 1000
  column-0 fences in pydantic's docs. ~20% of the doc corpus is invisible to
  the fence tracker. Every generic example inside one is a candidate link.
- **fix shape:** allow up to 3 leading spaces per CommonMark (`/^ {0,3}```/`),
  and ideally track the opening fence's indent to find its close. Patch-level
  (detector tuning + eval re-run).
- **severity:** real problem

### Vendored / minified assets: `*.min.js` is excluded, `*-min.js` is not
- **repo:** drf
- **what happened:** `rest_framework/static/rest_framework/js/prettify-min.js` —
  a 13 KB minified copy of Google's `prettify` — produced 2 findings
  (`direct_date.js` at :27, and `exact_duplicate_block.js`). Its siblings
  `bootstrap.min.js` (39 KB) and `jquery-3.7.1.min.js` (87 KB) produced **zero**.
- **root cause:** `packages/core/src/config.ts:216` — the only minification
  pattern in the default exclude list is `"**/*.min.js"`. The **hyphen** form
  `*-min.js` is not covered, and neither is `*.min.css` / `*-min.css`.
- **the duplicate finding is the worse half:** `crime_00053`
  `exact_duplicate_block.js` pairs `docs/theme/js/prettify-1.0.js:2-28` with
  `rest_framework/static/rest_framework/js/prettify-min.js:2-28` and proposes
  "extracting a shared helper". Those are the **pretty-printed and the minified
  build of the same vendored third-party file**. There is no shared helper to
  extract. `classifyScope`'s `vendored` bucket doesn't match `static/` either —
  and `exact_duplicate_block` doesn't call it.
- **severity:** real problem (config default — cheapest fix in the round)

### Schema: `lines` is optional, and 7 findings per repo omit it
- **repo:** pydantic (7) / drf (7)
- **what happened:** a consumer doing `f.lines.join("-")` crashes.
  File-level and repo-level findings carry no `lines`:
  `circular_dependency.py`, `docs_code_drift`, `oversized_raster`,
  `raster_should_be_vector`, `exact_duplicate_block.js`.
- **why it matters:** the JSON output is "the product contract". The judging
  protocol for this very round requires "file path, **line range**, the code
  ±30 lines" per card — for 14 findings across these two repos there is no line
  range to build a card from. Not wrong, but it needs to be documented, and
  card tooling needs a fallback.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `crimes context` prints finding IDs that `crimes explain` resolves to different findings
- **repo:** pydantic and drf (both)
- **commands:**
  ```
  crimes context pydantic/_internal/_generate_schema.py --no-init
    →  2. pydantic/_internal/_generate_schema.py:738-875 (_model_schema)
          Charge: God Function  …  id=crime_00002

  crimes explain crime_00002 --no-init
    →  charge: God File
       file:   pydantic/types.py
       lines:  1–3336
  ```
  Different file, different detector, different charge. No error, no warning.
  Reproduces on drf: `context rest_framework/fields.py` labels
  `fields.py:86-110` as `crime_00002`; `explain crime_00002` returns
  `fields.py:1-1966`.
- **mechanism:** `crime_NNNNN` is a **positional index within whichever report
  was just produced**. `context` renumbers from 1 within its file-scoped
  report; `explain` renumbers from 1 within a fresh repo-wide scan. The two
  numbering spaces are unrelated but look identical and share a prefix.
- **why it matters:** scan → note the id → `explain` the id is the documented
  loop, and `context` is the command the `crimes` agent skill tells agents to
  run *before editing*. An agent that reads a `context` finding and calls
  `explain` on the printed id gets a confident, well-formatted explanation of
  **the wrong finding**, and nothing anywhere says so. The `id=` suffix reads
  like a stable handle; it is a line number in disguise.
- **note:** 0.17.0 added `discriminator` fingerprints and `explain` accepts
  "id or fingerprint" — but only some detectors emit `discriminator` (of the
  578 pydantic findings I saw it on `exact_duplicate_block`), so it is not yet
  a general substitute.
- **fix shape:** either make the id content-derived and report-independent, or
  have `context` print the fingerprint instead of a positional id.
- **severity:** real problem

### Positive: output is deterministic
- **repo:** pydantic
- **command:** `crimes scan . --all --format json --no-init` run three times
- **what happened:** all three byte-identical, including the run captured 20
  minutes earlier as the artifact. `cmp` clean. Worth recording because the
  protocol asks specifically about evidence reproducibility across runs.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)

### `crimes context` "Related files" is just directory siblings, alphabetically
- **repo:** pydantic / drf
- **command:** `crimes context pydantic/main.py --no-init`
- **what happened:** Related files = `pydantic/__init__.py`,
  `alias_generators.py`, `aliases.py`, `annotated_handlers.py`,
  `_migration.py` — "— same directory" for every entry, and they are simply the
  first 5 filenames alphabetically in that directory. `main.py` actually
  imports `_internal/_model_construction.py`, `_internal/_fields.py`, etc.,
  and the import graph is clearly built (`circular_dependency.py` uses it,
  `blast_radius_importers: 285`). The section presents an alphabetical `ls` as
  if it were derived relatedness.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `crimes context` "Likely tests" is unbounded and imprecise
- **repo:** drf
- **command:** `crimes context rest_framework/serializers.py --no-init`
- **what happened:** **35 likely tests**, printed in full in the human view —
  longer than the findings section. The list includes non-tests that merely
  live under `tests/`: `tests/browsable_api/serializers.py`,
  `tests/browsable_api/views.py`, `tests/schemas/views.py`,
  `tests/importable/__init__.py`. `testBaseCovers()` matches
  `tests/browsable_api/serializers.py` to `rest_framework/serializers.py` on
  basename alone, ignoring that the former is a test *helper*.
- **why it matters:** basename matching across a deep test tree gets
  progressively worse the more conventional the naming. "Read the likely tests
  before editing" is not actionable at 35 entries.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `large_function.py` is 43% of the corpus, and ~42% of it isn't about size
- **repos:** pydantic 249, drf 47 = **296 of 682 total findings (43%)**
- **split by what actually triggered it** (comparing the finding's own
  "N lines (threshold T)" evidence):

  | repo | size-triggered (N > T) | nesting-triggered (N ≤ T) |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | pydantic | 148 (59%) | **101 (41%)** |
  | drf | 24 (51%) | **23 (49%)** |

  Of the 124 nesting-triggered findings, **81 are at nesting depth exactly 4** —
  the trigger point.
- **the summary is wrong for all 124 of them.** Example, `drf`
  `rest_framework/serializers.py:1452-1467`:
  > `get_unique_together_constraints` is **16 lines** and nests 5 levels deep.
  > **At this size** an agent must read the whole body before it can safely
  > change any part of it.

  "At this size", for a 16-line function, against a 50-line threshold. The
  charge is "God Function". The detector is really *large-or-deeply-nested*
  function, but the prose and the leading evidence line are both written for
  the size case only.
- **the nesting threshold is JS-calibrated and Python pays for it.** Probing
  `maxNestingDepth` with matched synthetic files:

  | source | visual indent levels | reported depth |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `def` → `for` → `if` → stmt | 3 | **2** |
  | `def` → `for` → `try/except` → stmt | 3 | **3** |
  | `def` → `with` → stmt | 2 | 1 |
  | `def` → `if` → `if` → `if` → stmt | 4 | 3 |
  | `def` → `for` → `with` → `try/except` → stmt | 4 | **4 — trips at 7 lines** |

  **`try/except` costs one more level than `if/else` at identical
  indentation**, because the `except` handler is counted as its own level.
  Python is an EAFP language: `try/except` is the idiomatic guard where JS
  would use `if`. Add `with` (context managers, ubiquitous) and idiomatic
  Python reaches depth 4 a full indent level sooner than equivalent JS.
  The concrete case in drf, `rest_framework/fields.py:86-110 get_attribute` —
  a 25-line module-level function that does `for` / `try` / `if` — is charged
  "God Function" at depth 4. There is nothing unusual about it.
- **why it matters:** this is the highest-volume detector in the round by a
  factor of 3, on two of the most-reviewed Python codebases in existence, and
  roughly 4 in 10 of its findings are produced by a nesting rule that Python's
  own idioms inflate.
- **fix shape:** per-language nesting threshold, and/or stop counting `except`
  and `with` as full levels; and fix the summary to say "nests N levels deep"
  rather than "at this size" when size was not the trigger.
- **severity:** real problem

### `todo_density` fires below its own stated threshold in 13 of 25 findings
- **repos:** pydantic 24, drf 1
- **what happened:** `pydantic/main.py` is charged "Unfinished Business" with
  evidence `["3× TODO", "1.6 markers per 1k LOC (threshold 10)"]`. The finding
  states a measurement **6× under** the threshold it cites, and fires anyway.
  13 of the 25 `todo_density` findings across both repos are below their own
  quoted threshold.
- **root cause:** `packages/core/src/detectors/todo-density.ts:40` —
  `if (matches.length < 3 && density < threshold) return [];` — it is an **OR**:
  ≥3 markers *or* density above threshold. The evidence renderer at line 67
  only ever emits the density clause, so the absolute-count trigger is invisible
  and the finding reads as self-contradictory.
- **why it matters:** a reader's first move is to check the number against the
  threshold, see it is 6× under, and conclude the tool is broken. It isn't —
  it just never says why it fired. Also: the detector is named `todo_density`
  and half its output is not about density.
- **fix shape:** emit the clause that actually fired — `"3 markers (floor: 3)"`
  — when the count trigger is what caught it. Patch-level, evidence text only.
- **severity:** real problem (cheap to fix, disproportionately corrosive)

### Human `scan` output buries the lede: 23 consecutive identical charges
- **repo:** pydantic
- **command:** `crimes scan <repo> --no-init`
- **what happened:** 131 lines of output. The default view shows the top 5
  files but prints **every** finding within each — `_generate_schema.py` gets
  29 numbered lines, of which **items 2 through 23 are all "God Function"**,
  each a near-identical sentence differing only in name/line-count. The
  genuinely distinct signals in that file (Boolean Naming Drift, 4× Commented-Out
  Code, Unfinished Business) are items 24–29, below the fold.
- **also:** the per-file footer prints `id=crime_00047 … crime_00345` — a
  *range* spanning ids that mostly belong to other files. It is not a usable
  handle for anything in the list, and given the `context`/`explain` id
  mismatch above, ids in the human view are doubly untrustworthy.
- **also:** the header says `578 findings across 223 files` but never says how
  many files were **scanned** (637). That number is the one that would have
  caught the broken clone.
- **why it matters:** "Signal over exhaustiveness" is a stated product
  constraint. Top-5-files-but-all-their-findings is exhaustive within a
  truncated set — the worst of both.
- **severity:** real problem

### `commented_out_code`: ~90% of a random sample is English prose, incl. Rust doc comments
- **repos:** pydantic **77**, drf 2. The 38× asymmetry is itself the clue.
- **sampling:** deterministic — every 8th of the 77 pydantic findings (n=10).
  **9 of 10 are explanatory prose comments, not commented-out code.** Examples:
  - `pydantic/fields.py:545-558` — "HACK: It is common for users to define
    'make model partial' (or similar) utilities, that convert all model fields
    to be optional…"  (14 lines of English)
  - `pydantic/_internal/_generate_schema.py:470-475` — six lines explaining why
    an isinstance check is used for empty enums.
  - **`pydantic-core/src/definitions.rs:1-5` — a Rust `///` doc comment.**
    "/// Definition / reference management…". Rustdoc is the language's
    *documentation* mechanism; flagging it as commented-out code is a category
    error, and it comes from the universal pack applying a JS/Python comment
    heuristic to `.rs`.
  - The single code-looking hit is
    `tests/mypy/outputs/.../plugin_fail_baseConfig.py` — `# MYPY: error: …`
    expected-output fixture data.
- **mechanism:** the stated rule is ">=3 lines contain code-shaped tokens
  (def/fn/function/class/let/var/const/etc.)". pydantic's **domain vocabulary
  is those tokens** — its prose is unavoidably about `class`, `def`, `type`,
  `field`, `subclass`, `dataclass`. drf's prose is about serializers and
  viewsets, so it scores 2. The detector is measuring how much a project's
  comments talk about language constructs, not how much dead code they contain.
- **why it matters:** 79 findings, third-highest volume in the round, on
  material that is the *opposite* of a crime — these are the comments a
  maintainer wrote so the next reader would not have to guess. The `context`
  "Agent guidance" then instructs: "Do not copy disabled code from comments;
  verify whether it should be deleted or explained as rationale" — pointing an
  agent at well-written rationale and suggesting deletion.
- **fix shape:** require code *structure* (balanced delimiters, assignment or
  call syntax, trailing `:` / `;`) rather than keyword presence; exclude `///`
  and `/** */` doc-comment forms; consider gating on the file's language.
- **severity:** real problem

### `circular_dependency.py` cannot distinguish `TYPE_CHECKING` imports from runtime ones
- **repo:** pydantic (3 findings, incl. a **46-module** cycle rooted at
  `pydantic/__init__.py`, severity high, confidence 0.95)
- **what I verified:** parsing this file with `parsePyFile`
  ```python
  from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
  if TYPE_CHECKING:
      from b import B
  def f(x: "B"): return x
  ```
  yields two import records, and the `from b import B` record is
  **indistinguishable** from a module-level one — no `typeOnly` /
  `typeChecking` / guard flag on `PyImport`. So the import graph the cycle
  detector consumes cannot exclude type-only edges.
- **why it matters:** a `TYPE_CHECKING`-guarded import **never executes**. The
  detector's entire stated rationale is runtime behaviour — "Python executes a
  module top-to-bottom on first import… whichever module is imported first
  decides whether this raises — so it can pass under `pytest` and fail under a
  production server". For a type-only edge that outcome is impossible by
  construction. **54 of 139 files in `pydantic/` contain `if TYPE_CHECKING:`
  blocks**, so the exposure is large — and modern typed Python (which is
  precisely what pydantic is) uses this pattern *specifically to break* import
  cycles. The tool may be reporting the cycle a maintainer already fixed.
- **what I did NOT verify:** whether pydantic's specific 46-module SCC survives
  removal of type-only edges. Proving that needs the graph rebuilt both ways.
  Flagging as unresolved rather than asserting a false positive.
- **severity:** real problem (unconfirmed on this instance, but the detector
  provably cannot tell the difference)

# hono

## Friction log — hono (CONTROL)

- **repo:** `~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/hono`
- **SHA:** `51db3131d5e97076327edaa0afdb60ebb77c264f`
- **build:** crimes 0.17.0
- **role:** CONTROL. Well-maintained, heavily-reviewed TS web framework. Few findings is the correct answer.

## Size baseline (pre-scan)
- 482 tracked files
- `.ts` 324 files / 65,989 lines; `.tsx` 32 / 14,947; `.mts` 27; `.mjs` 2 / 106; `.js` 1 / 37
- `src/` alone: 308 files, 61,879 lines of `.ts`
- `.md` 9 files / 677 lines total  <- NOTE: no markdown file anywhere near the 1000-line docs threshold
- `.json` 34 / 1,911; `.yml` 13 / 610

---

## Timings / robustness

| run | wall | user | exit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `scan --all --format json` | 34.55s | 9.01s | 0 |
| `scan` (human) | 19.17s | 8.72s | 0 |
| `hotspots` | 39.37s | 9.15s | 0 |
| `verdict` | 55.23s | 15.68s | 0 |
| `diff HEAD~30...HEAD` | 52.74s | 15.48s | 0 |
| p-limit `scan --all --format json` | 1.32s | 0.70s | 0 |

stderr empty on every run. No stack traces. No hangs.

`coverage` for hono: `files_total` 390, `files_by_language` `{js: 381}`,
`files_universal_only` 9 (`.md` 8, `.sh` 1), `packs_loaded`
`["universal","language-js","language-py"]`, `by_package` 5 entries
(all five `benchmarks/*` workspaces, all `dominant_language: js`).

### THE CONTROL NUMBER: 377 findings on hono
- **repo:** hono
- **what happened:** 377 findings — 22 high, 205 medium, 150 low — across 152 files.
  81,962 lines of scanned JS/TS → **4.6 findings per 1000 lines**.
  27,658 non-test lines → **13.6 per 1000 non-test lines**.
- **the distribution is top-heavy in a way that matters:** 3 detectors produce 65% of the
  total. `large_function` 143 (38%), `weak_test_signal` 69 (18%), `high_fan_in_fan_out` 34
  (9%). Add `large_file` 26 and `boolean_naming_drift` 24 and 5 detectors are 84% of output.
- **why it matters:** hono is one of the most-reviewed TypeScript codebases on npm. 377 is
  not a quiet result, and the shape of it says the volume comes from a handful of
  size/count detectors rather than from a broad true signal.
- **severity:** real problem

### 186 of 377 findings (49%) are in test files
- **repo:** hono
- **what happened:** the default human view's last section reads
  `Also flagged elsewhere / tests/ 186 findings / Run with --all to see them.`
  `tier` split is 191 domain / 186 nonDomain.
- **why it matters:** half the output is about test code. The default view does bucket it
  away, which is the right instinct — but it is still half of the number a user sees in the
  header line ("377 findings"), and all 377 are in the JSON with no `--exclude-tests` flag
  and no `tier` filter on the CLI.
- **severity:** real problem

### `--all` vs default: 35 of 377 shown (9.3%) — the constraint holds on hono, not on p-limit
- **repo:** both
- **what happened:** hono default human view lists 35 numbered findings across 5 of 85 files,
  then `Showing 5 of 85 files. Run with --all for every finding.`
  p-limit default human view lists **9 of 10**.
- **why it matters:** the "signal over exhaustiveness" constraint is implemented as
  `scan.topFiles: 5`, i.e. a **file** cap, not a **finding** cap. On a big repo that happens
  to compress 10×; on a small repo it does nothing at all. The gap is an accident of repo
  size, not a designed budget.
- **also:** `--all` does not affect `--format json` at all (byte-identical output).
- **severity:** real problem

### Finding ordering — and therefore `crime_NNNNN` ids — is NOT reproducible across runs
- **repo:** hono
- **command:** `crimes scan <hono> --all --format json --no-init`, run twice, same SHA
- **what happened:** the finding *multiset* is identical (377, same severity split, identical
  `coverage`). The **order is not**, and ids are assigned by position, so:
  - run A `crime_00024` = `large_file` on `src/middleware/secure-headers/secure-headers.ts`
  - run B `crime_00024` = `hardcoded_localhost` on `benchmarks/http-server/benchmark.ts`
  5 ids changed identity between two back-to-back runs. First order divergence at index 23.
  Likely cause: the `recency` score is wall-clock derived and drifted `0.71 → 0.70` between
  runs, flipping a sort tie.
  p-limit **was** byte-identical across two runs (too small to hit a tie).
- **why it matters:** `crimes explain crime_00024` is the documented workflow and it resolves
  to a different finding on a second run of an unchanged repo. The human `scan` output prints
  `id=crime_00019 … crime_00153` ranges for the user to feed back in. Any ticket, PR comment,
  or agent transcript quoting a `crime_` id is stale the moment anyone re-runs.
- **severity:** blocker

### Fingerprints are not unique — 97 collisions on hono
- **repo:** hono
- **what happened:** the stable fingerprint is `<type>::<file>::<symbol>`. hono's 377 findings
  reduce to **280 unique fingerprints — 97 collisions**. Worst offenders:
  9× `large_function::src/types.test.ts::describe callback`,
  8× `weak_test_signal::runtime-tests/deno-jsx/jsx.test.tsx::` (empty symbol),
  8× `weak_test_signal::src/jsx/dom/intrinsic-element/components.test.tsx::`.
- **consequence 1:** `crimes diff HEAD~30...HEAD` reports `New 4 / Fixed 12 / Unchanged 284`
  = **288 findings accounted for, against a scan total of 377**. 89 findings (24%) are
  invisible to `diff` because they collapse into a colliding fingerprint.
- **consequence 2:** `crimes ignore <fingerprint>` would silently suppress up to 9 findings.
- **consequence 3:** `crimes explain <fingerprint>` is ambiguous by construction.
- **severity:** blocker

### `hotspots` ranks hono's `package.json` as the #1 risk in the repo
- **repo:** hono
- **command:** `crimes hotspots --no-init`
- **what happened:** `1. package.json risk 0.72 (72%) · 29 changes · latest 2026-07-31 ·
  1 finding · highest low`. The #2 entry is at 0.58. Nothing else is close.
- **why it matters:** `package.json` churns 29× in 90 days because of version bumps and
  dependency updates. The risk model reads churn as danger with no notion that a manifest's
  churn is mechanical. The single most prominent line of output in the whole command is
  pointing a maintainer at their version field.
- **also:** `hotspots` ranks files that `scan` never scanned — `package.json`, `readme.md`,
  `index.d.ts` all appear in p-limit's hotspots list at risk 0.03 with 0 findings, while
  `coverage.files_total` for p-limit is 8 and does not include `package.json`. Two commands,
  two different file universes.
- **severity:** real problem

### `hotspots` on p-limit lists every file including 0-finding ones
- **repo:** p-limit
- **what happened:** `repo: p-limit · since 90d · 7 files`, and it lists all 7 — including
  three entries with `0 findings · highest none` at `risk 0.03 ( 3%)`.
- **why it matters:** on a clean repo this is the graceful-degradation case, and it half
  works: the numbers are honestly low (max 27%) and it does not manufacture urgency. But it
  still pads the list with rows whose only content is "nothing here". There is no "nothing is
  hot in this repo" statement — the user has to infer it from the percentages.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `verdict` degrades correctly on both repos
- **repo:** both
- **what happened:** `Verdict: UNCHANGED / New: 0 / Fixed: 0 / Reason: no new findings and no
  fixed findings / Recommended next action: no maintainability change vs. base — verdict is
  advisory only.` Exit 0.
- **why it matters:** this is the right answer and the right tone. Logged as a positive.
  Caveat: it took 55s on hono to say "nothing changed", because it does a full double scan.
- **severity:** annoyance (the 55s only)

### `crimes explain` hard-fails with exit 2 on a finding type the scanner emits
- **repo:** hono
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_00369 --from hono.json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `crimes: no detector registered for finding.type "oversized_raster".`
  Exit code **2**. `crime_00369` is a real finding in the report
  (`docs/images/hono-logo.png`, 436.2 KB, low).
- **why it matters:** `scan` can produce a finding that `explain` refuses to explain. The
  asset-pack detectors are not in whatever registry `explain` consults. Any agent looping
  `explain` over every id in a report will hit a non-zero exit on a valid input.
- **severity:** real problem

### `unsafe_retry` HIGH on a virtual-DOM reconciler, reasoning about "database write"
- **repo:** hono
- **finding:** `crime_00045` — `src/jsx/dom/render.ts:497-665`, **high**, conf 0.68
- **what happened:** evidence reads `retry construct: build() calls itself from its catch
  block`, `retried mutation — update at line 625 (name suggests a write)`, and
  `boundary: database write (matched on 'update') — a duplicate here is visible to the
  customer, not just to the logs`.
- **why it matters:** not judging correctness — but the evidence asserts a **database write**
  in a JSX DOM renderer, on the strength of the identifier `update`. This is the highest-
  severity finding in the file that the tool itself nominates as the repo's top risk, and its
  supporting sentence is about a database that does not exist in this package.
- **severity:** real problem

### `accessible_interaction_risk` says a test file "appears to ship" UI
- **repo:** hono
- **finding:** `crime_00304` — `src/jsx/dom/index.test.tsx`, medium, `tier: nonDomain`
- **what happened:** summary: "`src/jsx/dom/index.test.tsx` appears to ship 6 clickable
  non-button elements without accessibility metadata. Keyboard and screen-reader users will
  not be able to…"
- **why it matters:** the detector already knows the file is `nonDomain` — the tier is right
  there on the finding — and still emits a medium-severity claim about shipped user-facing
  UI for a test fixture. The word "ship" is doing real work in that sentence and it is false.
- **severity:** real problem

### `contract_drift` summary names the same type twice
- **repo:** hono
- **finding:** `crime_00262` — `src/helper/css/index.ts:217-223`, medium
- **what happened:** "`DefaultContextType` and `DefaultContextType` appear to describe the
  same record but disagree on 1 field."
- **why it matters:** the summary is unreadable without opening the file — the protocol
  explicitly asks for this. Two same-named types from different modules need qualifying.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `explain`'s "test gap" gloss states a fact that is false
- **repo:** p-limit (also fires on hono)
- **what happened:** every p-limit `explain` renders
  `test gap: 0.50 — a test file matches this module's name but does not import it`.
  For `index.js`: no test file matched the name (`crimes context index.js` says so in the
  same breath: *"no test file matched the target basename"*), **and** `test.js:8` is
  `import pLimit, {limitFunction} from './index.js';` — it does import it.
  So the two commands contradict each other, and the surviving sentence is wrong on both
  clauses.
- **why it matters:** 0.50 is the no-signal midpoint, but it is rendered with a confident
  causal explanation instead of "no signal". That is worse than printing nothing.
- **severity:** real problem

### Suggested `crimes ignore` commands are not shell-pasteable
- **repo:** both
- **what happened:** `explain` closes with a copy-paste block:
  - `crimes ignore swallowed_error::index.js::run → catch_clause --reason "…"` — unquoted
    fingerprint containing a space and a U+2192 arrow; the shell splits it.
  - `crimes ignore unbounded_async_fanout::benchmark.js::<module> → writeFile --reason "…"`
    — same, plus `<` and `>` which the shell treats as redirections.
  - `crimes ignore boolean_naming_drift::index.js:: --reason "…"` — trailing empty symbol.
- **why it matters:** the one command the tool tells you to run is broken as printed for any
  finding whose symbol has a space, an arrow, or angle brackets — i.e. most of them.
- **severity:** real problem

### Zero case: CONFIRMED clean on hono
- **repo:** hono
- **what happened:** `cross_language_*` detectors: **0 findings** (no `detector_id`
  containing `cross_language`). Python-pack detectors: **0 findings**
  (`pack == "language-py"` count = 0). Correct for a TS-only repo.
- **BUT** `coverage.packs_loaded` still lists `language-py` on a repo with zero `.py` files,
  and `coverage.files_by_language` correctly reports only `{js: 381}`. `packs_loaded` and
  `files_by_language` disagree and only the latter is informative.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `.json` / `.yaml` are never scanned at default config
- **repo:** hono
- **what happened:** hono's 735-line `benchmarks/query-param/package-lock.json` and 698-line
  `package.json` produce **no** `large_file` finding. `DEFAULT_SOURCE_INCLUDES` has no
  `json`/`yaml`/`yml`/`txt` entry. Verified against a synthetic probe in the p-limit clone:
  with an `include` override, a 1201-line `.json` fires `large_file` **high** at
  `4.0× the configured 300-line threshold` — so the intended behaviour is implemented and
  simply unreachable. See `p-limit-friction.md` for the full probe.
- **severity:** real problem

### Sandbox artefact (environment, not crimes)
- **command:** first `/usr/bin/time -p crimes scan <hono> --all --format json` under the
  agent sandbox was SIGTERMed at 17.5s → exit 143, leaving a **0-byte** `hono.json`.
  Re-ran unsandboxed: exit 0 in 34.55s. Recording it because three pre-existing artefacts in
  `~/crimes-dogfood/artifacts/` (`airflow.json`, `zulip.json`, `ortomate-studio.json`) are
  0 bytes with 0-byte `.time` files — same signature. Anyone reading those as "crimes found
  nothing" would be wrong.
- **severity:** annoyance (for this round: real problem for whoever inherits those files)
