### Honest first-time-user reaction — hono
- **repo:** hono
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/hono --no-init`
- **what happened / my reaction:** mixed, leaning toward distrust — but for different
  reasons than p-limit, and it is closer to salvageable.

  **What builds trust here.** The default view is *good*. It does not dump 377 findings; it
  shows 5 files, buckets 186 test findings out of the way, and closes with one concrete next
  action. The per-file rollups (`Risk: churn low · test gap bottom-quartile · blast
  top-quartile (48 importers)`) are the kind of thing no linter gives you, and
  `src/jsx/dom/render.ts` genuinely *is* the scariest file in hono — 826 lines, a 169-line
  `build()`, a self-recursive catch-block retry, in a circular dependency, imported by 48
  files. If the tool had shown me only that file I would have been impressed. `verdict` said
  UNCHANGED without inventing drama. Every evidence block shows its arithmetic.

  **What costs trust.**
  1. **The header number is 377.** Whatever the default view does downstream, the first
     thing on screen is a four-digit-adjacent count for a codebase that is famously tidy.
     And 84% of it comes from five detectors that mostly count lines and imports.
  2. **`hotspots` puts `package.json` first, at 72%,** ~14 points clear of anything real.
     That is the answer to "what should I worry about" and it is the version field.
  3. **The high-severity findings do not hold up under their own evidence.** The single
     `unsafe_retry` HIGH on `render.ts` argues from `boundary: database write (matched on
     'update')` inside a virtual-DOM reconciler. `accessible_interaction_risk` says a
     `.test.tsx` file "appears to ship" clickable elements while carrying `tier: nonDomain`
     on the same object. `Dev-Server URL` HIGH is `127.0.0.1:3000` in a benchmark script.
  4. **`Plural Mismatch` volunteers its own unreliability in the output:**
     `plural name, singular type: children: Child @L815, v1 detector — type aliases and
     generic types are silently skipped`. Shipping a caveat about detector immaturity inside
     the finding text is honest, but it reads as "we know this one is rough".
  5. **Two runs, same SHA, different ids.** Once I noticed that `crime_00024` names a
     different finding on a re-run, everything in the report that references an id became
     untrustworthy to quote.

  **Net.** I would keep using `crimes context <file>` before an edit — that command is
  genuinely good and it is the product's actual wedge. I would not put `crimes scan`'s
  headline number in front of a team, and I would not wire `hotspots` into anything. The
  tool's problem on hono is not that it is wrong about everything; it is that the loudest
  outputs (total count, hotspot #1, the HIGH findings) are the least reliable ones, and the
  quiet outputs (per-file context, evidence blocks, verdict) are the good ones. That ranking
  is exactly backwards from what earns trust on first contact.
- **severity:** real problem

---

# p-limit

## Friction log — p-limit (CONTROL / null hypothesis)

- **repo:** `~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/p-limit`
- **SHA:** `df476048d023ff868cd45b35ee47f5fb0ca2b25a`
- **build:** crimes 0.17.0
- **role:** THE null hypothesis. A few hundred lines of exemplary sindresorhus code. Honest expected total is ~0.

## Size baseline (pre-scan)
16 tracked files, 1,637 lines total including docs and license.

```
 12 .editorconfig      234 benchmark.js
  1 .gitattributes     136 index.d.ts
  3 .github/security.md 127 index.js
 22 .github/workflows/main.yml  33 index.test-d.ts
  2 .gitignore           9 license
  1 .npmrc              58 package.json
                       173 readme.md
                       213 recipes.md
                       236 scripts/benchmarker.js
                       377 test.js
```

Source proper (`index.js` + `index.d.ts`) = 263 lines. Everything else is tests,
benchmarks, docs, config.

**No markdown file over 1000 lines** (largest is `recipes.md` at 213), so the
0.17.0 `docs` shape cannot be exercised here on its own.

---

### THE CONTROL NUMBER: crimes finds 10 findings in a 263-line library
- **repo:** p-limit
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/p-limit --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** 10 findings. 0 high, 6 medium, 4 low. Across 4 of 8 scanned files.
  Scanned universe is 8 files (6 js + 2 md); `index.js` + `index.d.ts` = 263 lines.
  10 findings / 1,144 lines of scanned JS = **8.7 per 1000 lines**.
  3 of the 10 are in `index.js` itself (127 lines) = **23.6 per 1000 lines of the actual library**.
- **why it matters:** p-limit is the null hypothesis. It has ~1.5 billion downloads/wk of
  scrutiny on it. Zero-to-two would have been a strong result. Ten is not quiet.
- **severity:** real problem

### Default `crimes scan` (no `--all`) shows 9 of the 10 findings on p-limit
- **repo:** p-limit
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/p-limit --no-init`
- **what happened:** the default human view prints 9 findings across 3 files and then says
  "Also flagged elsewhere / scripts/ 1 finding / Run with --all to see them."
  So the "signal over exhaustiveness" default suppresses exactly **one** finding here.
- **why it matters:** the PRD constraint is "default `crimes scan` shows top findings only".
  On a small repo the default view is effectively `--all`. A first-time user running
  `npx crimes scan` on a tiny clean library sees a wall of nine charges.
- **severity:** real problem

### `--all` has NO effect on `--format json` — the JSON is byte-identical
- **repo:** both
- **command:** `crimes scan $R --format json --no-init` vs `... --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `diff` of the two JSON outputs is empty for p-limit and byte-identical
  for hono (529,405 bytes both ways).
- **why it matters:** `--all` is documented and rendered as a scope flag ("Run with --all to
  see them"), but it is purely a **human-renderer** flag. Any consumer of the JSON contract
  who assumes `--all` widens the result set is wrong, and anyone measuring "the --all vs
  default gap" from JSON will measure zero. Not necessarily a bug, but it is undocumented in
  the output and it makes the two surfaces disagree about what the word "all" means.
- **severity:** real problem

### `test.js` at repo root is NOT recognised as a test file
- **repo:** p-limit
- **finding:** `crime_00006` — God File, `test.js`, 378 lines, medium, `tier: "domain"`
- **what happened:** charged at `1.3× the configured 300-line threshold` — i.e. the **domain**
  budget, not the 1500-line `test_file` budget. `TEST_FILE_RE` in
  `packages/core/src/util/test-files.ts` requires `*.test.js` / `*_test.js` / `__tests__/` /
  `tests/`. A bare top-level `test.js` matches none of them.
  Same classifier fed `tier` — all 3 `test.js` findings are `tier: "domain"`.
- **why it matters:** `test.js` is the single most conventional test filename in the npm
  ecosystem (it is the ava/node:test default and what sindresorhus uses in ~every package).
  Missing it means every sindresorhus-shaped repo gets its test suite charged as production
  code, on both the `large_file` shape and the domain/nonDomain tier.
- **severity:** real problem

### `swallowed_error` fires on a `catch {}` whose reason is written on the line above it
- **repo:** p-limit
- **finding:** `crime_00003` — `index.js:44`, medium, confidence 0.86
- **what happened:** the evidence says "the error is never inspected", "no rethrow ... no
  logging". `index.js:39-41` is a three-line comment reading: *"Wait for the function to
  complete (success or failure) / We catch errors here to prevent unhandled rejections, /
  but the original promise rejection is preserved for the caller"*. The detector reads the
  handler body but not the comment immediately above the protected region.
- **why it matters:** not judging correctness here — but the *evidence text* asserts an
  absence ("there is no record the operation failed") that a reader can disprove by looking
  three lines up. Evidence that overstates is worse than a bare flag.
- **severity:** real problem

### `boolean_naming_drift` flags a public API option name
- **repo:** p-limit
- **finding:** `crime_00008` — `` `rejectOnClear` @L4``, low
- **what happened:** `rejectOnClear` is p-limit's documented public option
  (`pLimit({concurrency, rejectOnClear})`). The suggested action is "Rename to `is*`/`has*`".
- **why it matters:** the suggested fix is a breaking semver-major API change, offered at
  `effort: "quick"`. The detector has no notion of "this identifier crosses the package
  boundary".
- **severity:** annoyance

### `test_gap: 0.5` on every p-limit finding, including `index.js`
- **repo:** p-limit
- **what happened:** all 10 findings carry `test_gap: 0.5` and the human view renders
  "test gap ~median" for every file including `index.js`.
- **why it matters:** p-limit has a 377-line `test.js` covering `index.js`. 0.5 is the
  no-signal default. Because `test.js` is not matched as a test file (above), the test-gap
  scorer sees a repo with zero tests and falls back to the midpoint everywhere. So one
  naming-convention miss silently degrades a whole score dimension into a constant.
- **severity:** real problem

### 0.17.0 `docs` shape: WORKS — but it is not visible in the `tier` field
- **repo:** p-limit (synthetic probe, written into the disposable clone then reverted)
- **command:** wrote `DOCS_PROBE_1200.md` (1279 lines) / `DOCS_PROBE_2500.md` (2557 lines),
  rescanned, then `rm` + `git status --porcelain` clean at
  `df476048d023ff868cd45b35ee47f5fb0ca2b25a`.
- **what happened:** exactly as specified.
  - 1279 lines → `severity: low`, conf 0.74, "Document is 1279 lines (document threshold
    1000)", evidence `1.3× the configured 1000-line document threshold` +
    `shape: document (matches .md/.mdx/.markdown/.rst/.adoc/.txt)`, `agent_risk` 0.13.
  - 2557 lines → `severity: medium`, conf 0.93, `2.6×`, `agent_risk` 0.22.
  - `fix_shape` correctly switches to "split into per-topic pages; one subject per document".
  - **BUT `tier` is `"domain"` on both.** `tier` is the scope tier (`domain`/`nonDomain`) from
    `scoring/tier.ts`, a completely different axis. The `large_file` **shape**
    (`domain`/`test_file`/`docs`) is exposed **only as an English sentence inside the
    `evidence` array**. A JSON consumer cannot filter or group by shape without
    string-matching prose.
- **why it matters:** the JSON is the product contract. A behavioural tier that only exists
  in a human-readable evidence string is not in the contract.
- **severity:** real problem

### The `.json`/`.yaml` "deliberate exclusion" is unreachable at default config
- **repo:** p-limit (synthetic probe)
- **what happened:** `large-file.ts` comments that "`.json`, `.yaml`, and friends are data,
  and a 3000-line config is a finding worth keeping". A 1201-line `DOCS_PROBE.json` and a
  1201-line `DOCS_PROBE.yaml` dropped into the repo produced **zero findings** at default
  config. `coverage.universal_only_by_extension` listed only `.md`.
  With `include` overridden to add `{json,yaml,yml,txt}`, both immediately fire
  `large_file` **high**, `4.0× the configured 300-line threshold` — so the *shape logic* is
  correct. The files simply never reach the detector: `DEFAULT_SOURCE_INCLUDES` in
  `packages/core/src/config.ts` covers `{ts,tsx,js,jsx,mjs,cjs,cts,mts}`, `{py,pyi}`, a
  compiled-language list, and `{md,mdx}` — **no `json`, no `yaml`, no `yml`**.
  Real-world confirmation: hono's 735-line `benchmarks/query-param/package-lock.json` and
  698-line `package.json` produced no `large_file` finding.
- **also:** `.txt`, `.rst`, `.adoc`, `.markdown` are in `DOCS_EXT_RE` but **not** in
  `DEFAULT_SOURCE_INCLUDES` either — a 1279-line `.txt` produced nothing at default config,
  and fired `low` / docs-shape once included. So 5 of the 7 extensions the docs shape claims
  to handle are dead code at default config.
- **why it matters:** documented intent and shipped behaviour disagree. The docs shape as
  shipped applies to `.md`/`.mdx` only.
- **severity:** real problem

### Zero case: CONFIRMED clean
- **repo:** p-limit
- **what happened:** `cross_language_*` detectors: **0 findings**. Python-pack detectors:
  **0 findings** (`pack == "language-py"` count = 0; no `*.py` detector_id). Correct — the
  repo has no Python and one language.
- **BUT:** `coverage.packs_loaded` = `["universal", "language-js", "language-py"]`.
  `language-py` is reported as loaded on a repo with zero `.py` files. The protocol already
  warns that `packs_loaded` reports what was loaded, not what ran; this confirms it
  is uninformative as a health signal.
- **severity:** annoyance

---

## VERBATIM: default `crimes scan` on p-limit (no `--all`)

```
CRIME SCENE REPORT
repo: p-limit  ·  10 findings across 4 files  ·  0 high  6 medium  4 low

Top files by risk
index.js  3 findings · 2 medium, 1 low
   1. God Function · pLimit()   lines 3–115 (113 lines), 1.9× the domain function threshold (60 lines)
   2. Catch and Release · run → catch_clause   protected operation: await result;, handler kind: try/catch (line 44)
   3. Unprefixed Boolean   `rejectOnClear` @L4, lines: 4
     Risk: churn low · test gap ~median · blast bottom-quartile (3 importers)
     id=crime_00001 … crime_00008

benchmark.js  3 findings · 2 medium, 1 low
   1. Return Shape Roulette · <anonymous>   3 object-literal return shapes, lowest key overlap: 25%
   2. Temporal Recklessness   4× Date.now(), lines: 32, 59, 74, 140
   3. Concurrency Stampede · <module> → writeFile   fan-out: Promise.all at line 64, collection: fileData (← Array.from({length: 100}, (_, i) => ({ name: `data-${i}.jso…).map(…)
     Risk: churn low · test gap ~median · blast bottom-quartile (0 importers)
     id=crime_00002 … crime_00007

test.js  3 findings · 2 medium, 1 low
   1. Catch and Release · <module> → all   protected operation: await Promise.all(promises);, calls inside the protected region: Promise.all
   2. God File   378 non-empty lines, 1.3× the configured 300-line threshold
   3. Unprefixed Boolean   `ran` @L69, lines: 69
     Risk: churn low · test gap ~median · blast bottom-quartile (0 importers)
     id=crime_00004 … crime_00009

Also flagged elsewhere
  scripts/  1 finding
  Run with --all to see them.

→ Start with `crimes context index.js` — it concentrates the most risk in this scan.
```

### Honest first-time-user reaction — p-limit
- **repo:** p-limit
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/p-limit --no-init`
- **what happened / my reaction:** I would distrust the tool.

  I know this repo. It is 127 lines of library code that half the JavaScript ecosystem
  depends on, written and re-reviewed by one of the most careful maintainers in open
  source. The tool opens with `CRIME SCENE REPORT` and charges it with nine crimes, in a
  file-grouped layout that reads as a rap sheet.

  The specific things that cost trust, in order:
  1. **"God Function · pLimit() lines 3–115 (113 lines), 1.9× the domain function
     threshold"** — `pLimit` is a closure factory. Lines 3–115 are the *whole module*: the
     nested `resumeNext`/`next`/`run`/`enqueue`/`generator` arrows and one
     `Object.defineProperties` block. The detector is measuring the file and calling it a
     function. This is the first line of the report and it is the weakest one.
  2. **"God File · test.js · 378 non-empty lines, 1.3× the configured 300-line threshold"**
     — a test file charged on the production budget because the filename is `test.js` and
     not `something.test.js`. Both the shape and the tier got it wrong.
  3. **"Catch and Release · index.js:44"** with `the error is never inspected` — three lines
     above the flagged catch is a comment explaining exactly why the catch is empty. The
     evidence asserts an absence that the file itself refutes.
  4. **Three of the nine are in `benchmark.js`** — a benchmark harness — charged with
     `Temporal Recklessness` for calling `Date.now()` (that is what a benchmark does) and
     `Concurrency Stampede` for `Promise.all` over `Array.from({length: 100})`, where the
     evidence says *"N is a property of the data, not of this code"* about a literal `100`
     three tokens away, and simultaneously claims the collection is *"an awaited call
     (query, request, or listing)"* while also saying *"produced by: Array.from"*. Two
     clauses of the same evidence block contradict each other.
  5. **Every file shows `test gap ~median`** including `index.js`, which has a 377-line
     suite. And **"Likely tests: (no test file matched...)"** in `crimes context index.js`,
     for a package whose test file is sitting in the same directory importing it.
  6. The closing nudge — `→ Start with crimes context index.js — it concentrates the most
     risk in this scan` — points at the library's entire public implementation.

  What *would* keep me at the table: the evidence blocks are genuinely well built. Every
  finding shows its confidence arithmetic (`0.86 = 0.72 base + 0.14 (handler body is
  empty)`), line numbers, and a named fix shape. Nothing is a bare assertion. `verdict`
  correctly said UNCHANGED. The tool is honest about its reasoning — it is just reasoning
  from bad premises here.

  But the headline is: **on the cleanest small library I can think of, the default view
  fires nine charges and the very first one is wrong.** A first-time user runs
  `npx crimes scan` on a repo they know well precisely to calibrate the tool. p-limit is
  that repo for a lot of people, and it fails the calibration.
- **severity:** blocker (for adoption, not for correctness)

---

# thickeningtime

## Friction log — thickeningtime

**Repo:** `~/dev/thickeningtime` (Next.js app, TS/TSX, 324 commits)
**SHA:** baf0b22bbfc991b64d4838b556977ddd5c7a557b
**Binary:** `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes` 0.17.0
**Started:** 2026-08-02
**Focus:** command surface beyond `scan`

---
### `context` ids and `explain` ids are different namespaces — silent wrong answer
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes context lib/pdf-generator.ts --no-init` then `crimes explain crime_00001 --no-init`
- **what happened:** `context` prints `id=crime_00001` next to `generatePersonalizedPrintPdf` in `lib/pdf-generator.ts`. Feeding that exact id to `explain` returns `Sync I/O in Hot Path` in `lib/chapters.ts` — a different finding, different file, different detector. Exit 0, no warning. `context` renumbers findings 1..N *within the file it was given*; `scan` numbers them across the whole repo (the same finding is `crime_00004` in `scan`).
- **why it matters:** `context` → `explain` is the documented agent workflow and the tip printed by `crimes --help`. An agent that follows it gets a confidently-worded explanation of the wrong code, with no signal that anything went wrong. Worst failure class available: silent, plausible, wrong.
- **severity:** blocker

### No `fingerprint` field anywhere in `scan --format json`
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `grep -c fingerprint artifacts/thickeningtime.json` → 0
- **what happened:** `explain`, `ignore`, `unignore` and `feedback` all take a *stable fingerprint* (`<type>::<file>::<symbol>`), and `explain` even prints the `crimes ignore <fingerprint>` line for you. But the JSON contract — the thing the PRD calls the product — never emits a `fingerprint` field. The only machine-readable id is `crime_NNNNN`, which is scan-local and unstable.
- **why it matters:** the stable identifier is undiscoverable from the machine-readable output. Any programmatic consumer (CI, an agent, a dashboard) must either reconstruct the fingerprint by string-concatenating `type::file::symbol` — undocumented, and `symbol` is absent on ~25% of findings — or shell out to `explain` per finding and scrape the suppress line.
- **severity:** real problem

### `explain` has no `--root`; with no `--from` it silently full-scans cwd and can hang forever
- **repo:** thickeningtime (observed from a wrong cwd)
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_00004 --no-init` run with cwd=`~/crimes-dogfood`
- **what happened:** hung past a 600 s timeout with **zero output** — no "scanning…", no progress, no file count. It was recursively scanning cwd (which happened to hold 3.3 GB of unrelated clones). Every other repo-touching command (`scan`, `hotspots`, `context`, `diff`, `verdict`) takes a path argument or `--root`; `explain` takes neither.
- **why it matters:** `explain` is the one command you reach for while already deep in a session, and it is the one command that cannot be pointed at a repo. Wrong cwd = indefinite silent hang. There is also no progress output on *any* long scan, so "slow" and "wedged" are indistinguishable.
- **severity:** real problem

### `explain` fails outright on `oversized_raster`
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_00348 --from thickeningtime.json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `crimes: no detector registered for finding.type "oversized_raster".` exit 2. Tried one id of all 32 finding types present in this repo — 31 explained fine, `oversized_raster` is the only one that 500s on its own output.
- **why it matters:** a detector can emit a finding the tool then refuses to explain. It is a registry gap, not a content gap, so it will hit every user of that detector 100% of the time.
- **severity:** real problem

### `weak_test_signal` cannot see inside Playwright test callbacks — 8/8 wrong in one file
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes context e2e/social-reading.spec.ts --no-init`
- **what happened:** all 8 findings claim `0 expect/assert calls`. `e2e/social-reading.spec.ts:65-71` contains four `expect(...)` calls in the very first flagged test. The reported line range is `66-66` — the declaration line only — which suggests the callback body was never walked. The shape is `test("…", async ({ request }) => { … })`: a destructured-object arrow param.
- **why it matters:** this is a factual claim about the code that is checkable and false, stated at confidence 0.88. Playwright/`{ page }`/`{ request }` fixtures are the dominant e2e idiom in Next.js repos, so this is not an edge case.
- **severity:** real problem

### `context` on a test file does not know it is looking at a test file
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes context e2e/social-reading.spec.ts --no-init`
- **what happened:** "Likely tests: (no test file matched the target basename … and no test file imports it)" and "Clues · test gap: bottom-quartile" — reported *about a test file*. Agent guidance was "Nearby tests may not protect behaviour".
- **why it matters:** `context` is pitched as the pre-edit briefing for an agent. Given a spec file it should say "this is a test file, here is what it covers" — instead it emits two lines of noise that are literally inapplicable and mildly misleading.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `hotspots` puts 8 zero-churn files in the top 20 of a churn ranking
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes hotspots ~/dev/thickeningtime --no-init`
- **what happened:** top 10 tracks git well (positions 1–6 are exactly the git top-6 by 90-day commit count). But ranks 13–20 all read `0 changes`, at a flat risk of 0.40, and they outrank real churn: `app/page.tsx` (4 commits in 90 d) sits at rank 33 with risk 0.36, below `app/api/admin/chapters/route.ts` (0 commits, 1 finding) at rank 14. 18 of the top 30 have zero commits in the window.
- **why it matters:** the command's own one-line description is "Rank files by change risk using **Git churn** and current scan findings". Past the top ~10 it is a severity ranking wearing a churn ranking's name, and it demotes the files a maintainer would actually recognise as hot. Risk also saturates at 0.58 and is heavily quantised for zero-churn files (0.40 / 0.24 / 0.12), so ties are everywhere.
- **severity:** real problem

### `scores.recency` is 0.00 on all 503 findings
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes scan … --all --format json`
- **what happened:** every finding has `scores.recency = 0`. `scan` advertises `--no-recency` ("disable the recency multiplier on rank_score"), implying it is on by default and doing something. The repo's newest commit is 34 days old.
- **why it matters:** either the multiplier is dead code or its window is short enough that a repo idle for a month gets no signal from it at all. Either way `--no-recency` is currently a no-op flag on this repo, and rank ordering is not what the flag implies.
- **severity:** real problem

### `churn` is 0.00 on 316 of 503 findings in a 324-commit repo
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes scan … --format json`
- **what happened:** 63% of findings score `churn: 0`, and the human report prints "Risk: churn low" under every single one of the top 5 files — including `lib/pdf-generator.ts`, which is the 5th-most-changed file in the repo's whole history (19 commits). The window is hardcoded to 90 days in the scan path with no flag to widen it (`hotspots` has `--since`; `scan` does not).
- **why it matters:** "churn low" printed under the repo's genuinely hottest files is actively misleading, and there is no flag to fix it from the `scan` side.
- **severity:** real problem

### `scan` human output contradicts itself on the file count
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/thickeningtime --no-init`
- **what happened:** header says "503 findings across **187** files"; the footer says "Showing 5 of **163** files"; `hotspots` on the same repo says "**287** files"; `coverage.files_total` in the JSON says **298**.
- **why it matters:** four different denominators on one repo in two commands. None is labelled well enough to reconcile.

### 124 of 503 findings (25%) carry no `lines` field at all
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes scan … --format json`
- **what happened:** `lines` is absent (not null — absent) on 124 findings, across `orphaned_destination`, `design_token_escape`, `oversized_raster`, `route_metadata_drift`, `duplicate_component_shape`, `high_fan_in_fan_out`.
- **why it matters:** the schema is the product contract and a consumer cannot tell an optional field from a missing one. "Evidence before judgement… line ranges" is a stated PRD constraint.
- **severity:** real problem

### `explain` rejects a two-part fingerprint
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes explain "large_file::lib/pdf-generator.ts" --from … --no-init`
- **what happened:** exit 2, "no finding with id or fingerprint". The three-part form with an empty symbol (`large_file::lib/pdf-generator.ts::`) works.
- **why it matters:** for file-level findings there is no symbol, so the natural thing a human types is the two-part form. Requiring a trailing `::` is an unguessable ritual and the error message does not hint at it.
- **severity:** annoyance
### `crimes scan <path-that-does-not-exist>` reports a clean repo and exits 0
- **repo:** thickeningtime (control)
- **command:** `crimes scan /Users/andrew/dev/nope-not-here --no-init`
- **what happened:** `CRIME SCENE REPORT / repo: nope-not-here · 0 findings / No crimes detected. Suspiciously clean.` exit 0. `--format json` is the same: `files_total: 0`, `findings: []`, `packs_loaded` populated, exit 0. Nothing anywhere says the directory does not exist.
- **why it matters:** the single most likely CI misconfiguration — a typo'd or wrong-relative path — produces a permanently green gate. `coverage.files_total: 0` is the only tell and no consumer is told to check it. Contrast with `context`, which correctly errors with `file not found` and exit 2.
- **severity:** blocker

### `crimes scan <a-file>` leaks a raw Node ENOTDIR with an internal path
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes scan lib/pdf-generator.ts --no-init`
- **what happened:** `crimes: ENOTDIR: not a directory, open '/Users/andrew/dev/thickeningtime/lib/pdf-generator.ts/.crimes/triage.json'` exit 1.
- **why it matters:** unhandled errno surfaced to the user, from the triage-config loader rather than from argument validation. `scan <file>` is a completely reasonable thing to type (that is what `context` takes) and the message does not say "scan takes a directory, you probably want `crimes context`".
- **severity:** annoyance

### `crimes context <directory>` and `context <file outside the repo>` both answer "risk: NONE" with exit 0
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes context lib --no-init` / `crimes context /etc/hosts --no-init`
- **what happened:** both exit 0. The directory case prints `file: lib / risk: NONE (0 findings)`. The outside-the-repo case prints `file: ../../../../private/etc/hosts / risk: NONE (0 findings)` — it happily renders a path that escapes the root four levels up. The "agent guidance" in both cases is the boilerplate "no language pack claims (no extension) files".
- **why it matters:** `context` is the pre-edit safety check. "risk: NONE" for something it never analysed is the exact answer that makes an agent proceed. Nonexistent files error correctly (exit 2) — so the validation exists, it just does not cover directories or out-of-root paths.
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes diff` human output is three integers and nothing else
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~30...HEAD --no-init`
- **what happened:** 7 lines total: base, head, `New crimes: 33`, `Fixed crimes: 10`, `Unchanged crimes: 424`. No list of the new crimes, no files, no severities. `diff` has no `--all` or `--top`. The JSON form does contain all 33 with full detail — it is purely a renderer gap.
- **why it matters:** the stated use for `diff` is PR review. "33 new crimes" with no way to see them in human output means the reviewer must re-run with `--format json` and read raw JSON, or run a full `scan` and diff by eye. `--fail-on new-high` exists, so a CI job can *block* on findings it then cannot print.
- **severity:** real problem

### `diff`'s head-side scan disagrees with `scan --all` on the same working tree by 46 findings
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~30...HEAD --format json` vs `crimes scan --all --format json`
- **what happened:** `new_findings + unchanged_findings` = 457; `scan --all` on the same tree = 503. The gap is concentrated: `weak_test_signal` 6 vs 24, `parallel_destination` 4 vs 14, `swallowed_error` 63 vs 74, `oversized_raster` 4 vs 7, plus one each off `contract_drift`, `duplicated_policy`, `duplicated_role_status_plan_check`, `duplicated_navigation_source`. (Tree was dirty with 12 untracked paths, none of them `.ts`/`.tsx`, so that does not explain the test/error deltas.)
- **why it matters:** two commands give two different answers for the state of HEAD. Whichever is right, `diff`'s "unchanged" count cannot be reconciled against a scan, so a user cannot verify a gate.
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes verdict` takes 17 s to conclude base == head
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes verdict --no-init` (cwd=repo, HEAD identical to origin/main)
- **what happened:** 17.4 s wall, then `Verdict: UNCHANGED / New: 0 / Fixed: 0`. `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/main...HEAD` = `0 0` — the two refs are the same commit. It ran two full scans to discover that.
- **why it matters:** the answer is right, but 17 s for a same-SHA comparison is 17 s of a pre-commit or PR hook nobody will keep. A ref-equality short-circuit is one `git rev-parse` away.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `hotspots --since` default of 90d is wrong for the common case, and the header "N files" is a git artefact
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **command:** `crimes hotspots --since 1y --no-init` vs the 90d default
- **what happened:** at `--since 1y` the ranking is visibly better — `app/download/[token]/page.tsx` (24 commits) and `lib/personalization.ts` (20) at risk 1.00, `lib/pdf-generator.ts` (19) at 0.97 — and it matches the all-time git churn list. At the 90d default the top risk in the entire repo is 0.58 and 8 of the top 20 have zero commits. The header also flips from "287 files" to "**4634 files**" — larger than the repo has ever contained at one time, so that number counts historical/deleted paths from `git log`, not files that exist.
- **why it matters:** any repo whose last burst of work was >90 days ago (i.e. most side projects and most maintenance-mode services) gets a degenerate, near-flat ranking by default, and the header number is not a repo fact.
- **severity:** real problem

### `scan --changed` costs the same as a full scan and recommends running `context` on a PNG
- **repo:** thickeningtime (12 untracked paths, none of them source)
- **command:** `crimes scan --changed --no-init`
- **what happened:** 16.1 s wall (a full `scan --all` of this repo is 4.2 s) to return 7 findings, all `oversized_raster` on untracked PNGs and one `public/` bucket. Closing line: `→ Start with `crimes context generated_imgs/edited-2026-03-11T…png` — it concentrates the most risk in this scan.`
- **why it matters:** `--changed` is the pre-commit/CI path, so it is the one that must be fast, and it is 4× slower than scanning everything. And the next-action line is generated without checking that the target is a file `context` can say anything about.
- **severity:** real problem

### Flags that exist on one command and not its sibling
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **commands:** `crimes scan --since 1y` → `error: unknown option '--since'`; `crimes hotspots --top 5` → `unknown option '--top'`; `crimes hotspots --root .` → `unknown option '--root'`; `crimes explain --root …` → not offered.
- **what happened:** `--since` is on `hotspots` only, `--top` is on `scan` only, `--root` is on `context`/`diff`/`verdict` only, `--all` is on `scan`/`hotspots` but not `diff`. The churn window that `hotspots --since` controls is the same 90d window `scan` bakes into every `scores.churn`, but `scan` cannot change it.
- **why it matters:** four commands, four different option vocabularies for the same three concepts (where, how far back, how much). Every one of these was a first-guess.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `diff` range validation is genuinely good
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **commands:** `diff HEAD~5..HEAD`, `diff HEAD~5`, `diff nosuchref...HEAD`
- **what happened:** double-dot → "double-dot ranges are not supported — use triple-dot (...) form", with an example. Missing separator → "missing triple-dot separator", with an example. Unresolvable ref → `unknown git ref "nosuchref". Pass a ref Git can resolve, e.g. main, origin/main, or a commit SHA.` All exit 2. Same for `explain --from` on a missing file and on a non-JSON file.
- **why it matters:** noting the positive — this is the standard the rest of the surface should be held to, and it makes the `scan <nonexistent-dir>` exit-0 case look worse by contrast, not better.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)

### Confirmed: no writes into the repo
- **repo:** thickeningtime
- **what happened:** full recursive path listing before/after the whole session is byte-identical; no `.crimes/` was created; `git status --porcelain` unchanged at 12 untracked paths. Every command was run with `--no-init`.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)

# choreograph.cc

## Friction log — choreograph.cc

**Repo:** `~/dev/choreograph.cc` (Next.js app, 228 .ts / 52 .tsx, 380 commits)
**SHA:** 9e9720c9d47170f0fafc6dfd3fcc5b473c26dc6d (clean tree)
**Binary:** `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes` 0.17.0
**Started:** 2026-08-02
**Focus:** command surface beyond `scan`

---
### `scan` is not reproducible: `scores.recency` reads the wall clock, so ids and ordering change between runs
- **repo:** choreograph.cc (clean tree, same SHA, same command)
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/dev/choreograph.cc --all --format json --no-init` run twice, ~25 minutes apart
- **what happened:** identical finding *multiset* (490 both times), but 51 findings changed `scores.recency` (e.g. 0.13 → 0.12) and the output **order** diverged from index 14 onwards. Since `crime_NNNNN` ids are assigned by rank position, `crime_00015` is a different finding in run 2 than in run 1. `recency` is the only field that moved; evidence and all other scores were byte-identical.
- **why it matters:** three things break at once. (1) You cannot diff or cache two scan artifacts — every run is a spurious diff. (2) `crimes explain crime_00015` answers a different question depending on what time you run it, on the *same commit*. (3) `scan` (top-5 view) can show a different set of files morning vs afternoon. The tool ships a detector called Temporal Recklessness for exactly this pattern.
- **severity:** blocker

### `context` silently drops cross-file findings that `scan` reports for the same file
- **repo:** choreograph.cc
- **command:** `crimes context src/components/admin/JobDetail.tsx` vs `crimes scan --all`
- **what happened:** `scan` reports 12 findings for `JobDetail.tsx` (3 high, 7 medium, 2 low); `context` reports 11 (3 high, 6 medium, 2 low). The missing one is `duplicated_policy` at lines 10–19 — `Policy Doppelgänger · status "completed" "failed" rule … 2 occurrence(s) across 2 file(s)`. Verified by diffing the two JSON outputs on (type, lines, symbol): exactly one finding in `scan` and not in `context`, nothing the other way.
- **why it matters:** `context` is the pre-edit briefing, and the finding it drops is a **cross-file** one — precisely the class an agent cannot discover by reading the file it is about to edit. Dropping it inverts the command's whole reason to exist. `context` on a file is also what `crimes hook` runs on PreToolUse, so this is the default agent path.
- **severity:** blocker

### `test_gap: 0.00 — "a test file imports this module"` in a repo with zero test files
- **repo:** choreograph.cc (no `*.test.*`, no `*.spec.*`, no `__tests__/`, no `test` script in package.json)
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_00482 --from choreograph.cc.json` (`agent_permission_sprawl` on `.mcp.json`)
- **what happened:** Risk profile prints `test gap: 0.00 — a test file imports this module`. There is no test file in this repository. 12 findings carry `test_gap: 0`, all on non-source files: 4 `.png`, 6 `.md` plan documents, `CLAUDE.md`, `.mcp.json`. The remaining 478 findings all carry exactly `0.5`, rendered as "test gap ~median".
- **why it matters:** the score's *prose* is generated from the number without checking whether the number was ever measured. 0 means "not applicable / not computed" and gets narrated as an affirmative claim about test coverage that is provably false. And 0.5 on every source finding in a repo with no tests at all means the signal is a constant, not a measurement — while the renderer says "~median", implying a comparison happened.
- **severity:** real problem

### `name_behavior_mismatch` matches prefixes by substring, not word boundary — `cancel` reads as `can`
- **repo:** choreograph.cc (and thickeningtime)
- **command:** `crimes scan --all --format json`
- **what happened:** 4 findings whose evidence line is "name prefix suggests a **predicate**": `cancel` ×2 (`src/components/admin/JobDetail.tsx`), `cancelEdit` (`src/components/admin/QuotesAdmin.tsx`), `isoWeek` (`scripts/_backfill-song-type.ts`). `cancel`/`cancelEdit` are matching on `can`; `isoWeek` on `is`. None is a predicate. `cancel` setting React state is the single most ordinary thing in the file.
- **why it matters:** trivially checkable false positives at confidence 0.80, on a detector whose entire premise is that names should be trusted. Every one of the four is the same one-line fix (require a boundary: prefix followed by an uppercase letter or end-of-name).
- **severity:** real problem

### `crimes diff` misses findings that `scan` reports — including the repo's only `agent_permission_sprawl`
- **repo:** choreograph.cc (**clean** tree, so tree state is not the explanation)
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~30...HEAD --format json` vs `crimes scan --all --format json`
- **what happened:** `new_findings + unchanged_findings` = 479 vs 490 from `scan`. Missing: 4 `swallowed_error`, 3 `duplicated_role_status_plan_check`, 1 each of `contract_drift`, `logic_in_comments`, `name_behavior_mismatch`, and the repository's **only** `agent_permission_sprawl` finding (`.mcp.json`). The same effect appeared on thickeningtime (457 vs 503).
- **why it matters:** `diff --fail-on new-high` is the CI gate. A finding that `diff` cannot see can never be gated on, and the one it drops here is the agent-supply-chain finding — an unpinned `npx -y n8n-mcp@latest` MCP server. Repo-level findings (`.mcp.json`, `package.json`) appear to be outside `diff`'s scope entirely.
- **severity:** real problem

### `diff` is 5.5× slower than a full `--all` scan of the same repo
- **repo:** choreograph.cc
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~30...HEAD --no-init` = 26.4 s wall; `crimes scan --all --format json` = 4.8 s
- **what happened:** 26 s to produce three integers. (thickeningtime: 13.1 s diff vs 4.2 s scan.)
- **why it matters:** two scans should cost ~2×, not 5.5×. Whatever `diff` does to materialise the base ref is the dominant cost, and it is the command most likely to sit in a pre-push hook.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `hotspots` promotes five throwaway probe scripts over a genuinely-churning source file
- **repo:** choreograph.cc
- **command:** `crimes hotspots ~/dev/choreograph.cc --no-init`
- **what happened:** rank 1 is correct (`src/lib/daily-agents.ts`, 12 commits, risk 0.76). Ranks 7–11 are `scripts/_check-limitless-recent.ts`, `scripts/_melvin-fleetonomics-transcript.ts`, `scripts/_probe-github-events.ts`, `scripts/_probe-melvin-day.ts`, `scripts/_telos-andrew-conversations.ts` — five underscore-prefixed one-off probe scripts, 1 commit each, all tied at exactly 0.43. Meanwhile `src/lib/comedian/system-prompts.ts` (6 commits in the window, 3rd-hottest file in the repo) sits at **rank 138**, risk 0.18, because it has 0 findings. 5 of the top 20 have zero commits.
- **why it matters:** `core` ships `util/scope-class.ts` as "one answer to generated / vendored / migration / fixture / test / config / production", and `hotspots` does not consult it. The command also cannot see a hot file that happens to be clean — which is the one thing churn is supposed to add over severity. `scan` compounds it by binning 149 of the repo's 490 findings (30%) into a one-line "Also flagged elsewhere · scripts/" that you can only expand by re-running with `--all`.
- **severity:** real problem

### `verdict` output is identical whether the branch is genuinely unchanged or the comparison is degenerate
- **repo:** choreograph.cc (HEAD == origin/main)
- **command:** `crimes verdict --no-init`, 11.6 s
- **what happened:** `Verdict: UNCHANGED / New: 0 / Fixed: 0 / Reason: no new findings and no fixed findings`. Correct — but it does not say that base and head are the *same commit*, which is the actual reason and a completely different situation from "I made changes and they were neutral". Same output shape on thickeningtime.
- **why it matters:** on a CI branch that failed to fetch its base ref, `verdict` would print a confident UNCHANGED and exit 0. The one line that would disambiguate ("base and head resolve to the same commit") is missing.
- **severity:** real problem

### `explain`'s "Likely remedies" boilerplate is written for naming detectors and leaks into every other type
- **repo:** choreograph.cc
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_00482 --from …` (`agent_permission_sprawl` on `.mcp.json`)
- **what happened:** remedy 2 reads "If this reflects a real project convention, configure the detector or record feedback instead of **renaming code blindly**." The finding is about an unpinned MCP server in a JSON config file. There is no code and nothing to rename. The same sentence appears verbatim under all 25 explainable types in this repo.
- **why it matters:** the first-party long-form explanation is otherwise the best thing on the surface — genuinely specific, with a confidence ladder broken out term by term. One templated sentence written for `name_behavior_mismatch` undercuts it on every finding that is not about a name.
- **severity:** annoyance

### Positive: `explain` is excellent on the 0.16 detectors
- **repo:** choreograph.cc
- **command:** `crimes explain <id> --from …` on one instance of each of the 26 finding types present
- **what happened:** 25/26 succeeded. `agent_permission_sprawl` produced a genuinely useful writeup: identified `npx -y n8n-mcp@latest` as unpinned, named the environment variables passed through (`N8N_API_KEY`, `N8N_API_URL`, "names only; no values read"), explained that MCP servers inherit the agent's environment, and broke out `confidence 0.75 = 0.70 base + 0.05 (launch command read verbatim from committed configuration)`. `swallowed_error`, `unsafe_retry`, `config_drift`, `duplicated_policy`, `contract_drift` are similarly specific.
- **why it matters:** this is the part of the product that most justifies the positioning, and it works. Recording it so the failures above are read in proportion.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)

### Zero case: confirmed clean
- **repo:** choreograph.cc (single-language: 283 JS/TS files, 37 `.md`, no `.py`)
- **what happened:** `cross_language_contract_drift`, `cross_language_duplicate_logic`, `cross_language_orphan_endpoint` produced **zero** findings, as required. All Python detectors produced **zero** findings. No cross-language pack appears in `coverage.packs_loaded` at all — the list is `["universal", "language-js", "language-py"]`. `language-py` is *loaded* on a repo with no `.py` files and correctly produces nothing.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)

### `coverage.by_package` is absent from the 0.15 surface on both TS monorepo-ish repos
- **repo:** choreograph.cc (and thickeningtime)
- **command:** `crimes scan --all --format json`
- **what happened:** `coverage` contains only `files_total`, `files_by_language`, `files_universal_only`, `universal_only_by_extension`, `packs_loaded`. No `by_package` key.
- **why it matters:** the protocol lists `coverage.by_package` as a 0.15 robustness check "where present"; it is present on neither single-package repo, so there is nothing to check. Recording it as a negative result: the field appears to require a workspace/monorepo layout to materialise.
- **severity:** n/a (observation)

### Confirmed: no writes into the repo
- **repo:** choreograph.cc
- **what happened:** recursive path listing before/after identical; no `.crimes/` created; `git status --porcelain` empty before and after.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)

# improbable

## Friction log — improbable

**Repo:** `~/dev/improbable` (130 .ts / 71 .tsx, 408 commits, clean tree)
**SHA:** ec22855b2c8f820c914b91f32d98909c6819151a
**Binary:** `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes` 0.17.0
**Started:** 2026-08-02
**Note:** `artifacts/improbable.json` pre-existed; not re-run. Everything else fresh.
**Focus:** command surface beyond `scan`

---
### Root cause found: fingerprints are not unique, and `diff` silently dedupes on them
- **repo:** improbable (and reproduced on both other repos)
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~30...HEAD --format json` vs `crimes scan --all --format json`
- **what happened:** `diff` head-set = 424; `scan --all` = 488. **64 findings missing (13%)**, concentrated in `weak_test_signal` (−38), `large_function` (−16), `logic_in_comments` (−4), `swallowed_error` (−4); by directory, `test/` loses 42 of 90. Computing `type::file::symbol` over the scan output explains it almost exactly: **488 findings collapse to 420 distinct fingerprints — 68 collisions.** Worst offenders: `large_function::src/MetricTreeSurface.tsx::<anonymous>` ×12, `weak_test_signal::test/server.test.ts::` ×7, `weak_test_signal::test/connector.test.ts::` ×6, `logic_in_comments::src/MetricTreeSurface.tsx::` ×5. The same arithmetic holds on the other two repos: choreograph.cc 490 findings / 477 fingerprints / 11-finding diff gap; thickeningtime 503 / 455 / 46.
- **why it matters:** this is one root cause behind three separate symptoms. (1) `diff` and `verdict` undercount by 2–13%, so `--fail-on new-high` gates on an incomplete set. (2) `crimes ignore <fingerprint> --reason "…"` silences *every* colliding finding — one reason retires 12 distinct God Functions across 10 000 lines of `MetricTreeSurface.tsx`, or all 7 weak tests in `server.test.ts`. (3) `explain <fingerprint>` returns an arbitrary member of the set with no indication there are 11 others (verified: it returned lines 9864–10261 out of 12 candidates spanning lines 2948–13653, exit 0, silent). Anything without a symbol, or with `<anonymous>`, `<module>`, or a repeated `→ callee` suffix, collides by construction.
- **severity:** blocker

### 0.17's `discriminator` fingerprints reach only 3 detectors, none of the colliding ones
- **repo:** improbable / choreograph.cc / thickeningtime
- **command:** `grep -o '"discriminator"' *.json`
- **what happened:** `discriminator` is present on 20 / 18 / 17 findings respectively, and only ever on `magic_domain_literal_scatter`, `exact_duplicate_block`, `near_duplicate_block`. It never appears on `large_function`, `weak_test_signal`, `logic_in_comments`, `swallowed_error` — the four detectors that actually generate the collisions above.
- **why it matters:** 0.17 shipped the mechanism that would fix fingerprint collisions and wired it to the duplication family, which was already the least collision-prone (its findings carry a natural discriminator). The uniqueness problem is untouched.
- **severity:** real problem

### `context` rewrites paths inside a workspace package and prints a path that does not exist
- **repo:** improbable (pnpm workspace: root `@ortomate/improbable` + `app` package)
- **command:** `crimes context app/src/middleware.ts --no-init` (cwd = repo root)
- **what happened:** header reads `file: **src/middleware.ts**`. There is no `src/middleware.ts` in this repo — `ls src/middleware.ts` → No such file. It analysed the right file (the churn clue "1 commits over 90d · last touched 10 days ago" matches `app/src/middleware.ts` exactly), but re-rooted the display path into the `app/` package and dropped the prefix. "Related files · src/instrumentation.ts" is the same rewrite of `app/src/instrumentation.ts`. `crimes scan` on the same repo reports these files correctly as `app/…` (67 findings under `app/`).
- **why it matters:** `scan` and `context` disagree about what a file is called, and `context`'s version is unopenable. The repo also has a real top-level `src/`, so `src/middleware.ts` is not merely wrong, it is *plausible* — an agent will try to open it, or worse, create it.
- **severity:** real problem

### `weak_test_signal`: a second, distinct failure mode — assertions behind a helper
- **repo:** improbable
- **command:** `crimes context test/server.test.ts --no-init`
- **what happened:** `test/server.test.ts:50-52`, "rejects creation without edit rights, using the configured message" → "0 expect/assert calls", confidence 0.88. The test body is one line: `await expectStatus(server.createNode(...), 403, "editors only");`, and `expectStatus` is a local helper defined 5 lines above that calls `expect(...)`. This is different from the thickeningtime failure (where `expect(` was literally inside the callback and still missed) — here the detector's premise is simply that assertions must be lexically inline.
- **why it matters:** `weak_test_signal` is 59 of improbable's 488 findings (12%) and 24 of thickeningtime's. A shared `expectStatus`/`assertOk` helper is standard practice in any test suite past a few hundred lines, so the detector penalises exactly the suites that have been refactored. Two independent failure modes on one detector across two repos.
- **severity:** real problem

### `hotspots` is genuinely good on an actively-developed repo — the 90d default is the whole problem
- **repo:** improbable (408 commits in the last 90 days, last commit 2 days ago)
- **command:** `crimes hotspots ~/dev/improbable --no-init`
- **what happened:** top 10 is git's top 10 by 90-day commit count, near-perfectly ordered, and **zero** zero-churn files appear in the top 30. `src/MetricTreeSurface.tsx` (117 commits, 57 findings) at 1.00 is unarguably the right #1.
- **why it matters:** the ranking failures logged against the other two repos are not a ranking-model failure, they are a **window** failure — a repo idle for >90 days has no churn signal in the default window and the formula falls back to severity. Recording it here so the recommendation is "widen or auto-select `--since`", not "rewrite the ranker". One residual issue: 7 files tie at exactly 1.00, and `src/index.ts` (45 commits, 2nd-hottest in the repo) sits at rank 10 / 0.84 while `src/types.ts` (34 commits) is rank 3 / 1.00 — the clamp makes the top tier arbitrary.
- **severity:** real problem (window), annoyance (saturation)

### `context` on the hot file costs 13.5 s — and `crimes hook` runs it before every edit
- **repo:** improbable
- **command:** `crimes context src/MetricTreeSurface.tsx --no-init` → 13.5 s wall, **651 lines** of output for 57 findings
- **what happened:** the output is high quality — 8 likely tests correctly identified, `churn: 117 commits over 90d · last touched 4 days ago · 3 authors`, 7 lines of specific agent guidance. It is also 651 lines long and takes 13.5 s.
- **why it matters:** `crimes hook` registers `context` as a Claude Code / Codex PreToolUse hook. A 13.5 s stall plus 651 lines injected into the context window before every edit to the repo's most-edited file is not a thing anyone leaves switched on. `context` has no `--top`/`--max-findings` (`scan` has `--top`), so there is no way to ask for the short version.
- **severity:** real problem

### `diff` and `verdict` are the two slowest commands and the two most likely to be in a hook
- **repo:** improbable
- **command:** `diff HEAD~30...HEAD` = 27.8 s; `verdict` = 23.8 s; `scan --all --format json` = 11.7 s; `scan` human = 11.7 s
- **what happened:** `verdict` spent 23.8 s to report `UNCHANGED` on a HEAD identical to `origin/main`. Across the three repos: verdict 17.4 s / 11.6 s / 23.8 s, all on same-SHA comparisons.
- **why it matters:** consistent with the other repos — `diff` costs ~2.4× a full scan and `verdict` ~2× even when the answer is decidable from `git rev-parse` alone.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `explain` clears every finding type in this repo — 27/27
- **repo:** improbable
- **command:** `crimes explain <id> --from improbable.json` on one instance of each of the 27 types present
- **what happened:** all 27 succeeded with full "What this detector looks for" / "Why it matters" / evidence / confidence-ladder / suggested-actions sections, including the 0.16 detectors `agent_permission_sprawl` (×2, on `.claude/settings.json::hooks` and `.agents/skills/clerk-cli/SKILL.md::agent instructions`), `unsafe_retry` (`trigger.config.ts`), `return_shape_roulette`, `option_bag_junk_drawer`, `duplicated_policy`, `config_drift`. Fingerprint form works both with `--from` and against a fresh scan.
- **why it matters:** across all three repos, one instance of each of 32 / 26 / 27 types was explained and **only `oversized_raster` ever failed**. `explain` is the most solid command on the surface.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)

### Id stability: intermittent, which is worse than consistently broken
- **repo:** improbable
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_00100 --from <artifact captured 90 min earlier>` vs `crimes explain crime_00100` (fresh scan)
- **what happened:** both returned `large_function` / `buildKpiColumns` / `src/KpiTreeGrid.tsx:399–677`. Stable here — whereas on choreograph.cc two runs 25 minutes apart reordered from index 14 onwards because `scores.recency` crossed a rounding boundary (0.13 → 0.12).
- **why it matters:** ids are stable *most* of the time and silently shift when a recency value happens to round differently. Intermittent id drift is harder to notice than total instability, and it lands on `explain`, `ignore`, and `feedback` — all of which take an id.
- **severity:** real problem

### Zero case: confirmed clean
- **repo:** improbable (183 JS/TS files, 105 `.md`, no `.py`)
- **what happened:** `cross_language_contract_drift`, `cross_language_duplicate_logic`, `cross_language_orphan_endpoint` → **zero** findings. All Python detectors → **zero** findings. `coverage.packs_loaded` = `["universal","language-js","language-py"]`; `language-py` is loaded and correctly silent. No cross-language pack is listed in `packs_loaded` on any of the three repos.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)

### `coverage.by_package` does not appear even on a real pnpm workspace
- **repo:** improbable (`pnpm-workspace.yaml` declaring `packages: [app]`, `app/package.json` present, 67 findings under `app/`)
- **command:** `crimes scan --all --format json`
- **what happened:** `coverage` has exactly five keys — `files_total`, `files_by_language`, `files_universal_only`, `universal_only_by_extension`, `packs_loaded`. No `by_package`. `grep -c by_package` = 0 across all three repo artifacts.
- **why it matters:** the protocol names `coverage.by_package` as the 0.15 robustness check. This is the strongest workspace case in my three repos and the field is absent, so the 0.15 coverage surface is unverifiable in the wild — it may require an explicitly-configured layout.
- **severity:** real problem (negative result on the 0.15 surface)

### 0.17 `large_file` `docs` shape works
- **repo:** improbable / choreograph.cc / thickeningtime
- **what happened:** 1 / 6 / 8 `large_file` findings on `.md`, each with a correct shape line: `1.7× the configured 1000-line document threshold · shape: document (matches .md/.mdx/.markdown/.rst/.adoc/.txt)`. Documents get their own 1000-line budget rather than the 300-line code budget.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)

### Confirmed: no writes into the repo
- **repo:** improbable
- **what happened:** recursive path listing before/after identical; no `.crimes/`; `git status --porcelain` empty. Every command run with `--no-init`.

# posthog

## Friction log — posthog

**Repo:** `~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/posthog` @ `96e243726db1ca001c2aad520e281719f2e3cbd6`
**Binary:** `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes` → crimes 0.17.0
**Host:** macOS 26.5.1, 10 cores, 64 GB RAM, `ulimit -n` = 1048576
**Started:** 2026-08-02

## Size / language mix (git ls-files)

| ext | files |
| --- | --- |
| .py | 17690 |
| .ts | 8473 |
| .tsx | 6430 |
| .rs | 1342 |
| .json | 1493 |
| .md | 1025 |
| (total tracked) | 41747 |

## Notes on measurement conditions

Between 5 and 8 other `crimes` processes (other repos in the same
dogfooding round) were running concurrently on this 10-core box for the
whole duration of the PostHog scan. **All wall-clock timings below are
contaminated by CPU contention** and should be read as an upper bound,
not a clean measurement. Max RSS is less affected.

---

### `--no-init` is a global option but is not listed in `scan --help`
- **repo:** posthog
- **command:** `crimes scan --help`
- **what happened:** the protocol's canonical invocation is
  `crimes scan "$R" --all --format json --no-init`. `scan --help` lists 13
  options and `--no-init` is not among them. It only appears under
  `crimes --help` as a top-level option. First reaction was "this flag
  doesn't exist on scan" and I nearly dropped it.
- **why it matters:** the flag that every CI/agent invocation needs is
  invisible from the help of the command everyone actually runs.
- **severity:** annoyance

### `scan` on a 1.6M-LOC repo prints nothing for >10 minutes
- **repo:** posthog
- **command:** `crimes scan . --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** no progress output of any kind. No file count, no
  "parsing 32k files", no spinner, nothing on stderr. For the first eight
  minutes the only way to tell it was alive was `ps`. RSS oscillated
  between 50 MB and 340 MB, so it was clearly working, but from the
  outside a hung process and a working one are indistinguishable.
- **why it matters:** the first thing a new user does is point it at
  their monorepo. If it looks hung for ten minutes they Ctrl-C and never
  come back. There is no `--verbose`, no `--progress`, no ETA.
- **severity:** real problem

### The scan spends most of its wall clock inside one `git log` subprocess
- **repo:** posthog
- **command:** `crimes scan . --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** sampling the stuck-looking process showed the main
  thread blocked and one child:
  `git log --since=90 days ago --pretty=format:CRIMES_COMMIT %cI %ae --name-only --no-merges`
  That subprocess alone ran for >5 minutes. Run standalone the same
  command takes ~88 s and emits **138,253 lines** (PostHog's 90-day churn
  window). Git also warns
  `exhaustive rename detection was skipped due to too many files` — that
  warning goes to the subprocess's stderr and is swallowed; nothing
  appears in `crimes`' own stderr.
- **why it matters:** the churn window is a fixed 90 days with no config
  knob and no cap on output size. On any repo with PostHog's commit rate
  this dominates the scan. Node also has to buffer the whole 138k-line
  blob in memory before parsing it.
- **severity:** real problem

### Run 1 of the JSON scan: killed externally at 773 s, no partial output
- **repo:** posthog
- **command:** `/usr/bin/time -l crimes scan . --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** `EXIT=143` (SIGTERM) after **773.52 s real**,
  164.29 s user, 51.67 s sys, **max RSS 678 MB / peak footprint 1.09 GB**.
  The SIGTERM came from outside crimes (a sibling dogfooding session
  running `pkill`), so this is *not* a crimes crash — but the consequence
  is: `posthog.json` was **0 bytes**. Thirteen minutes of work produced
  nothing at all, because the JSON reporter buffers the entire document
  and writes it in one go at the very end.
- **why it matters:** on a repo this size any interruption — Ctrl-C, CI
  step timeout, OOM killer — costs the whole run with zero salvage. There
  is no incremental/streaming output and no cache, so a retry starts from
  zero.
- **severity:** real problem

### Run 2: >22 minutes and still running, RSS ~580 MB, still zero output
- **repo:** posthog (41,747 tracked files; 17,690 `.py`, 8,473 `.ts`,
  6,430 `.tsx`; ~1.6M LOC of py+ts; 1.0 GB working tree, 401 MB `.git`)
- **command:** `/usr/bin/time -l crimes scan . --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** second attempt, this time with no other crimes
  processes competing. Past the `git log` phase by ~6 min; then a long
  silent parse/detect phase with RSS climbing to 577 MB.
- **why it matters:** `scan-detect.ts` keeps a `PackedFile` — the full
  parsed AST summary — for **every** JS and Python file in the repo, for
  the whole scan, purely so the three cross-language detectors can run at
  the end. That is ~32,600 retained parses on PostHog. The cost is paid
  on every scan whether or not the cross-language detectors will produce
  anything, and it is the reason peak footprint hits ~1 GB.
- **severity:** real problem

### THE HEADLINE: `cross_language_route_drift` cannot see Django/DRF at all
- **repo:** posthog
- **command:** read-only probe running the *shipped* parsers
  (`packages/language-py/dist`, `packages/language-js/dist`) over all
  17,704 `.py` and 15,133 `.ts/.tsx` files — i.e. exactly what
  `runCrossLanguageDetectors` would be handed.
- **what happened:**
  - Backend routes extracted: **28**. Every single one comes from
    `services/llm-gateway/` (a FastAPI LLM proxy) and
    `services/stripe-mock/` (a **mock of Stripe's API**, for tests).
    **Zero** routes from PostHog's own API.
  - PostHog's real API surface is Django + DRF: 206 `router.register(...)`
    calls, 183 `path()`/`re_path()` entries in `urlpatterns`, and 205
    `@action(detail=…, methods=[…])` viewset actions. `routes.ts` only
    recognises a **decorator whose callee is `<app|router|api|bp|blueprint|mod|server>.<verb>`
    with a positional string literal starting with `/`**. Django declares
    none of that shape. The dominant Python web framework is invisible.
  - Frontend fetch sites extracted: **42**, of which **0 match any backend
    route** — a 100% orphan rate. Composition of the 42: 11 from
    `frontend/src/lib/api.test.ts` (unit-test assertions, several of them
    *deliberately invalid* — `/api/projects/null`, `/api/projects/089908?x`),
    4 from `playwright/e2e/*.spec.ts`, 1 from a **semgrep rule fixture**
    (`.semgrep/rules/devex/toolbar-no-raw-fetch.test.ts`), and 22 from
    `products/desktop/packages/api-client/src/posthog-client.ts`, a
    generated client whose paths carry `{uuid}` / `{project_id}`
    placeholders.
  - PostHog's ~2,000 *actual* frontend calls are invisible for two
    independent reasons: they are written `api.get('api/environments/@current')`
    with **no leading slash** (104 such literal call sites — rejected by
    `if (!url.startsWith("/")) return;`), and the rest are template
    literals with substitutions (rejected by `literalText`).
  - Net effect: the detector emits **exactly one** finding —
    "42 frontend calls reach a path no backend route declares" — produced
    by comparing PostHog's test suite and desktop client against a Stripe
    mock's route table. Every listed call is a false positive, and the
    evidence block leads with test files.
- **why it matters:** this is the flagship 0.15.0 detector meeting the
  exact repo shape it was designed for, and it is not merely silent — it
  is confidently wrong. The `backend.length === 0` guard that exists to
  prevent this is defeated by 28 routes from two unrelated sidecar
  services. Worse, the failure is invisible: nothing in the output says
  "I found 28 routes and they all came from `services/`".
- **severity:** blocker

### `cross_language_type_drift` is the one that works — 22 candidate findings
- **repo:** posthog
- **what happened:** same probe: 870 Python closed sets, 1,622 TS
  string-literal unions, 22 name-matched pairs that diverge. Several look
  substantive rather than cosmetic, e.g.
  `QuotaResource` — `ee/billing/quota_limiting.py:78` vs
  `nodejs/src/common/services/quota-limiting.service.ts:8`, drifting in
  **both** directions (12 Python-only members, 2 TS-only), which is a real
  Python↔Node billing contract. `MetricAggregation`, `RunStatus`,
  `LLMProvider` are the same shape across `products/*/backend` vs
  `products/*/frontend`.
  Counterweight: ~11 of the 22 pair `posthog/schema_enums.py` against
  `products/desktop/packages/api-client/src/generated.ts`, which is a
  **generated** client. Those are all one-directional (Python-only extras)
  and are really one finding — "the desktop client's codegen is stale" —
  reported eleven times. `generated.ts` does not match the default
  `**/*.generated.*` exclude (the dot is in the wrong place), so it is
  scanned as hand-written source.
- **severity:** annoyance (dedupe / generated-file handling), but the
  detector itself is the success story of the release

### Python pack parses PostHog cleanly, including modern syntax
- **repo:** posthog
- **what happened:** 17,704 `.py` files, **0 parse exceptions**, 151,558
  functions and 33,099 classes extracted. Positive syntax confirmation by
  sampling files known to contain each construct and checking that symbols
  came *out* (not merely that nothing threw):
  `match` statements 25/25 produced symbols; walrus 24/25; PEP 604 `X | None`
  25/25; PEP 695 3.12 generics (`def f[T]`, `class C[T]`, `type X = …`)
  23/25; `except*` 5/5.
- **severity:** n/a — positive result

### `crimes init` silently amputates Python — and `packs_loaded` still says it ran
- **repo:** posthog
- **command:** `crimes init` then `crimes scan`
- **what happened:** `crimes init` wrote
  `"include": ["**/*.{ts,tsx,js,jsx,mjs,cjs}"]`. That is hardcoded in
  `packages/cli/src/init-detect.ts:85`; `detectRepoShape()` looks only for
  `pnpm-workspace.yaml` / `turbo.json` / `next.config.*` / `vite.config.*`
  and a `.js` file. It has **no notion of Python, Rust, Go, or Markdown**.
  On PostHog — 17,690 `.py` files, 42% of the repo — the config it writes
  drops every one of them, plus `.md` (which `docs_code_drift` needs) and
  every universal-only language.

  Measured on `products/metrics` (a subtree with both halves):

  | | files_total | by_language | findings | by pack |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | default config | 100 | `{py: 48, js: 37}` | 75 | py 31 / js 29 / universal 14 / **cross-language 1** |
  | after `crimes init` | 37 | `{js: 37}` | 33 | js 27 / universal 6 / **cross-language 0** |

  The whole Python pack, both cross-language detectors, and 8 universal
  findings vanish. **And in both runs `coverage.packs_loaded` reports
  `["universal","language-js","language-py"]`.** The report asserts the
  Python pack is loaded while zero Python files were even discovered.
- **also:** the `init` command's own help text (`init.ts:45-49`) documents
  the eight `language-py` detectors *in the same breath* as writing a
  config that disables all of them. `scopeTiers.nonDomain` likewise only
  lists TS/JS test globs, so on a polyglot repo Python tests get treated
  as domain code. And `$schema` is pinned to `0.1.0` on a 0.17.0 tool
  emitting `schema_version` 0.4.0.
- **why it matters:** `crimes init` is the documented first command and
  `crimes.config.json` is meant to be committed. On any polyglot repo the
  onboarding step permanently disables the two headline capabilities of
  0.14 and 0.15 — and the coverage block is not just silent about it, it
  affirmatively misreports.
- **severity:** blocker

### Config is resolved from the scan root, not the cwd
- **repo:** posthog
- **command:** `crimes scan products/metrics` run from the repo root
- **what happened:** the repo-root `crimes.config.json` written by
  `crimes init` was **not** applied; the scan used built-in defaults. Only
  after copying the config into `products/metrics/` did it take effect.
- **why it matters:** the natural workflow — `crimes init` at the root,
  then `crimes scan some/subdir` — silently ignores your config, and
  nothing in the output names the config file that was (or wasn't) loaded.
  There is no `--config` flag and no "using config: …" line.
- **severity:** real problem

# drf

## Friction log — drf (Django REST Framework)

Repo: `~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/drf` @ `0e25e53582df802da4fcc329bd57e4ba5f61010b`
Binary: `~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes` (crimes 0.17.0)

Size at scan time: 154 `.py` (40,450 LOC), 127 `.png`, 77 `.md`, 60 `.po`/`.mo`,
53 `.html`, 8 `.js`, 8 `.css`.
81 of the 154 `.py` files are tests/conftest (25,065 LOC = 62% of Python LOC).

### Corpus clone was broken on arrival (environment, not crimes)
- **repo:** drf
- **command:** `git -C ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/drf status --short`
- **what happened:** The clone had an **empty git index** (0 files) and only 15
  files on disk — `rest_framework/` and `tests/` were entirely absent. Cause:
  `git-lfs` is not installed on this machine, and `git checkout` aborts the
  whole worktree write at `docs/theme/src/drf-logos.fig` (an LFS pointer):
  `git-lfs filter-process: git-lfs: command not found` … `fatal: smudge filter
  lfs failed`. The abort left the checkout half-done and the index blown away.
- **fix applied:** set `filter.lfs.{smudge,clean}=cat`, `filter.lfs.process=""`,
  `filter.lfs.required=false` in the clone's local config, then
  `git reset --hard <SHA>`. Restored 154 `.py` files.
- **why it matters:** Not a crimes bug, but a **corpus-integrity** one. Had I
  scanned as-received, crimes would have reported ~0 findings on a repo with
  0 source files and looked beautifully calibrated. Any dogfooding round needs
  a source-file count assertion before it trusts a low finding count.
- **severity:** blocker (for the round, until fixed)

### BLOCKER: `crimes scan` on a nonexistent path exits 0 and reports "Suspiciously clean"
- **repo:** drf (reproduces anywhere)
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/drff --no-init`  ← note the typo
- **what happened:**
  ```
  CRIME SCENE REPORT
  repo: drff  ·  0 findings

  No crimes detected. Suspiciously clean.
  ```
  **Exit code 0.** Nothing on stderr. `--format json` is equally confident:
  `summary.total = 0`, `coverage.files_total = 0`, and
  `packs_loaded: ["universal","language-js","language-py"]` — a full,
  well-formed, apparently-successful report for a directory that does not exist.
  `crimes scan /no/such/path` behaves identically.
- **why it matters:** this is the most dangerous output the tool can produce,
  because it is indistinguishable from a genuine pass and it *praises* you for
  it. It fails in the direction of false confidence.
  - It would have silently absorbed the broken clone logged above: had I not
    counted `.py` files by hand first, drf would have scored "0 findings" and
    been written up as crimes' best control result of the round.
  - `coverage.files_total: 0` is the tell, but it is buried in JSON and absent
    from the human view entirely — the human renderer never prints how many
    files it looked at.
  - CI wrapping `crimes scan` as a gate passes green forever on a path typo,
    a moved directory, or a bad `--root`.
  - Note `~/crimes-dogfood/artifacts/zulip.json` and `ortomate-studio.json` are
    **0 bytes** from earlier sessions in this same round. Worth checking whether
    they are the same class of silent no-op.
- **contrast:** `crimes verdict --base <bad-ref>` and `crimes explain <bad-id>`
  both get this right — clear stderr message, **exit 2**. `scan` is the outlier.
- **fix shape:** error + non-zero exit when the root does not exist or is not a
  directory; and print `files scanned: N` in the human header so `0` is visible.
  Both patch-level.
- **severity:** blocker

### `large_file` evidence says "N non-empty lines" but N is total lines + 1. 127/127 wrong.
- **repo:** drf (18/18) and pydantic (109/109) — **every single `large_file`
  finding in the round**
- **command:** `crimes scan <repo> --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** the finding for `rest_framework/decorators.py` reads:
  > File is 301 lines (threshold 300) … evidence: `"301 non-empty lines"`,
  > `"1.0× the configured 300-line threshold"`

  Ground truth: `wc -l` = **300**, and **non-empty lines = 223**.
  The claimed number is `source.split("\n").length`, i.e. total lines plus the
  phantom empty string after the trailing newline. Verified across all 127
  findings in both repos — claimed value is `totalLines + 1` every time, and
  the string "non-empty lines" is false every time.
- **it is not just cosmetic — it manufactures findings.** 13 of the 127
  `large_file` findings are on files that are **under** the 300 threshold on
  the metric the evidence claims to measure:

  | repo | file | claimed | real total | **real non-empty** |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | drf | `rest_framework/decorators.py` | 301 | 300 | **223** |
  | drf | `rest_framework/permissions.py` | 315 | 314 | **237** |
  | drf | `rest_framework/utils/field_mapping.py` | 329 | 328 | **268** |
  | drf | `rest_framework/validators.py` | 355 | 354 | **295** |
  | pydantic | `pydantic/_internal/_dataclasses.py` | 316 | 315 | **253** |
  | pydantic | `pydantic/v1/env_settings.py` | 351 | 350 | **290** |
  | pydantic | `pydantic/_migration.py` | 318 | 317 | **298** |
  | pydantic | `pydantic-core/src/tools.rs` | 321 | 320 | **279** |
  | pydantic | `.../validators/decimal.rs` | 306 | 305 | **277** |
  | pydantic | `.../validators/validation_state.rs` | 307 | 306 | **261** |
  | pydantic | `.../validators/shared/lookup_tree.rs` | 311 | 310 | **278** |
  | pydantic | `.../serializers/mod.rs` | 314 | 313 | **296** |
  | pydantic | `.../input/input_string.rs` | 349 | 348 | **299** |

  `decorators.py` is **26% under** the threshold. Docstring- and blank-line-rich
  Python is exactly the style this hits hardest — the gap between total and
  non-empty runs 20–35% in both repos.
- **the sharpest version of it:** `crimes diff HEAD~5...HEAD` on drf reports
  exactly **one** new crime in five upstream commits. That one crime is
  `large_file` on `decorators.py` — a file that is not large. The single
  highest-signal output the tool produced on this repo is an artefact of this
  bug.
- **`large_function` is unaffected** — spot-checks match the source.
- **fix shape:** either count non-empty lines as advertised, or change the
  string. Counting non-empty removes 13 findings and re-scores 127 —
  a patch bump + eval re-run.
- **severity:** real problem (highest-value fix in my two repos)

### `crimes diff` human output reports counts and nothing else
- **repo:** drf
- **command:** `crimes diff HEAD~5...HEAD --no-init`
- **what happened:** entire output was
  `New crimes: 1 / Fixed crimes: 0 / Unchanged crimes: 99`. **It does not say
  what the new crime is.** You have to re-run with `--format json` and read
  `new_findings[]` to learn it was `large_file` on `rest_framework/decorators.py`.
  With one new finding there is no length excuse for eliding it.
- **why it matters:** `diff` is the PR-gate command (`--fail-on new-high`).
  Failing a build with a count and no location is the least useful place to
  omit detail.
- **severity:** real problem

### `large_file` on Markdown (0.17.0 `docs` shape): flags an auto-generated changelog
- **repo:** drf / pydantic
- **what happened:** `docs/community/release-notes.md` (2708) and
  `docs/api-guide/serializers.md` (1232) in drf, plus `.md` files in pydantic,
  are charged **"God File"** with fix_shape "split by responsibility; one
  concern per module". `release-notes.md` is an append-only changelog; it grows
  by one section per release forever and splitting it is not a thing anyone
  would do.
- **severity:** annoyance (characterisation for the judge, not a verdict)

### Positive: `hotspots`, `explain`, `verdict` error paths, determinism
- `hotspots` was the most immediately readable output on both repos and needed
  no explanation. (Minor: drf's #6 hotspot is `docs/img/api-docs.gif` with
  "0 changes", and #5 is a test file.)
- `explain` output is genuinely good — detector rationale, evidence, the
  churn/test-gap/blast breakdown, and remedies including "configure the
  detector or record feedback instead of renaming code blindly".
- `crimes verdict --base origin/master` (a ref that does not exist in either
  repo) printed a clear message and exited **2**. `crimes explain crime_99999`
  likewise exited **2**. Both correct — which is what makes `scan`'s exit-0
  on a nonexistent path stand out.
- Three consecutive `scan --all --format json` runs on pydantic were
  byte-identical to each other and to the artifact captured earlier.
