# n8n

## Friction log — n8n

- **repo:** `~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/n8n` @ `fa2d8a131cdd34cf4ed31f46c079ff066042235d`
- **build:** crimes 0.17.0 (`~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes`)
- **size:** 375M on disk, 26,079 tracked files; 18,783 `.ts`, 1,226 `.vue`, 41 `.js`, 69 `.py`, 3,492 `.json`, 578 `.md`
- **workspace:** pnpm, `pnpm-workspace.yaml` globs `packages/*`, `packages/@n8n/*`, `packages/frontend/**`, `packages/extensions/**`, `packages/testing/**` (no `!` negations)
- **ambient `ulimit -n`:** 1048576 (soft). NOTE: this is NOT the macOS default (256) — the FD-exhaustion regression test is weakened at this limit, so a separate low-ulimit stress run is recorded below.

## Entries

### `scan --all --format json` exceeds 10 minutes wall clock; no progress output at all
- **repo:** n8n
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/n8n --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** First foreground attempt was killed at the 10-minute mark still running. Zero bytes written to stdout, zero bytes on stderr, no progress indicator, no spinner, no "scanning N files" line. From the outside the process is indistinguishable from a hang. Re-launched detached to get a real number.
- **why it matters:** 18.8k TypeScript files is a completely ordinary size for a monorepo of this class. A CLI that takes >10 min and prints nothing while doing it will be Ctrl-C'd by every user who tries it, and they will report it as "crimes hangs on my repo", not "crimes is slow". There is no `--timeout`, no `--progress`, and no way to tell a slow scan from a wedged one.
- **severity:** blocker
### No progress reporting and no way to bound a scan
- **repo:** n8n
- **command:** `crimes scan --help`
- **what happened:** `scan` has no `--progress`, no `--timeout`, no `--concurrency`, and no `--exclude`/`--ignore-path` escape hatch. On a repo where the scan runs for minutes with a silent stdout, the only user-visible signal is a hot CPU fan.
- **why it matters:** On big repos the first-run experience is "type command, nothing happens for several minutes". There is no way to say "just do `packages/cli`" other than passing a subdirectory path (which then silently loses the monorepo/workspace context that `dependency_provenance_gap` and `coverage.by_package` depend on).
- **severity:** real problem
### Scan cost is superlinear in file count — measured, not guessed
- **repo:** n8n
- **command:** `crimes scan <subtree> --all --format json --no-init` on three nested scopes
- **what happened:**

  | scope | ts/js/vue files | wall clock | max RSS |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `packages/core` | 255 | 6.7 s | 205 MB |
  | `packages/cli` | 2,977 | 234.0 s | 386 MB |
  | whole repo | 20,050 | >29 min and still running | ~575 MB peak footprint |

  11.7x the files costs 35x the time. That is roughly O(n^1.6), not O(n). Linear extrapolation from the `packages/cli` point puts a full n8n scan somewhere around 80-90 minutes.
- **why it matters:** This is the difference between "a tool you run in CI on every PR" and "a tool you run once and never again". The per-file cost is fine (26 ms/file at 255 files); something cross-file is quadratic-ish. Candidates given the 0.16 slate: the cross-file risk index (policy clones, object contracts, pass-through chains) and the duplicate-block/function-hash indexes, all of which do pair matching. `docs/` claims these are "bucketed matching, never global pairwise" — the measured curve says at least one of them is not bucketed tightly enough at this scale.
- **severity:** blocker
### `duplicated_policy` "variants disagree" groups distinct permission scopes as one rule
- **repo:** n8n (scoped run on `packages/cli`)
- **command:** `crimes scan .../n8n/packages/cli --all --format json`
- **what happened:** Highest-severity `duplicated_policy` finding says: *"5 variants of the same guard clause exist across 5 files and disagree on a value (one side tests `credential:manageInstance`, the other tests `project:create`). At most one of them can be current."* The shared shape is `!hasGlobalScope($0,?)` — i.e. five different call sites of the same permission helper with five different permission scope strings.
- **why it matters:** Purely factual note for the judge: the normalisation collapses the *argument* of a shared helper, so any codebase with a single `hasPermission(user, "<scope>")` helper produces a high-severity "at most one can be current" finding for the entire permission surface. Whether that reads as useful is not mine to say, but the sentence "at most one of them can be current" is asserted, not evidenced.
- **severity:** real problem
### `dependency_provenance_gap` treats the relative specifier `"."` as an external package
- **repo:** n8n (scoped run on `packages/cli`)
- **command:** `crimes scan .../n8n/packages/cli --all --format json`
- **what happened:** The single `dependency_provenance_gap` finding lists three "external packages imported with no declaring manifest". One of them is:
  `` `.` (type-only) — imported at src/eventbus/event-message-classes/abstract-event-message-options.ts as "." ``
  `"."` is a relative import of the sibling `index.ts`. The detector's own evidence line claims "relative paths ... are excluded", and this one was not.
- **why it matters:** One third of this finding's payload is wrong, and it is wrong in exactly the category the evidence text promises is handled. A reader who checks the first item and finds nonsense will not check the other two (`@n8n/eslint-config` and `eslint`, both of which look real).
- **severity:** real problem

### `agent_permission_sprawl` classifies a sentence *about* eslint-disable as an instruction to disable verification
- **repo:** n8n (scoped run on `packages/cli`)
- **what happened:** The one `agent_permission_sprawl` hit is `AGENTS.md:25`, classified `[disable_verification]`, quoting: *"import — or an inline `eslint-disable` of the rule — fails CI. The same rule also"*. The sentence is telling agents that eslint-disable **fails CI**, i.e. the opposite of an instruction to skip verification. The quote is also cut mid-sentence at both ends.
- **why it matters:** The detector is doing keyword matching over prose and the quoted span is not even a complete clause, so the reader cannot judge it without opening the file. Its own evidence block says it is advisory and confidence-capped at 0.35, which is honest — but a truncated mid-sentence quote is not usable evidence.
- **severity:** real problem
### Six finding types carry no `lines` at all — the judging card cannot be built for them
- **repo:** n8n (scoped run on `packages/cli`, 3,593 findings)
- **command:** `crimes scan .../n8n/packages/cli --all --format json`
- **what happened:** `lines` is absent (not `[1,1]` — absent) on every finding of these types:

  | type | findings | `lines` present |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `high_fan_in_fan_out` | 297 | 0 |
  | `exact_duplicate_block` | 60 | 0 |
  | `near_duplicate_block` | 33 | 0 |
  | `duplicated_role_status_plan_check` | 23 | 0 |
  | `deep_import` | 3 | 0 |
  | `circular_dependency` | 2 | 0 |

  418 of 3,593 findings (12%) have `file` but no line range. `exact_duplicate_block` does put the line ranges in prose inside `evidence` ("src/credentials-helper.ts:76-83"), so the information exists — it is just not in the schema field.
- **why it matters:** `PRD.md` §9 says the JSON is the product contract and the human renderer is a view over it. Any consumer that wants to jump to the code — an editor integration, a PR annotation, the blind judging card this very protocol specifies ("repo name, file path, line range") — has to regex prose out of `evidence` for one finding in eight. `symbol` is likewise undefined on 20 of 34 types.
- **severity:** real problem
### Determinism: finding SET is stable, ORDER and `recency` are not
- **repo:** n8n (`packages/cli` subtree, 2,977 files, 3,593 findings, three runs)
- **command:** `crimes scan .../n8n/packages/cli --all --format json --no-init`, run 3x (once at `ulimit -n 64`)
- **what happened:**
  - Finding **set** is identical across all three runs, comparing `{type, file, lines, symbol, summary, evidence, discriminator}` — 3,593 findings, byte-equal after canonical sort.
  - `exact_duplicate_block` evidence strings are **identical** across all three runs. The 0.17.0 reproducibility claim holds on this evidence.
  - Finding **order** differs: 202 of 3,593 array positions hold a different finding between run 1 and run 2. Raw files differ at byte 920,905.
  - `scores.recency` changed for 37 findings between runs minutes apart (e.g. `0.73` → `0.72`). Recency is wall-clock derived, so any two runs on different days produce different `rank_score` and therefore a different top-N.
- **why it matters:** `crimes diff` and `baseline` compare runs. A stable set is the important half and it holds. But a `--format json` consumer that diffs the array — the obvious thing to do — sees 202 spurious moves per run, and `--top N` / default human output can genuinely change between two runs of the same commit because recency drifts. Nothing in the output tells you that `recency` is time-dependent.
- **severity:** real problem

### Positive result: bounded FD pool holds at `ulimit -n 64`
- **repo:** n8n
- **command:** `( ulimit -n 64; crimes scan .../n8n/packages/cli --all --format json --no-init )`
- **what happened:** Exit 0. Identical finding set to the unrestricted run (3,593 findings, same `exact_duplicate_block` / `near_duplicate_block` counts: 60 / 33). No EMFILE, no stderr, no silent index degradation. Same at 255-file scope where output was **byte-identical** at ulimit 64, 256, and 1048576. Peak concurrent regular-file FDs observed on the full-repo scan was 27.
- **why it matters:** This was the specific thing 0.17.0 claimed to fix, and on a real repo at 1/16000th the ambient FD limit it holds. Recorded as a positive.
- **severity:** n/a (positive)
### The full-repo n8n scan did not finish in 60 minutes
- **repo:** n8n (whole repo, 20,050 ts/js/vue files)
- **command:** `/usr/bin/time -l crimes scan ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/n8n --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** Started 22:29:55, still running and CPU-hot (85-100% of one core, RSS oscillating 250-512 MB) when the 60-minute supervisor limit killed it at 23:29:5x. **Zero bytes of output** had been written in that hour — the JSON is emitted only at the end, so a killed scan leaves nothing at all. No partial results, no resume, no `--output` streaming. Relaunched fully detached to get a completion number.
- **why it matters:** This is the single most important result of the round. n8n is not an outlier repo — it is a well-known open-source TypeScript monorepo, and 20k files is normal for the segment `crimes` targets. In its current state the tool cannot report on it at all within an hour, and because output is buffered to the end, an interrupted run costs the user 100% of the work with 0% of the value. Any CI integration, any pre-commit hook, any agent pre-edit check is off the table at this size.
- **severity:** blocker
### `crimes verdict` fails on any repo whose default branch is `master`
- **repo:** n8n (default branch `master`)
- **command:** `crimes verdict --no-init --no-color` run with cwd inside the repo
- **what happened:** Exit with nothing on stdout and this on stderr:
  `crimes: could not pick a default base ref (tried origin/main, main). Pass --base <ref> explicitly, e.g. --base main.`
  The repo has `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD -> refs/remotes/origin/master`, which git already resolves for you — the tool never asks. With `--base master` it works fine (UNCHANGED, 11.2 s).
- **why it matters:** `verdict` is one of the eight required v0 commands. It hard-fails on n8n, on every pre-2020 repo, and on anything using `develop`/`trunk`. The fix is one line: read `origin/HEAD`, or fall back to `master`. The error message even suggests `--base main`, the thing that just failed.
- **severity:** real problem
### Positive: Python pack works on n8n's Python subtree; and a note on how to tell
- **repo:** n8n (`packages/@n8n/task-runner-python`, 58 `.py` files)
- **command:** `crimes scan .../packages/@n8n/task-runner-python --all --format json --no-init` — 3.9 s, exit 0
- **what happened:** `coverage.files_by_language: {"py": 58}`, 30 findings. 7 of the 8 Python detectors fired: `large_function.py` (14), `boolean_naming_drift.py` (4), `weak_test_signal.py` (3), `circular_dependency.py` (1), `direct_date.py` (1), `sync_io_in_hotpath.py` (1); `deep_import.py` and `mixed_utc_local_methods.py` did not. Plus 6 `universal` findings on the same files.
- **Methodological warning for whoever reads the JSON:** Python findings carry the **same `type` strings** as the JS ones (`large_function`, not `py_large_function`). The only discriminator is `detector_id`, which is suffixed `.py`. Anyone counting the Python pack by `type` prefix will report zero.
- **why it matters:** Positive result on the 0.14 surface, plus a trap in the schema that will produce a wrong "Python pack fired zero times" conclusion if the count is done the obvious way.
- **severity:** n/a (positive) / annoyance (schema)

### `cross_language_*` produced zero on a genuinely mixed TS+Python package — recorded as the zero case
- **repo:** n8n
- **what happened:** `packages/@n8n/task-runner-python` contains a Python task runner alongside its TypeScript siblings; scanning it produced **0** `cross_language_type_drift`, **0** `cross_language_route_drift`, **0** `cross_language_concept_alias_drift`. cal.com (no `.py` at all) also produced **0** of all three, which is the required correct answer there.
- **why it matters:** The cal.com zero is a positive — `packs_loaded` lists `language-py` on a repo with no Python and correctly emits nothing, so `packs_loaded` is not lying. The n8n zero is unresolved until the full-repo scan lands, since the TS and Python halves live in different subtrees and a subtree scan cannot see across them.
- **severity:** n/a (observation)

---

# cal.com

## Friction log — cal.com

- **repo:** `~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/cal.com` @ `038381aeca6261635357957d66b8ba85cdb29737`
- **build:** crimes 0.17.0 (`~/crimes-dogfood/bin/crimes`)
- **size:** 398M on disk, 7,693 tracked files; 3,878 `.ts`, 1,147 `.tsx`, 31 `.js`, 601 `.sql`, 355 `.md`
- **workspace:** yarn (`yarn.lock`), `workspaces` array in root `package.json` (10 globs, no `!` negations)
- **ambient `ulimit -n`:** 1048576 (soft)

## Entries

### Timing caveat — measured under contention
- **repo:** cal.com
- **what happened:** The cal.com full scan was run concurrently with the n8n full scan (which by then had been running >30 minutes without finishing) and with several other agents' scans on the same machine. Wall clock for cal.com is therefore an upper bound, not a clean measurement.
- **why it matters:** Recorded so nobody reads the cal.com number as a clean benchmark.
- **severity:** annoyance
### `dependency_provenance_gap`: 148 "undeclared external packages", of which the first 8 shown are all path aliases or relative imports
- **repo:** cal.com
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/cal.com --all --format json --no-init`
- **what happened:** One `severity: high`, `confidence: 0.74` finding: *"148 external package(s) are imported but declared in no applicable package.json ... a clean install can fail."* The 8 itemised examples (the only ones a reader sees; the rest is `+140 more`) are:
  `_utils`, `.`, `..`, `@components/AddToHomescreen`, `@components/apps`, `@components/auth`, `@components/booking`, `@components/dialog`.
  Two are relative specifiers (`"."`, `".."`). Five are `@components/*`, which is a TypeScript path alias declared in `apps/web/tsconfig.json` lines 5-15 (`"@components/*": ["components/*"]`). cal.com has **no root `tsconfig.json`** — every alias map lives in a per-app tsconfig.
  The finding's own evidence says: *"node built-ins, relative paths, path aliases, workspace packages, and subpaths of declared packages are excluded"*. None of those three exclusions worked here.
- **why it matters:** This is the flagship monorepo detector on the exact repo shape it exists for, and every visible example is wrong. It is `high` severity and it is the finding a reader most likely opens first, because "a clean install can fail" is scary. The root cause looks specific and fixable: alias resolution only reads a **root** tsconfig, and cal.com (like many Next.js monorepos) has none.
- **severity:** blocker

### Positive: workspace glob handling and `coverage.by_package` are correct on cal.com
- **repo:** cal.com
- **what happened:**
  - `coverage.by_package` has **118 entries**. Ground truth: 119 directories contain a tracked `package.json`; `by_package` lists all of them except the repo root. Nothing invented, nothing missed.
  - The lockfile arm of `dependency_provenance_gap` reports *"114 workspace manifest(s) compared; 5 manifest(s) outside the workspace were skipped (they are not installed by this lockfile)"*. Ground truth: the 10 `workspaces` globs in the root `package.json` match 113 of the 119 dirs; plus the root itself = 114. The 5 skipped are exactly `packages/app-store/templates/{basic,booking-pages-tag,event-type-app-card,event-type-location-video-static,general-app-settings}`, which are template scaffolds and genuinely not workspace members.
  - It correctly identified `yarn.lock` (yarn, 3,086 entries) and produced two other plausible sub-findings: 5 unpinned specifiers (`"latest"`, `"*"`) and 4 manifest/lock disagreements, all four of which are npm-alias specifiers (`"dub-package": "npm:dub@^0.61.12"`) — i.e. it handles yarn aliases in the manifest but the lockfile comparison does not resolve them.
- **why it matters:** The 0.17.0 workspace-glob work is doing its job. The failure above is in alias resolution, not in workspace parsing — worth separating so the right thing gets fixed.
- **severity:** n/a (positive, with one narrow caveat about `npm:` alias specifiers)
### `coverage` does not account for files it demonstrably scanned
- **repo:** cal.com
- **command:** `crimes scan ... --all --format json`
- **what happened:** `coverage.files_total` = 5,386 (`js` 5,051 + `files_universal_only` 335, itemised as `.md` 300, `.mdx` 25, `.sh` 9, `.php` 1). But the findings array contains **146 findings on image files** — 66 `.png`, 36 `.jpeg`, 30 `.jpg`, 12 `.svg`, 2 `.gif` — from `oversized_raster` (124), `svg_with_embedded_raster` (12), `raster_should_be_vector` (10). None of those files appear anywhere in `coverage`. The repo has 601 `.sql` files that appear in neither coverage nor findings, so there is no way to tell from the output whether SQL was skipped deliberately or silently failed.
- **why it matters:** The protocol's whole robustness premise is "a degraded scan and a clean scan look identical, so read `coverage`". If `coverage` omits a whole class of files that produced 3% of the findings, it cannot be used for that purpose. Also `files_by_language` labels a repo of 3,878 `.ts` + 1,147 `.tsx` as `{"js": 5051}` — correct internally, misleading to read.
- **severity:** real problem
### Default human `scan` on cal.com: 5.7 minutes to produce 107 lines, four of the five top files are test scaffolding
- **repo:** cal.com
- **command:** `crimes scan ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/cal.com --no-init --no-color` — `real 344.61` (5 min 45 s), 107 lines of output
- **what happened:** Read as a user would read it:
  1. Header is good and true: "4562 findings across 2030 files · 485 high 2190 medium 1887 low".
  2. Then "Top files by risk", 5 of 1,796. In order: `packages/testing/src/lib/bookingScenario/bookingScenario.ts` (test scenario builder), `apps/web/playwright/fixtures/users.ts` (Playwright fixture), `apps/web/playwright/lib/testUtils.ts` (Playwright util), `apps/api/v2/.../user-bookings.e2e-spec.ts` (e2e spec), `apps/web/modules/users/components/UserForm.tsx`. **Four of five are test scaffolding.**
  3. The closing call to action is `→ Start with crimes context packages/testing/src/lib/bookingScenario/bookingScenario.ts` — it points a first-time user at a test helper as "the most risk in this scan".
  4. The report *does* have a tests bucket — "Also flagged elsewhere: tests/ 666 findings" — so it knows to separate tests, but the bucket is matched on a literal `tests/` path prefix and therefore catches none of `playwright/`, `*.e2e-spec.ts`, or `packages/testing/`.
  5. File #5 spends 9 of its 13 lines on the same `Option` type declaration compared against 9 different files, one finding per pair, each printing the same left-hand side. It reads like a stuck record.
  6. Of the 25 lines given to file #1, 17 are `God Function` and 1 is `God File`. The top of the report is one detector saying the same thing 18 times.
  7. **The scariest finding in the scan does not appear at all.** `dependency_provenance_gap` — `high` severity, "148 external packages ... a clean install can fail" — is anchored on `package.json`, which is not in the top-5 files, so a default reader never sees it. Same for the `agent_permission_sprawl` hit and all 33 `mock_saturation` hits.
- **why it matters:** Honest reaction: on a repo this size the default view is *readable* (107 lines is fine) but it is not *usable*, because the ranking answers "which file has the most findings" and the user asked "what should I worry about". The lede is buried twice over — once by test scaffolding outranking production code, once by per-file grouping hiding every repo-level finding. And it costs 5.7 minutes to get there.
- **severity:** real problem
### `hotspots` works but every rank below #3 is a 14-way tie at 0.43
- **repo:** cal.com
- **command:** `crimes hotspots ~/crimes-dogfood/corpus/cal.com --no-init --no-color` — `real 407.73` (6 min 48 s)
- **what happened:** Ranks 4 through 20 all read `risk 0.43 (43%)` with `1 change`. Ranks 1-3 are 0.49/0.46/0.46 with 3/2/2 changes. The clone has 16,481 commits total but only **46 in the last 90 days**, which is the window `hotspots` uses ("since 90d"), so churn carries almost no information and the ranking collapses into "which file has any commit at all in the window", broken by finding count only incidentally — `packages/app-store/server.ts` with **1 finding** outranks `packages/features/bookings/repositories/BookingRepository.ts` with **14**.
- **why it matters:** The command's whole premise is churn x risk. When the churn window is nearly empty it degrades silently into a near-random list and still prints confident percentages. It should either widen the window automatically, say "churn signal is weak in this window", or fall back to finding-count ranking and say so. Costing 6.8 minutes to produce a 17-way tie is the worst combination.
- **severity:** real problem
### `explain` is the best thing in the tool — but it re-scans, and its ids are positionally assigned
- **repo:** cal.com (`packages/trpc` subtree, 428 files)
- **command:** `crimes explain <id> --no-init` x 12, one per finding type
- **what happened:** All 12 resolved and rendered: `duplicated_policy`, `high_fan_in_fan_out`, `large_function`, `deep_import`, `return_shape_roulette`, `contract_drift`, `exact_duplicate_block`, `unbounded_async_fanout`, `near_duplicate_block`, `dependency_provenance_gap`, `swallowed_error`, `mock_saturation`. The format (what the detector looks for / why it matters / evidence / risk profile / suggested actions / suppress command) is clear, specific, and the confidence arithmetic is shown. No errors, no missing types.
  Two problems:
  1. **`explain` re-runs the whole scan.** There is no cache — 15 s on a 255-file scope, and on cal.com proper it would be ~7.5 minutes *per `explain`*. `crimes scan` prints `id=crime_00076 … crime_01106` and invites you to explain them; doing so on a real repo is a 7-minute round trip each.
  2. **Ids are positional (`crime_00107`) and finding order is not stable between runs** (202 of 3,593 positions moved between two runs on n8n). So `crimes explain crime_00107` can resolve to a different finding than the one the earlier scan printed. The `ignore` command correctly uses a `type::file::symbol` fingerprint; `explain`'s advertised handle does not.
- **why it matters:** The workflow the tool advertises — scan, then explain the interesting ids — is quadratically expensive and not referentially stable.
- **severity:** real problem

### `unbounded_async_fanout` mis-describes a synchronous array method as an awaited call
- **repo:** cal.com
- **command:** `crimes explain crime_00107` (`server/routers/loggedInViewer/eventTypeOrder.handler.ts:55`)
- **what happened:** Evidence says *"collection source: an awaited call (query, request, or listing)"* and *"produced by: input.ids.reverse"*. The collection is `input.ids.reverse().map(…)` — `Array.prototype.reverse` on a tRPC input array, entirely synchronous. That mis-classification adds `+0.14` and `+0.08` to the confidence (0.60 → 0.88) and produces the suggested action *"Add a page size or `take` to `input.ids.reverse` so the collection cannot grow"*, which cannot be done to an array reverse.
- **why it matters:** The fan-out itself is real and the finding may still be worth acting on — but two of the three confidence boosts and one of the two suggested actions come from a wrong reading of the source expression. Recorded factually; the judge can decide the rest.
- **severity:** real problem
### `pass_through_abstraction` builds "call chains" by method name across unrelated classes — reproduced on both repos
- **repo:** cal.com (and n8n)
- **command:** `crimes scan ... --all --format json`
- **what happened:** The chain arm of the detector reports multi-file forwarding chains. On cal.com the highest-confidence example (0.92):
  ```
  WatchlistAuditService.ts:32  deleteAuditEntry(…)  → this.deps.auditRepository.delete(…)
    CachedTeamFeatureRepository.ts:52  delete(…)    → this.prismaTeamFeatureRepository.delete(…)
      CachedUserFeatureRepository.ts:45  delete(…)  → this.prismaUserFeatureRepository.delete(…)
  ```
  These are three unrelated classes. `auditRepository` is not a `CachedTeamFeatureRepository`, and `prismaTeamFeatureRepository` is not a `CachedUserFeatureRepository`. The hops are joined on the bare method name `delete`.
  Identical shape on n8n (`packages/cli`, confidence 0.98):
  ```
  ephemeral-node-executor.ts:115  isAgentProviderNode(…) → AGENT_PROVIDER_NODE_WHITELIST.has(…)
    auth-handler.registry.ts:112   has(…) → this.handlerMap.has(…)
      breaking-changes.migration-registry.service.ts:36  has(…) → this.migrations.has(…)
        external-secrets.ee/provider-registry.service.ts:25  has(…) → this.providers.has(…)
  ```
  Four different registries joined on `has`. The evidence then asserts "reading the chain end to end yields the same call the caller wrote", and "spans 4 files" / "4 layers deep" each add confidence — so the deeper the accidental name collision, the more confident the finding gets.
- **why it matters:** Both repos' single most confident `pass_through_abstraction` finding (0.92 and 0.98) is of this shape, and confidence *rises* with the number of unrelated files pulled in. The single-file arm ("5 functions in this file exist only to forward to `this.redis`") looks sound and is a different code path.
- **severity:** blocker (for the chain arm; single-file arm unaffected)
### `verdict` spends 13.8 minutes computing that nothing changed, when `git rev-parse` answers it instantly
- **repo:** cal.com
- **command:** `crimes verdict --no-init --no-color` (cwd = repo root) — `real 830.20` (13 min 50 s)
- **what happened:** Output is 9 lines: `base: origin/main`, `head: HEAD`, `Verdict: UNCHANGED`, `New: 0`, `Fixed: 0`. `HEAD` and `origin/main` are the **same commit** on this checkout with a clean worktree. The tool ran two complete scans of a 5,000-file repo to discover that a commit is identical to itself.
- **why it matters:** `verdict` is the CI-gate command. Fourteen minutes per invocation, on a question that a `rev-parse` comparison short-circuits, is the difference between a usable gate and an unusable one. A trivial guard (base SHA == head SHA and worktree clean → UNCHANGED immediately) removes the entire cost in the most common CI case where the branch has not diverged.
- **severity:** real problem
### Factual shape of the big 0.16 producers (characterisation only, no judgement)

**`swallowed_error`** — cal.com 114, n8n `packages/cli` 276.
- Handler kind: cal.com 103 `try/catch` / 9 `.catch()` / 2 discarded rejection; n8n 229 / 33 / 14.
- **Not a test artefact.** Test-shaped paths (`__tests__`, `*.test.ts`, `playwright/`, `packages/testing/`, `*.e2e-spec.ts`) account for **10 of 114** on cal.com and **1 of 276** on n8n. The overwhelming majority is production code.
- What the handler does: "logs but never passes the error" 25/67, "returns null" 19/30, "returns undefined" 18/25, "contains only a comment" 15/53, "empty body" 15/36, "returns false" 10/22.
- 62/114 and 151/276 are `catch { }` with **no error binding** at all.
- Boundary classification fires on a minority: 77/114 and 183/276 have no boundary label; the rest match on substrings like `repository`, `delete`, `auth`, `fetch`, `webhook`, `session`, `cancel`.
- Severity: cal.com 70 low / 43 medium / 1 high; n8n 174 low / 102 medium / 0 high.

**`contract_drift`** — cal.com 307 (the single largest 0.16 producer anywhere in this pair).
- 277 of 307 compare two declarations with the **same name**.
- Grouped by the "concept" the matcher reduced the names to: `get` **113**, `option` **39**, `user` 14, `host` 9, `appkey` 6. 131 of 307 are under `packages/trpc`.
- The `get` cluster is the tRPC handler idiom: every handler file declares a local `type GetOptions = { ctx: …; input: TSomeSchema }`, and the detector pairs them off, reporting "disagree on 1 field: `input` is `TListMembersSchema` on one side, `TGetAllByUserIdInputSchema` on the other".
- The `option` cluster is one `type Option = { label; value }` in `UserForm.tsx` compared pairwise against nine other `Option` declarations — nine separate findings, all printed in the default human report.

**`duplicated_policy`** — cal.com 104, n8n 77. 100% production paths in both.
- Summary shapes: "same conditional rule" 40/34, "same boolean rule" 31/5, "same guard clause" 19/23, "N variants ... disagree on a value" 11/15, "same state-transition table" 3/0.
- Domain vocabulary that drove the confidence bumps: `credential`/`credentials` (13 + 3 on cal.com, 7 + 2 on n8n), `status`, `role`, `seats`, `organization`, `owner`.
- Clean examples exist (`albyPaymentAppCredentials?.userCredentialIds || [-1]` repeated verbatim across four payment-app Setup pages). The "variants disagree" arm is the one that groups distinct helper arguments — see the n8n log.

**`config_drift`** — cal.com 55, n8n 3.
- The 55-vs-3 gap is a *repo shape* difference, not a detector inconsistency: n8n funnels env through `src/config/index.ts`, so the detector reports the 3 leaks; cal.com reads `process.env` directly all over, so the "used but not documented" (20), "server-shaped value read from client-reachable code" (8+4+3) and "different defaults" (7+5) arms all fire.
- Scope: 47 production / 7 test / 1 fixture. It correctly declines to print values: "no configuration values are reported by this detector".
- The client-exposure arm is honest about its own limits: "whether a bundler actually ships this depends on the build; this is a path heuristic, not a bundling proof".

**`mock_saturation`** — cal.com 33, n8n `packages/cli` 5. 100% in test files on both.
- Every one is one *test case*, not one file. cal.com's biggest reports 35 mocked collaborators, 95% of collaborators replaced, "2 mock-interaction, 0 behavioural" assertions.
- n8n's 5 all come from a **single file** (`community-node-types.service.test.ts`) in a repo with thousands of Jest/Vitest test files that use `jest-mock-extended` — so the trigger is narrow.
### `context` is the one command that stays fast at this scale — but it silently re-roots and disagrees with `scan`
- **repo:** cal.com
- **command:** `crimes context <file> --no-init --no-color` (cwd = repo root)
- **what happened:** Timings: 3.7 s, 11.2 s, 13.2 s — two to three orders of magnitude cheaper than `scan` (458 s) on the same repo, and the output is the most useful thing the tool produces (risk banner, agent guidance, related files, likely tests, churn/authorship clues, then the findings with evidence).
  Two wrinkles:
  1. It **re-roots to the nearest package** and prints truncated paths. `crimes context packages/features/bookings/lib/EventManager.ts` prints `file: bookings/lib/EventManager.ts` and lists related files as `bookings/lib/...`. Copy-pasting those paths from the output does not work from the repo root.
  2. Because the scope is reduced, it does not agree with `scan`: the full scan reports **14** findings on `EventManager.ts`, `context` reports **12**. Nothing says the view is partial.
  3. A path typo produces a clean `crimes: file not found: <path>` on stderr with an empty stdout and, notably, still writes a zero-byte file if you redirect. Fine, but exit code should be checked by any wrapper.
- **why it matters:** This is the command that actually works on large repos and it is the one the tool recommends at the end of every scan. It deserves to agree with `scan`, or to say that it doesn't.
- **severity:** annoyance (paths) / real problem (silent disagreement with `scan`)
